# APG and axe-core conformance baseline

Normative baseline extracted for a headless UI component library's conformance test suite. Each bullet is written as a testable behavior. Source tags in parentheses point at the exact file and table the behavior comes from.

Where the source marks something optional or recommended rather than required, the bullet says so: MUST for behavior the source states flatly, SHOULD for "recommended"/"strongly recommended"/"advised", MAY for items the APG labels "(Optional)" or "may".

## Sources

- `github.com/w3c/aria-practices` (WAI-ARIA Authoring Practices Guide). Commit `7e4034b262bc0d25332e330d8a582aaf34113829`, committed 2026-07-22. Shallow-cloned 2026-09-13.
- `github.com/dequelabs/axe-core`. Commit `4d306cbb7c456849c6f964444a6a7174d2be502a`, committed 2026-09-10. Shallow-cloned 2026-09-13.
- WHATWG HTML Living Standard, `html.spec.whatwg.org`, fetched 2026-09-13. Section numbers are those of the multipage edition as of that date.
- Supporting APG cross-cutting prose used throughout: `content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html`, `content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html`, `content/practices/hiding-semantics/hiding-semantics-practice.html`.

## button

### Roles and attributes

- Given a non-native element used as a command button, then it MUST expose `role="button"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a button, then it MUST have an accessible label, computed by default from its text content, or supplied by `aria-labelledby` or `aria-label` (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a button with a separate description element, then the button MUST carry `aria-describedby` pointing at the id of that description element (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a button whose action is unavailable, then the button MUST have `aria-disabled="true"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a button that opens a menu, then it MUST have `aria-haspopup` set to `menu` or `true` so assistive technology reports it as a menu button (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a `role="button"` element, then all of its descendant elements are made presentational by the user agent, so semantics of nested headings, lists or images inside a button MUST NOT be relied on (aria-practices: content/practices/hiding-semantics/hiding-semantics-practice.html, roles that require presentational children).
- Given an element styled as a link that performs a button action, then giving it `role="button"` helps users, but the preferred fix is to change the visual design so appearance and role match (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, note) SHOULD.
- Given the APG button example, then `role="button"` is placed on a `div` for the command button and on an `a` for the toggle button, proving the pattern is tag-agnostic (aria-practices: test/tests/button_button.js, button-role assertion).
- Given a button built from a non-native element, then it MUST carry `tabindex="0"` to enter the page tab sequence (aria-practices: test/tests/button_button.js, button-tabindex assertion).
- Given an icon `svg` inside a button, then the `svg` MUST have `aria-hidden="true"` so it does not contribute to the accessible name (aria-practices: test/tests/button_button-idl.js, svg-aria-hidden assertion).
- Given a library that supports the ARIA 1.2 IDL reflection API, then `element.role` MUST read `"button"` and `element.ariaPressed` MUST read the string `"false"` or `"true"`, matching the attribute form (aria-practices: test/tests/button_button-idl.js, button-role and button-aria-pressed assertions via assertDotValue).

### Keyboard

- Given focus is on a button, when Space is pressed, then the button MUST activate (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus is on a button, when Enter is pressed, then the button MUST activate (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a button built on a non-native element, then Space and Enter MUST be handled in script because browsers supply key activation only for native controls (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, introduction).
- Given a button activated by an assigned shortcut key rather than by focus, when the shortcut fires, then focus MUST stay in the context the shortcut was pressed from, not move to the button (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, keyboard table, focus-placement list).

### Pointer

- Given a button, when it is clicked with a pointer, then it MUST perform the same activation as Enter and Space (aria-practices: test/tests/button_button-idl.js, button-aria-pressed assertion, which drives state changes by click).
- Given a composite that manages focus with roving `tabindex` or `aria-activedescendant`, when one of its items is clicked or tapped, then the same `tabindex` or `aria-activedescendant` update MUST be applied as for keyboard navigation, otherwise pointer and keyboard state diverge (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, note after aria-activedescendant section) MUST.
- Given an element with an activation behavior, then activation is always caused by a `click` event, and when the user triggers it by any other means such as keyboard or voice, the default action of that interaction event MUST be to fire a `click` event at the element; the user agent SHOULD allow such non-pointer triggering (WHATWG HTML: §6.5 Activation behavior of elements) MUST for the click-firing, SHOULD for the allowance.
- Given a headless control that synthesizes activation, then the HTML spec defines no Enter or Space key binding for buttons at all: key activation is a platform convention the user agent supplies for native elements and which the library MUST therefore implement itself (WHATWG HTML: §6.5, which defers key triggering to the UA; §4.16.3 gives the Space-on-button `:active` example as non-normative prose).
- Given `element.click()`, then it MUST return early if the element is a disabled form control, MUST return early if a click is already in progress on that element, and MUST otherwise fire a synthetic pointer event named `click` with the not-trusted flag set, so `isTrusted` is false and the method is reentrancy-guarded per element (WHATWG HTML: §6.5, the click() steps) MUST.

### Focus management

- Given a button that opens a dialog, when it is activated, then focus MUST move inside the dialog (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, keyboard table, focus-placement list).
- Given a button inside a dialog that closes the dialog, when it is activated, then focus typically returns to the button that opened the dialog, unless the completed task logically leads elsewhere (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, keyboard table) SHOULD.
- Given a button whose action does not dismiss the current context, such as Apply or Recalculate, when it is activated, then focus MUST remain on the button (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a button whose action is a context change, such as "next step" in a wizard or "add search criteria", when it is activated, then focus SHOULD move to the starting point of that new context (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a button that is removed from the DOM or hidden as a result of its own activation, then focus MUST be explicitly moved to a logical successor element, because otherwise browsers move focus to `body` and the focus indicator is lost (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, persistence of focus).
- Given an element with a negative `tabindex`, then the user agent MUST consider it a focusable area but SHOULD omit it from tabindex-ordered focus navigation, so `tabindex="-1"` is programmatically and click focusable but not Tab-reachable; this is the spec basis for roving tabindex (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3 The tabindex attribute) MUST for focusability, SHOULD for the omission.
- Given `tabindex="0"`, then the element MUST be included in the sequential focus navigation order at its DOM position (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3) MUST.
- Given positive `tabindex` values, then all of them come before every element with an omitted, invalid, zero or negative value, ordered ascending by value with ties broken by tree order; a headless library MUST NOT emit positive values (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3, processing model) MUST NOT.
- Given the `tabIndex` IDL attribute with no content attribute present, then it MUST default to 0 for `a`, `area`, `button`, `frame`, `iframe`, `input`, `object`, `select`, `textarea`, SVG and MathML `a`, and a `summary` that is its parent `details`'s summary, and to -1 otherwise (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3) MUST.
- Given an element to be a focusable area, then ALL of these MUST hold: its tabindex value is non-null or the user agent determines it focusable; it is either not a shadow host or has a shadow root whose `delegatesFocus` is false; it is not actually disabled; it is not inert; and it is being rendered, delegating rendering to its children, or used as canvas fallback content (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
- Given an element can be focusable without being either sequentially focusable or click focusable, then a headless library MUST treat "focusable", "Tab-reachable" and "click-focusable" as three separate properties rather than one (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2, explicit clarification).
- Given a shadow host whose shadow root has `delegatesFocus: true`, then getting the focusable area MUST return the host's focus delegate: the first descendant with `autofocus`, otherwise the first focusable area in tree order, recursing into further delegating hosts, otherwise null (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.4 Focus, get the focusable area and focus delegate) MUST.
- Given the focusing steps, then they MUST return early when the resolved target's DOM anchor is inert, MUST no-op when the target is already the currently focused area, and MUST otherwise run the focus update steps, firing `blur` on the old chain and `focus` on the new chain with `relatedTarget` set (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.4, focusing steps and focus update steps) MUST.
- Given `element.focus(options)`, then `preventScroll` defaults to false, so scrolling into view is the DEFAULT and MUST be suppressed explicitly with `{ preventScroll: true }` (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.6 The focus() method) MUST.
- Given `element.blur()`, then the user agent SHOULD run the unfocusing steps but MAY selectively or uniformly do nothing, so a headless library MUST NOT rely on `blur()` to move focus anywhere in particular (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.6) SHOULD/MAY.
- Given a focus navigation scope, then each Document, shadow host, slot and popover trigger element owns its own tabindex-ordered focus navigation scope, and Tab traversal walks the flattened recursive composition of those scopes, skipping negative-tabindex elements (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.5 Sequential focus navigation; §6.6.2).

### State

- Given a command button, then `aria-pressed` MUST NOT be present, because only the toggle variant carries that state (aria-practices: test/tests/button_button.js, button-aria-pressed assertion, which checks `#toggle` only).

### Forms

- Given a control that mimics a native form control without using one, then it MUST be made form-associated deliberately: only `button`, `fieldset`, `input`, `object`, `output`, `select`, `textarea`, `img` and form-associated custom elements are form-associated elements, and only `button`, `input`, `select`, `textarea` and form-associated custom elements are submittable (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.2 Categories, form-associated elements, listed elements and submittable elements lists) MUST.
- Given a control with a `form` attribute, then that attribute's value MUST be the id of a `form` element in the control's tree, and only listed elements may carry it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3 Association of controls and forms) MUST.
- Given a form-associated element with no `form` attribute, then its form owner MUST be its nearest ancestor `form` element, and the owner MUST be recomputed whenever the element is inserted or removed, its `form` attribute changes, or a matching id appears or disappears (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3, reset the form owner algorithm) MUST.
- Given a headless control that needs form participation, then the supported mechanism is a form-associated custom element: set `static formAssociated = true`, call `attachInternals()` once, report the value with `setFormValue(value, state)`, and report validity with `setValidity(flags, message, anchor)`; a second `attachInternals()` call MUST throw `NotSupportedError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13 Custom elements, ElementInternals) MUST.
- Given `setFormValue(null)`, then the element MUST be excluded from the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setFormValue) MUST.
- Given `setValidity` called with any flag true, then the message argument MUST be non-empty or the call MUST throw `TypeError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setValidity) MUST.
- Given a form-associated custom element, then it MUST handle `formAssociatedCallback` when its form owner changes, `formResetCallback` when the owner is reset, `formDisabledCallback` with the new boolean when its disabled state changes, and `formStateRestoreCallback` with a reason of `"autocomplete"` or `"restore"` when the user agent restores its value (WHATWG HTML: §4.13.6, form lifecycle callbacks).
- Given a control that exposes constraint validation, then `willValidate` MUST return whether it is a candidate for constraint validation, `checkValidity()` MUST fire a cancelable `invalid` event and return false when constraints are unmet, `reportValidity()` MUST additionally report the problem to the user and MAY focus the element, and `setCustomValidity(error)` MUST normalize newlines in the message before storing it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3 The constraint validation API) MUST.
- Given a validity state, then the full set of flags is `valueMissing`, `typeMismatch`, `patternMismatch`, `tooLong`, `tooShort`, `rangeUnderflow`, `rangeOverflow`, `stepMismatch`, `badInput`, `customError` and `valid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3, ValidityState).
- Given a submitter with `formnovalidate`, or a form with `novalidate`, then its no-validate state is true and interactive constraint validation MUST be skipped on submission (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.6) MUST.
- Given a form that is reset, then a `reset` event MUST be fired that bubbles and is cancelable, and only if it is not cancelled MUST each resettable element's own reset algorithm run; changes made by those algorithms MUST NOT count as user changes, so they MUST NOT fire `input` events (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.23 Resetting a form) MUST.
- Given `:user-invalid`, then it MUST match only elements whose user validity is true, which is set when the element has been through a change or blur with a changed value or when submission has been attempted, and which is therefore distinct from `:invalid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes).
- Given a control the library renders as disabled without using a native disabled element, then `aria-disabled="true"` alone changes only the accessibility tree: the browser supplies no click suppression, no focus exclusion and no constraint-validation exemption, so every one of those behaviors MUST be implemented in script if they are wanted (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 and §4.15, contrasted with ARIA's `aria-disabled`) MUST.
- Given a native form control that is disabled, then the user agent MUST prevent any click events queued on the user interaction task source from being dispatched on it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 Enabling and disabling form controls: the disabled attribute) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST NOT be a focusable area at all, so it is neither click-focusable nor tab-reachable nor focusable by `.focus()`; "the element is not actually disabled" is a precondition of being a focusable area (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST be barred from constraint validation and MUST NOT contribute an entry to the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5; §4.10.22.4 Constructing the entry list) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then being disabled MUST NOT prevent programmatic modification: setting `value` or `checked` from script still works, so a "disabled" state in a headless library MUST NOT be relied on to freeze state (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5, explicit note).
- Given a `fieldset` with `disabled`, then every form control descendant MUST be disabled except those inside the fieldset's first `legend` child, and this propagates transitively through nested fieldsets (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.15 The fieldset element) MUST.
- Given the `:disabled` pseudo-class, then it MUST match any element that is "actually disabled", whose exhaustive list is a disabled `button`, `input`, `select` or `textarea`, an `optgroup` or `option` that is disabled or whose nearest ancestor `select` is disabled, a disabled `fieldset`, and a disabled form-associated custom element (WHATWG HTML: §4.15 Disabled elements; §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes) MUST.
- Given a button, then its `type` attribute has three states, `submit` which submits the form, `reset` which resets it, and `button` which does nothing, and both the missing-value default and the invalid-value default are the Auto state (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.6 The button element).
- Given a `button` in the Auto state with neither `command` nor `commandfor` present and whose parent is not a `select`, then it IS a submit button; a button in the explicit Submit Button state is also a submit button (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.6, submit button definition).
- Given a button's activation behavior, then it MUST run in this order: return if the element is disabled; return if its node document is not fully active; if it has a form owner, submit for a submit button, reset for the Reset state, or return for the Auto state; otherwise resolve the `commandfor` target and dispatch a cancelable `CommandEvent` named `command` at it; otherwise run the popover target activation behavior (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.6, activation behavior algorithm) MUST.
- Given a `command` attribute, then its valid keywords are `toggle-popover`, `show-popover`, `hide-popover`, `close`, `request-close`, `show-modal`, or a custom keyword starting with `--` that only dispatches the `command` event and has no built-in effect; missing and invalid values are the Unknown state (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.6, the command attribute).
- Given a `command` event that is cancelled with `preventDefault()`, or whose target has become disconnected, then the built-in command steps MUST NOT run (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.6, activation behavior algorithm) MUST.
- Given a `popovertarget` attribute, then its value MUST be the id of an element with a `popover` attribute in the same tree, and `popovertargetaction` has the states `toggle`, which is the default, `show` and `hide` (WHATWG HTML: §6.12.1 The popover target attributes) MUST.
- Given a button that is not a submit button, then it MUST be barred from constraint validation (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.6) MUST.
- Given a form with a default button, which is the first submit button in tree order owned by that form, then implicit submission MUST fire a `click` event at that button provided it has an activation behavior and is not disabled (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.22.2 Implicit submission) MUST.
- Given a form with no submit button, then implicit submission MUST return without submitting if the form has more than one field that blocks implicit submission, and MUST otherwise submit the form from the form element itself with user involvement `"activation"` (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.22.2) MUST.
- Given the fields that block implicit submission, then they are `input` elements owned by the form whose type is Text, Search, Telephone, URL, Email, Password, Date, Month, Week, Time, Local Date and Time, or Number (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.22.2).
- Given a submission, then a cancelable `submit` event carrying a `SubmitEvent` with a `submitter` property MUST be fired before the entry list is constructed, and cancelling it MUST abort submission (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.22.3 Form submission algorithm) MUST.
- Given a button that acts as a label's target, then a `label` element's labeled control is resolved either explicitly via `for`, whose value MUST be the id of a labelable element in the same tree, or implicitly as the first labelable descendant in tree order (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.4 The label element) MUST.
- Given a click on a `label`'s interactive content descendant, then the label's own activation behavior MUST do nothing, so activation MUST NOT be double-forwarded (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.4) MUST.
- Given a labeled control, then it MUST also match `:active` and `:hover` whenever its `label` does, but that matching MUST NOT count as being activated (WHATWG HTML: §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes).
- Given the labelable elements list, then it is exactly `button`, `input` whose type is not Hidden, `meter`, `output`, `progress`, `select`, `textarea` and form-associated custom elements, so a `div`-based control can never be the target of a `label` and MUST use `aria-labelledby` instead (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.2 Categories, labelable elements) MUST.
- Given `autofocus`, then at most one element MUST have it within a given nearest-ancestor autofocus scoping root, which is the nearest ancestor `dialog` or popover, or the document; the attribute applies to all elements, not only form controls (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.7 The autofocus attribute) MUST NOT for a second one in the same scope.
- Given page load autofocus, then the first eligible candidate in the queue wins, the processed flag is then set permanently, and the whole mechanism MUST be abandoned if focus has already moved elsewhere in the top document (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.7, flush autofocus candidates).

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given a native `button` element, then it MUST have discernible text from inner visible text, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, `title`, an implicit or explicit `label`, or a presentational role; missing it is critical impact and a section508 and WCAG 4.1.2 failure (axe: lib/rules/button-name.json, checks button-has-visible-text, aria-label, aria-labelledby, non-empty-title, implicit-label, explicit-label, presentational-role).
- Given `role="button"`, `role="link"` or `role="menuitem"` on a non-native element, then it MUST have an accessible name from visible text, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-command-name.json).
- Given a button implemented as `input[type="button"]`, `input[type="submit"]` or `input[type="reset"]`, then it MUST have a non-empty `value`, or a name from `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, `title` or a label, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/input-button-name.json, checks non-empty-if-present, non-empty-value).
- Given a `role="button"` element, then it MUST NOT contain any focusable widget-role descendant; a negative `tabindex` on the nested control is explicitly NOT an acceptable fix, because assistive technology can still reach it, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/nested-interactive.json, check no-focusable-content with its notHidden message; lib/rules/nested-interactive-matches.js, which matches only roles whose `childrenPresentational` is true).
- Given a `div`, `span`, `p` or heading given `tabindex="0"`, then it MUST carry a widget or composite role; `button` is a widget-type role so it satisfies the check, while `tooltip`, `dialog`, `alertdialog` and `tabpanel` satisfy only the scrollable-region branch, at minor impact and experimental (axe: lib/rules/focus-order-semantics.json, checks has-widget-role, valid-scrollable-semantics; lib/rules/inserted-into-focus-order-matches.js, which matches only non-natively-focusable elements with `tabindex > -1`).
- Given a `role` attribute on a host element that does not support it, then `aria-allowed-role` flags it as a best-practice violation at minor impact, with `allowImplicit: true` by default (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-role.json, check aria-allowed-role).
- Given a widget-role element with visible text that also carries `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby`, then the visible text MUST be part of the accessible name; this is WCAG 2.1 A 2.5.3 at serious impact, experimental, with `pixelThreshold: 0.1` and `occurrenceThreshold: 3` (axe: lib/rules/label-content-name-mismatch.json).
- Given `role="button"` in the axe role table, then `aria-expanded` and `aria-pressed` are its only role-specific allowed attributes, an accessible name is required, the name may come from content, and its children are presentational; it has no required attributes, no required owned elements and no required context (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, button).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a button that launches a dialog, then appending an ellipsis to the label, for example "Save as…", is the conventional way to signal that (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, about this pattern) MAY.
- Given a widget whose function is navigation rather than an action, then it MUST use the link role and not the button role, because appearance and role both need to match function (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, note).

## toggle

### Roles and attributes

- Given a two-state toggle button, then it MUST have `role="button"` plus an `aria-pressed` state (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a toggle button that is on, then `aria-pressed` MUST be `"true"`; when off, `aria-pressed` MUST be `"false"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a toggle button, then its label MUST NOT change when its state changes, so a mute button stays labelled "Mute" whether pressed or not (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, about this pattern, marked "Important").
- Given a design where the label does change between two words, for example "Mute" and "Unmute", then `aria-pressed` MUST NOT be used at all and the control is an ordinary command button (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a toggle button, then it MAY support a third partially-pressed state, which is the characteristic that distinguishes it from `switch` (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a toggle button, then all descendants are forced presentational by the user agent because `button` is in the children-presentational role list (aria-practices: content/practices/hiding-semantics/hiding-semantics-practice.html, roles that require presentational children).
- Given a toggle button, then `aria-pressed` MUST be present in the initial markup with an explicit value before any interaction, not added on first activation (aria-practices: test/tests/button_button.js, button-aria-pressed assertion asserting hasAttribute before click).

### Keyboard

- Given focus is on a toggle button, when Space is pressed, then the pressed state MUST flip (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus is on a toggle button, when Enter is pressed, then the pressed state MUST flip (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a toggle button reading `aria-pressed="false"`, when Enter is pressed once then again, then `aria-pressed` MUST read `"true"` then `"false"` (aria-practices: test/tests/button_button.js, key-enter assertion).
- Given a toggle button reading `aria-pressed="false"`, when Space is pressed once then again, then `aria-pressed` MUST read `"true"` then `"false"` (aria-practices: test/tests/button_button.js, key-space assertion).

### Pointer

- Given a toggle button, when it is clicked, then `aria-pressed` MUST flip exactly as it does on Enter or Space (aria-practices: test/tests/button_button-idl.js, button-aria-pressed assertion, which drives the toggle by click).
- Given an element with an activation behavior, then activation is always caused by a `click` event, and a non-pointer trigger's default action MUST be to fire `click` first (WHATWG HTML: §6.5 Activation behavior of elements) MUST.
- Given `element.click()` on a toggle button, then it MUST return early if the element is a disabled form control or if a click is already in progress, and MUST otherwise fire an untrusted synthetic `click` (WHATWG HTML: §6.5, the click() steps) MUST.

### Focus management

- Given a toggle button, then focus MUST remain on the button after activation, because toggling does not dismiss or change context (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, keyboard table, "focus typically remains on the button after activation").
- Given a toggle button, then its accessible name MUST NOT change on activation, so a screen reader user who re-reads the focused element after toggling hears the same name with a changed pressed state (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, about this pattern, marked Important).

### State

- Given a toggle button, when it is activated twice, then `aria-pressed` MUST return to its original value, i.e. the toggle is idempotent over two activations (aria-practices: test/tests/button_button.js, key-enter and key-space assertions via assertAttributeCanBeToggled).
- Given a toggle button whose pressed state changes, then `aria-pressed` MUST be updated on the same element that carries `role="button"`, not on a wrapper (aria-practices: content/patterns/button/button-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).

### Forms

- Given a control that mimics a native form control without using one, then it MUST be made form-associated deliberately: only `button`, `fieldset`, `input`, `object`, `output`, `select`, `textarea`, `img` and form-associated custom elements are form-associated elements, and only `button`, `input`, `select`, `textarea` and form-associated custom elements are submittable (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.2 Categories, form-associated elements, listed elements and submittable elements lists) MUST.
- Given a control with a `form` attribute, then that attribute's value MUST be the id of a `form` element in the control's tree, and only listed elements may carry it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3 Association of controls and forms) MUST.
- Given a form-associated element with no `form` attribute, then its form owner MUST be its nearest ancestor `form` element, and the owner MUST be recomputed whenever the element is inserted or removed, its `form` attribute changes, or a matching id appears or disappears (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3, reset the form owner algorithm) MUST.
- Given a headless control that needs form participation, then the supported mechanism is a form-associated custom element: set `static formAssociated = true`, call `attachInternals()` once, report the value with `setFormValue(value, state)`, and report validity with `setValidity(flags, message, anchor)`; a second `attachInternals()` call MUST throw `NotSupportedError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13 Custom elements, ElementInternals) MUST.
- Given `setFormValue(null)`, then the element MUST be excluded from the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setFormValue) MUST.
- Given `setValidity` called with any flag true, then the message argument MUST be non-empty or the call MUST throw `TypeError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setValidity) MUST.
- Given a form-associated custom element, then it MUST handle `formAssociatedCallback` when its form owner changes, `formResetCallback` when the owner is reset, `formDisabledCallback` with the new boolean when its disabled state changes, and `formStateRestoreCallback` with a reason of `"autocomplete"` or `"restore"` when the user agent restores its value (WHATWG HTML: §4.13.6, form lifecycle callbacks).
- Given a control that exposes constraint validation, then `willValidate` MUST return whether it is a candidate for constraint validation, `checkValidity()` MUST fire a cancelable `invalid` event and return false when constraints are unmet, `reportValidity()` MUST additionally report the problem to the user and MAY focus the element, and `setCustomValidity(error)` MUST normalize newlines in the message before storing it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3 The constraint validation API) MUST.
- Given a validity state, then the full set of flags is `valueMissing`, `typeMismatch`, `patternMismatch`, `tooLong`, `tooShort`, `rangeUnderflow`, `rangeOverflow`, `stepMismatch`, `badInput`, `customError` and `valid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3, ValidityState).
- Given a submitter with `formnovalidate`, or a form with `novalidate`, then its no-validate state is true and interactive constraint validation MUST be skipped on submission (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.6) MUST.
- Given a form that is reset, then a `reset` event MUST be fired that bubbles and is cancelable, and only if it is not cancelled MUST each resettable element's own reset algorithm run; changes made by those algorithms MUST NOT count as user changes, so they MUST NOT fire `input` events (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.23 Resetting a form) MUST.
- Given `:user-invalid`, then it MUST match only elements whose user validity is true, which is set when the element has been through a change or blur with a changed value or when submission has been attempted, and which is therefore distinct from `:invalid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes).
- Given a control the library renders as disabled without using a native disabled element, then `aria-disabled="true"` alone changes only the accessibility tree: the browser supplies no click suppression, no focus exclusion and no constraint-validation exemption, so every one of those behaviors MUST be implemented in script if they are wanted (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 and §4.15, contrasted with ARIA's `aria-disabled`) MUST.
- Given a native form control that is disabled, then the user agent MUST prevent any click events queued on the user interaction task source from being dispatched on it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 Enabling and disabling form controls: the disabled attribute) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST NOT be a focusable area at all, so it is neither click-focusable nor tab-reachable nor focusable by `.focus()`; "the element is not actually disabled" is a precondition of being a focusable area (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST be barred from constraint validation and MUST NOT contribute an entry to the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5; §4.10.22.4 Constructing the entry list) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then being disabled MUST NOT prevent programmatic modification: setting `value` or `checked` from script still works, so a "disabled" state in a headless library MUST NOT be relied on to freeze state (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5, explicit note).
- Given a `fieldset` with `disabled`, then every form control descendant MUST be disabled except those inside the fieldset's first `legend` child, and this propagates transitively through nested fieldsets (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.15 The fieldset element) MUST.
- Given the `:disabled` pseudo-class, then it MUST match any element that is "actually disabled", whose exhaustive list is a disabled `button`, `input`, `select` or `textarea`, an `optgroup` or `option` that is disabled or whose nearest ancestor `select` is disabled, a disabled `fieldset`, and a disabled form-associated custom element (WHATWG HTML: §4.15 Disabled elements; §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes) MUST.

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given a toggle button, then all of the `button-name`, `aria-command-name`, `nested-interactive`, `focus-order-semantics` and `label-content-name-mismatch` rules that apply to a command button apply unchanged (axe: lib/rules/button-name.json, aria-command-name.json, nested-interactive.json, focus-order-semantics.json, label-content-name-mismatch.json).
- Given `aria-pressed` on a `role="button"` element, then it is in the role`'`s allowed-attribute list, so `aria-allowed-attr` passes; putting `aria-pressed` on any other role fails at critical impact (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js button `allowedAttrs`; lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json).
- Given `aria-roledescription` on a toggle button, then `button` is one of the supported roles for it, though the whole rule ships disabled and is tagged deprecated in this version (axe: lib/rules/aria-roledescription.json, `supportedRoles` including button, `enabled: false`).
- Given a toggle button, then `aria-pressed` MUST take one of the ARIA-valid values, so `aria-valid-attr-value` flags a typo such as `"pressd"` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a binary input, then choosing between `button` + `aria-pressed`, `checkbox` + `aria-checked`, and `switch` + `aria-checked` is a semantics decision, not a behavior one: pick the role that matches both the visual design and the wording of the label (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, about this pattern).

## checkbox

### Roles and attributes

- Given a non-native checkbox, then it MUST expose `role="checkbox"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a checkbox, then it MUST have an accessible label from exactly one of: visible text content inside the `checkbox` element, a visible label referenced by `aria-labelledby`, or `aria-label` (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a checked checkbox, then `aria-checked` MUST be `"true"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given an unchecked checkbox, then `aria-checked` MUST be `"false"`, i.e. the attribute is present with an explicit false value rather than omitted (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a partially checked tri-state checkbox, then `aria-checked` MUST be `"mixed"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a set of checkboxes presented as a logical group with a visible label, then the checkboxes MUST be wrapped in an element with `role="group"` carrying `aria-labelledby` pointing at the group label (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given descriptive static text relevant to a checkbox or to a checkbox group, then the checkbox or group MUST carry `aria-describedby` referencing the description element (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a `role="checkbox"` element, then all of its descendants are forced presentational, so semantic markup inside the checkbox label MUST NOT be relied on (aria-practices: content/practices/hiding-semantics/hiding-semantics-practice.html, roles that require presentational children).
- Given a group of independent checkboxes, then every checkbox MUST have `tabindex="0"`, i.e. checkboxes do NOT use roving tabindex and each is its own tab stop (aria-practices: test/tests/checkbox_checkbox.js, checkbox-tabindex assertion).
- Given a `role="group"` wrapper around a checkbox set, then the wrapper MUST have `aria-labelledby` resolving to an element with non-empty text (aria-practices: test/tests/checkbox_checkbox.js, group-aria-labelledby assertion).
- Given a tri-state checkbox controlling a set of checkboxes, then its `aria-controls` MUST be a space-separated list naming every controlled checkbox id, and every id MUST resolve to exactly one element (aria-practices: test/tests/checkbox_checkbox-mixed.js, checkbox-aria-controls assertion, which splits on spaces and requires 4 ids).
- Given a tri-state checkbox, then it MUST have `tabindex="0"` (aria-practices: test/tests/checkbox_checkbox-mixed.js, checkbox-tabindex assertion).

### Keyboard

- Given focus is on a checkbox, when Space is pressed, then the checked state MUST change (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, keyboard interaction).
- Given a dual-state checkbox, the APG keyboard table lists Space only; Enter is NOT specified for the checkbox pattern, in contrast to `switch` where Enter is listed as optional (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html vs content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, keyboard tables).
- Given a checkbox reading `aria-checked="false"`, when Space is pressed then pressed again, then `aria-checked` MUST read `"true"` then `"false"` (aria-practices: test/tests/checkbox_checkbox.js, key-space assertion).
- Given a set of checkboxes, when Tab is pressed repeatedly, then focus MUST visit every checkbox in DOM order (aria-practices: test/tests/checkbox_checkbox.js, key-tab assertion via assertTabOrder).
- Given a tri-state checkbox followed by its controlled native checkboxes, when Tab is pressed repeatedly, then focus MUST visit the tri-state checkbox first and then each controlled checkbox in DOM order (aria-practices: test/tests/checkbox_checkbox-mixed.js, key-tab assertion).
- Given a tri-state checkbox in the `mixed` state, when Space is pressed three times, then `aria-checked` MUST read `"true"`, then `"false"`, then `"mixed"` again with the previously-checked subset restored (aria-practices: test/tests/checkbox_checkbox-mixed.js, key-space assertion).

### Pointer

- Given a checkbox, when it is clicked, then `aria-checked` MUST change exactly as it does on Space (aria-practices: test/tests/checkbox_checkbox.js, checkbox-aria-checked assertion, which drives state by click).
- Given a tri-state checkbox, when it is clicked, then it MUST advance through the same false-true-mixed cycle as Space, so clicks and key presses MUST share one state machine (aria-practices: test/tests/checkbox_checkbox-mixed.js, checkbox-aria-checked-mixed assertion, which uses three clicks).
- Given a checkbox activation whose `click` default action is prevented, then the speculative checkedness and indeterminateness flip MUST be rolled back (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.15 Checkbox state, legacy-canceled-activation behavior) MUST.

### State

- Given a tri-state checkbox that controls a group, when all group options are checked, then the controlling checkbox MUST read `aria-checked="true"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a tri-state checkbox that controls a group, when some but not all group options are checked, then the controlling checkbox MUST read `aria-checked="mixed"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a tri-state checkbox that controls a group, when no group options are checked, then the controlling checkbox MUST read `aria-checked="false"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a tri-state checkbox reading `false` or `mixed`, when it is activated, then every option in the group MUST become checked (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a tri-state checkbox reading `true`, when it is activated, then every option in the group MUST become unchecked (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given an implementation that remembers the option set from the last time the controlling checkbox was `mixed`, when the user activates the controlling checkbox a third time, then that remembered partially-checked set MAY be restored (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a tri-state checkbox, then the three-value cycle is a distinct state machine from the two-value cycle and the library MUST expose which cycle a given checkbox uses (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a tri-state checkbox with no controlled checkbox checked, when it is activated an even number of times, then `aria-checked` MUST return to `"false"` and no controlled checkbox MUST be checked (aria-practices: test/tests/checkbox_checkbox-mixed.js, checkbox-aria-checked-false assertion).
- Given a tri-state checkbox with exactly one of four controlled checkboxes checked, when it is activated an odd number of times greater than one, then `aria-checked` MUST return to `"mixed"` and exactly one controlled checkbox MUST be checked (aria-practices: test/tests/checkbox_checkbox-mixed.js, checkbox-aria-checked-mixed assertion, three clicks).
- Given a tri-state checkbox with all four controlled checkboxes checked, when it is activated an even number of times, then `aria-checked` MUST return to `"true"` and all four MUST remain checked (aria-practices: test/tests/checkbox_checkbox-mixed.js, checkbox-aria-checked-true assertion).
- Given a tri-state checkbox, then the remembered "last state" is stored per-checkbox so the mixed state can be recreated, in the APG example on a `data-last-state` attribute (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/examples/js/checkbox-mixed.js, setCheckboxes('last')).

### Forms

- Given a control that mimics a native form control without using one, then it MUST be made form-associated deliberately: only `button`, `fieldset`, `input`, `object`, `output`, `select`, `textarea`, `img` and form-associated custom elements are form-associated elements, and only `button`, `input`, `select`, `textarea` and form-associated custom elements are submittable (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.2 Categories, form-associated elements, listed elements and submittable elements lists) MUST.
- Given a control with a `form` attribute, then that attribute's value MUST be the id of a `form` element in the control's tree, and only listed elements may carry it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3 Association of controls and forms) MUST.
- Given a form-associated element with no `form` attribute, then its form owner MUST be its nearest ancestor `form` element, and the owner MUST be recomputed whenever the element is inserted or removed, its `form` attribute changes, or a matching id appears or disappears (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3, reset the form owner algorithm) MUST.
- Given a headless control that needs form participation, then the supported mechanism is a form-associated custom element: set `static formAssociated = true`, call `attachInternals()` once, report the value with `setFormValue(value, state)`, and report validity with `setValidity(flags, message, anchor)`; a second `attachInternals()` call MUST throw `NotSupportedError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13 Custom elements, ElementInternals) MUST.
- Given `setFormValue(null)`, then the element MUST be excluded from the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setFormValue) MUST.
- Given `setValidity` called with any flag true, then the message argument MUST be non-empty or the call MUST throw `TypeError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setValidity) MUST.
- Given a form-associated custom element, then it MUST handle `formAssociatedCallback` when its form owner changes, `formResetCallback` when the owner is reset, `formDisabledCallback` with the new boolean when its disabled state changes, and `formStateRestoreCallback` with a reason of `"autocomplete"` or `"restore"` when the user agent restores its value (WHATWG HTML: §4.13.6, form lifecycle callbacks).
- Given a control that exposes constraint validation, then `willValidate` MUST return whether it is a candidate for constraint validation, `checkValidity()` MUST fire a cancelable `invalid` event and return false when constraints are unmet, `reportValidity()` MUST additionally report the problem to the user and MAY focus the element, and `setCustomValidity(error)` MUST normalize newlines in the message before storing it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3 The constraint validation API) MUST.
- Given a validity state, then the full set of flags is `valueMissing`, `typeMismatch`, `patternMismatch`, `tooLong`, `tooShort`, `rangeUnderflow`, `rangeOverflow`, `stepMismatch`, `badInput`, `customError` and `valid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3, ValidityState).
- Given a submitter with `formnovalidate`, or a form with `novalidate`, then its no-validate state is true and interactive constraint validation MUST be skipped on submission (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.6) MUST.
- Given a form that is reset, then a `reset` event MUST be fired that bubbles and is cancelable, and only if it is not cancelled MUST each resettable element's own reset algorithm run; changes made by those algorithms MUST NOT count as user changes, so they MUST NOT fire `input` events (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.23 Resetting a form) MUST.
- Given `:user-invalid`, then it MUST match only elements whose user validity is true, which is set when the element has been through a change or blur with a changed value or when submission has been attempted, and which is therefore distinct from `:invalid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes).
- Given a control the library renders as disabled without using a native disabled element, then `aria-disabled="true"` alone changes only the accessibility tree: the browser supplies no click suppression, no focus exclusion and no constraint-validation exemption, so every one of those behaviors MUST be implemented in script if they are wanted (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 and §4.15, contrasted with ARIA's `aria-disabled`) MUST.
- Given a native form control that is disabled, then the user agent MUST prevent any click events queued on the user interaction task source from being dispatched on it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 Enabling and disabling form controls: the disabled attribute) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST NOT be a focusable area at all, so it is neither click-focusable nor tab-reachable nor focusable by `.focus()`; "the element is not actually disabled" is a precondition of being a focusable area (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST be barred from constraint validation and MUST NOT contribute an entry to the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5; §4.10.22.4 Constructing the entry list) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then being disabled MUST NOT prevent programmatic modification: setting `value` or `checked` from script still works, so a "disabled" state in a headless library MUST NOT be relied on to freeze state (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5, explicit note).
- Given a `fieldset` with `disabled`, then every form control descendant MUST be disabled except those inside the fieldset's first `legend` child, and this propagates transitively through nested fieldsets (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.15 The fieldset element) MUST.
- Given the `:disabled` pseudo-class, then it MUST match any element that is "actually disabled", whose exhaustive list is a disabled `button`, `input`, `select` or `textarea`, an `optgroup` or `option` that is disabled or whose nearest ancestor `select` is disabled, a disabled `fieldset`, and a disabled form-associated custom element (WHATWG HTML: §4.15 Disabled elements; §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes) MUST.
- Given a checkbox activation, then a legacy-pre-activation behavior MUST run before the click event's default action, flipping checkedness to its opposite value and setting indeterminateness to false, and a legacy-canceled-activation behavior MUST restore both to their prior values if the click's default action is prevented (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.15 Checkbox state) MUST.
- Given a checkbox whose activation completes, then an `input` event that bubbles and composes MUST be fired, followed by a `change` event that bubbles, in that order (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.15, input activation behavior) MUST.
- Given a required checkbox whose checkedness is false, then it MUST be suffering from being missing (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.15) MUST.
- Given the `indeterminate` IDL attribute, then it is NOT reflected as a content attribute, it is initially false, it only affects checkbox controls, and it is independent of checkedness, so a tri-state checkbox MUST track "mixed" separately from checked (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.15, indeterminateness) MUST.
- Given an indeterminate checkbox, then the control's selection SHOULD be obscured in the rendering (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.15).
- Given `:indeterminate`, then for a checkbox it follows the `indeterminate` IDL state rather than any content attribute (WHATWG HTML: §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes).

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="checkbox"`, then it MUST have an accessible name from visible text, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`, and there MUST NOT be a mismatch between a wrapping `label` and that name, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-toggle-field-name.json, checks has-visible-text, aria-label, aria-labelledby, non-empty-title, and none check no-implicit-explicit-label).
- Given `role="checkbox"`, then `aria-checked` is a REQUIRED attribute, so omitting it fails `aria-required-attr` at critical impact (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js checkbox `requiredAttrs`; lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json).
- Given a native `input[type="checkbox"]` carrying a stray `aria-checked` whose value disagrees with the real checkedness, then it MUST be removed or corrected; the failure message is "Remove aria-checked, or set it to \"${data.checkState}\" to match the real checkbox state" at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-conditional-attr.json, check aria-conditional-attr, fail.checkbox).
- Given a native `input[type="checkbox"]`, then it MUST have a label from an implicit wrapping `label`, an explicit `label[for]`, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, `title`, a non-empty `placeholder` or a presentational role, and the explicit `label` MUST NOT be hidden, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/label.json, checks implicit-label, explicit-label, hidden-explicit-label).
- Given a native checkbox labelled only by `title` or `aria-describedby`, then it SHOULD have a visible label instead; best practice at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/label-title-only.json, check title-only).
- Given a native checkbox with more than one `label` element, then that MUST be avoided; multiple labels are not widely supported and the result is reported as incomplete at moderate impact (axe: lib/rules/form-field-multiple-labels.json, check multiple-label).
- Given `role="checkbox"`, then it MUST NOT contain a focusable widget-role descendant, because `checkbox` has `childrenPresentational: true`, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/nested-interactive.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js checkbox).
- Given `role="checkbox"` in the axe role table, then its role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-readonly`, `aria-expanded` and `aria-required`, its superclass is `input`, a name is required, the name may come from content, and children are presentational (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, checkbox).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a binary input, then checkbox, switch and toggle button are often functionally interchangeable, and the role SHOULD be chosen to match both the visual design and the wording, so a "Lights" control reads better as a switch while an item in a "which must be included" checklist reads better as a checkbox (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a tri-state checkbox, then the canonical use is a group-controlling checkbox in an installer, where the mixed state means some but not all of the group is selected (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a tri-state checkbox, then only checkbox and toggle button support a third middle state; `switch` MUST NOT (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a group of checkboxes without a visible group label, then no `role="group"` wrapper is required by the pattern; the wrapper requirement is conditional on the presence of a visible label (aria-practices: content/patterns/checkbox/checkbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).

## radio-group

### Roles and attributes

- Given a set of radio buttons, then they MUST be contained in or owned by an element with `role="radiogroup"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given each radio button, then it MUST have `role="radio"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a checked radio, then `aria-checked` MUST be `"true"`; given an unchecked radio, `aria-checked` MUST be `"false"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a radio, then it MUST be labelled by its own content, by `aria-labelledby`, or by `aria-label` (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a radiogroup, then it MUST have a visible label referenced by `aria-labelledby`, or a label from `aria-label` (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given additional descriptive elements for either the group or an individual radio, then the `radiogroup` or `radio` MUST reference them with `aria-describedby` (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a `role="radio"` element, then all of its descendants are forced presentational by the user agent (aria-practices: content/practices/hiding-semantics/hiding-semantics-practice.html, roles that require presentational children).
- Given a radio group implemented with roving tabindex, then exactly one `radio` in the group has `tabindex="0"` and every other has `tabindex="-1"` at any moment (aria-practices: test/tests/radio_radio.js, radio-tabindex assertion via assertRovingTabindex).
- Given a radio group implemented with `aria-activedescendant`, then the `radiogroup` element MUST have `tabindex="0"` and the individual radios MUST NOT be tab stops (aria-practices: test/tests/radio_radio-activedescendant.js, radiogroup-tabindex and key-tab assertions).
- Given a radio group implemented with `aria-activedescendant`, then on load the `radiogroup` MUST already carry `aria-activedescendant` pointing at the radio that will be active when the group receives focus (aria-practices: test/tests/radio_radio-activedescendant.js, radiogroup-aria-activedescendant assertion).
- Given a radio group drawn with SVG, then the decorative wrapping `svg` MUST carry `role="none"` and each interactive `g` MUST carry `role="radio"` plus a non-empty `aria-label`, because a `g` has no text content to name it (aria-practices: test/tests/radio_radio-rating.js, svg-none, radio-role and radio-aria-label assertions).

### Keyboard

- Given a radio group not inside a toolbar, when Tab or Shift+Tab moves focus into the group and a radio is checked, then focus MUST land on the checked radio (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, keyboard table for radio groups not in a toolbar).
- Given a radio group not inside a toolbar with no radio checked, when focus moves into the group, then focus MUST land on the first radio (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on an unchecked radio, when Space is pressed, then that radio MUST become checked (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on an already-checked radio, when Space is pressed, then nothing changes (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, keyboard table, "checks the focused radio button if it is not already checked").
- Given focus on a radio in a group not inside a toolbar, when Right Arrow or Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the next radio, the previously focused radio MUST be unchecked, and the newly focused radio MUST be checked (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on the last radio, when Right Arrow or Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST wrap to the first radio and check it (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, keyboard table; test/tests/radio_radio.js, key-down-right-arrow assertion).
- Given focus on a radio, when Left Arrow or Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the previous radio, unchecking the old and checking the new (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on the first radio, when Left Arrow or Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST wrap to the last radio and check it (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, keyboard table; test/tests/radio_radio.js, key-up-left-arrow assertion).
- Given a radio group, then selection always follows focus for arrow keys, so a conformance test MUST assert focus position and `aria-checked` together after every arrow press (aria-practices: test/tests/radio_radio.js, key-down-right-arrow and key-up-left-arrow assertions, which check focus and aria-checked in the same step).
- Given a radio group using `aria-activedescendant`, when an arrow key is pressed, then DOM focus MUST stay on the `radiogroup` element while `aria-activedescendant` moves to the next radio and that radio becomes checked (aria-practices: test/tests/radio_radio-activedescendant.js, key-down-right-arrow assertion via assertAriaActivedescendant).
- Given a radio group nested inside a toolbar, when an arrow key moves focus among its radios, then the checked radio MUST NOT change, because users need to traverse all toolbar elements without altering selection (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, keyboard table for radio group in a toolbar).
- Given a radio group in a toolbar with focus on an unchecked radio, when Space is pressed, then the currently checked radio MUST be unchecked and the focused radio checked; if the focused radio is already checked, nothing happens (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, toolbar keyboard table).
- Given a radio group in a toolbar, when Enter is pressed on an unchecked radio, then it MAY check that radio in the same way Space does (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, toolbar keyboard table, marked optional).
- Given focus on a radio in a toolbar that is not the last radio in its group, when Right Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the next radio (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, toolbar keyboard table).
- Given focus on the last radio of a group in a toolbar where that radio is not the last toolbar element, when Right Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST leave the group and move to the next toolbar element (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, toolbar keyboard table).
- Given focus on the last radio of a group that is also the last toolbar element, when Right Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST wrap to the first toolbar element (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, toolbar keyboard table).
- Given focus on a radio in a toolbar that is not the first radio in its group, when Left Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the previous radio (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, toolbar keyboard table).
- Given focus on the first radio of a group in a toolbar where that radio is not the first toolbar element, when Left Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the previous toolbar element (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, toolbar keyboard table).
- Given focus on the first radio of a group that is also the first toolbar element, when Left Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST wrap to the last toolbar element (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, toolbar keyboard table).
- Given a radio group in a toolbar, when Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MAY move to the next radio within the group only, wrapping from last to first (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, toolbar keyboard table, marked optional).
- Given a radio group in a toolbar, when Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MAY move to the previous radio within the group only, wrapping from first to last (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, toolbar keyboard table, marked optional).

### Pointer

- Given a radio group, when a radio is clicked, then it MUST become checked, every sibling MUST become unchecked, and the roving `tabindex="0"` MUST move to the clicked radio so a later Tab lands there (aria-practices: test/tests/radio_radio.js, radio-aria-checked and key-tab assertions).
- Given a radio group using `aria-activedescendant`, when a radio is clicked, then `aria-activedescendant` MUST move to it while the group element remains the only tab stop (aria-practices: test/tests/radio_radio-activedescendant.js, radio-aria-checked and key-tab assertions).
- Given a radio activation whose `click` default action is prevented, then the previously checked radio MUST be restored, or this radio set to false if that radio is gone (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.16 Radio Button state, legacy-canceled-activation behavior) MUST.

### Focus management

- Given a radio group, when Tab moves focus into it after a radio has been checked by pointer, then focus MUST land on the checked radio rather than the first radio, i.e. the roving `tabindex="0"` MUST follow pointer selection as well as keyboard selection (aria-practices: test/tests/radio_radio.js, key-tab assertion, which clicks radio 2 then re-checks tab order).
- Given a radio group using `aria-activedescendant`, when Tab moves focus into it, then the tab stop MUST remain the `radiogroup` element regardless of which radio is active (aria-practices: test/tests/radio_radio-activedescendant.js, key-tab assertion).
- Given a radio group using `aria-activedescendant` where the newly active radio is scrolled out of view, then the implementation MUST scroll it into view itself, because the user agent will not, DOM focus having not moved (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/examples/js/radio-activedescendant.js, setChecked calls scrollIntoView; content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, aria-activedescendant section).
- Given an element with a negative `tabindex`, then the user agent MUST consider it a focusable area but SHOULD omit it from tabindex-ordered focus navigation, so `tabindex="-1"` is programmatically and click focusable but not Tab-reachable; this is the spec basis for roving tabindex (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3 The tabindex attribute) MUST for focusability, SHOULD for the omission.
- Given `tabindex="0"`, then the element MUST be included in the sequential focus navigation order at its DOM position (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3) MUST.
- Given positive `tabindex` values, then all of them come before every element with an omitted, invalid, zero or negative value, ordered ascending by value with ties broken by tree order; a headless library MUST NOT emit positive values (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3, processing model) MUST NOT.
- Given the `tabIndex` IDL attribute with no content attribute present, then it MUST default to 0 for `a`, `area`, `button`, `frame`, `iframe`, `input`, `object`, `select`, `textarea`, SVG and MathML `a`, and a `summary` that is its parent `details`'s summary, and to -1 otherwise (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3) MUST.
- Given an element to be a focusable area, then ALL of these MUST hold: its tabindex value is non-null or the user agent determines it focusable; it is either not a shadow host or has a shadow root whose `delegatesFocus` is false; it is not actually disabled; it is not inert; and it is being rendered, delegating rendering to its children, or used as canvas fallback content (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
