- Given an element can be focusable without being either sequentially focusable or click focusable, then a headless library MUST treat "focusable", "Tab-reachable" and "click-focusable" as three separate properties rather than one (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2, explicit clarification).
- Given a shadow host whose shadow root has `delegatesFocus: true`, then getting the focusable area MUST return the host's focus delegate: the first descendant with `autofocus`, otherwise the first focusable area in tree order, recursing into further delegating hosts, otherwise null (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.4 Focus, get the focusable area and focus delegate) MUST.
- Given the focusing steps, then they MUST return early when the resolved target's DOM anchor is inert, MUST no-op when the target is already the currently focused area, and MUST otherwise run the focus update steps, firing `blur` on the old chain and `focus` on the new chain with `relatedTarget` set (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.4, focusing steps and focus update steps) MUST.
- Given `element.focus(options)`, then `preventScroll` defaults to false, so scrolling into view is the DEFAULT and MUST be suppressed explicitly with `{ preventScroll: true }` (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.6 The focus() method) MUST.
- Given `element.blur()`, then the user agent SHOULD run the unfocusing steps but MAY selectively or uniformly do nothing, so a headless library MUST NOT rely on `blur()` to move focus anywhere in particular (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.6) SHOULD/MAY.
- Given a focus navigation scope, then each Document, shadow host, slot and popover trigger element owns its own tabindex-ordered focus navigation scope, and Tab traversal walks the flattened recursive composition of those scopes, skipping negative-tabindex elements (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.5 Sequential focus navigation; §6.6.2).

### State

- Given a radio group, then at most one radio MUST have `aria-checked="true"` at any time and every other radio in the same group MUST read `"false"` (aria-practices: test/tests/radio_radio.js, radio-aria-checked assertion, which checks this exhaustively for every possible selection).
- Given two radio groups on one page, then checking a radio in one group MUST NOT change `aria-checked` in the other (aria-practices: test/tests/radio_radio.js, radio-aria-checked assertion scoped per group).
- Given a radio group that starts with nothing checked, then all radios MUST read `aria-checked="false"` on load, i.e. an empty initial selection is a valid state (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, about this pattern; test/tests/radio_radio.js, radio-aria-checked assertion initial condition).

### Forms

- Given a control that mimics a native form control without using one, then it MUST be made form-associated deliberately: only `button`, `fieldset`, `input`, `object`, `output`, `select`, `textarea`, `img` and form-associated custom elements are form-associated elements, and only `button`, `input`, `select`, `textarea` and form-associated custom elements are submittable (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.2 Categories, form-associated elements, listed elements and submittable elements lists) MUST.
- Given a control with a `form` attribute, then that attribute's value MUST be the id of a `form` element in the control's tree, and only listed elements may carry it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3 Association of controls and forms) MUST.
- Given a form-associated element with no `form` attribute, then its form owner MUST be its nearest ancestor `form` element, and the owner MUST be recomputed whenever the element is inserted or removed, its `form` attribute changes, or a matching id appears or disappears (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3, reset the form owner algorithm) MUST.
- Given a headless control that needs form participation, then the supported mechanism is a form-associated custom element: set `static formAssociated = true`, call `attachInternals()` once, report the value with `setFormValue(value, state)`, and report validity with `setValidity(flags, message, anchor)`; a second `attachInternals()` call MUST throw `NotSupportedError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13 Custom elements, ElementInternals) MUST.
- Given `setFormValue(null)`, then the element MUST be excluded from the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setFormValue) MUST.
- Given `setValidity` called with any flag true, then the message argument MUST be non-empty or the call MUST throw `TypeError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setValidity) MUST.
- Given a form-associated custom element, then it MUST handle `formAssociatedCallback` when its form owner changes, `formResetCallback` when the owner is reset, `formDisabledCallback` with the new boolean when its disabled state changes, and `formStateRestoreCallback` with a reason of `"autocomplete"` or `"restore"` when the user agent restores its value (WHATWG HTML: §4.13.6, form lifecycle callbacks).
- Given a control that exposes constraint validation, then `willValidate` MUST return whether it is a candidate for constraint validation, `checkValidity()` MUST fire a cancelable `invalid` event and return false when constraints are unmet, `reportValidity()` MUST additionally report the problem to the user and MAY focus the element, and `setCustomValidity(error)` MUST normalize newlines in the message before storing it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3 The constraint validation API) MUST.
- Given a validity state, then the full set of flags is `valueMissing`, `typeMismatch`, `patternMismatch`, `tooLong`, `tooShort`, `rangeUnderflow`, `rangeOverflow`, `stepMismatch`, `badInput`, `customError` and `valid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3, ValidityState).
- Given a submitter with `formnovalidate`, or a form with `novalidate`, then its no-validate state is true and interactive constraint validation MUST be skipped on submission (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.6) MUST.
- Given a form that is reset, then a `reset` event MUST be fired that bubbles and is cancelable, and only if it is not cancelled MUST each resettable element's own reset algorithm run; changes made by those algorithms MUST NOT count as user changes, so they MUST NOT fire `input` events (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.23 Resetting a form) MUST.
- Given `:user-invalid`, then it MUST match only elements whose user validity is true, which is set when the element has been through a change or blur with a changed value or when submission has been attempted, and which is therefore distinct from `:invalid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes).
- Given a control the library renders as disabled without using a native disabled element, then `aria-disabled="true"` alone changes only the accessibility tree: the browser supplies no click suppression, no focus exclusion and no constraint-validation exemption, so every one of those behaviors MUST be implemented in script if they are wanted (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 and §4.15, contrasted with ARIA's `aria-disabled`) MUST.
- Given a native form control that is disabled, then the user agent MUST prevent any click events queued on the user interaction task source from being dispatched on it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 Enabling and disabling form controls: the disabled attribute) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST NOT be a focusable area at all, so it is neither click-focusable nor tab-reachable nor focusable by `.focus()`; "the element is not actually disabled" is a precondition of being a focusable area (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST be barred from constraint validation and MUST NOT contribute an entry to the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5; §4.10.22.4 Constructing the entry list) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then being disabled MUST NOT prevent programmatic modification: setting `value` or `checked` from script still works, so a "disabled" state in a headless library MUST NOT be relied on to freeze state (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5, explicit note).
- Given a `fieldset` with `disabled`, then every form control descendant MUST be disabled except those inside the fieldset's first `legend` child, and this propagates transitively through nested fieldsets (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.15 The fieldset element) MUST.
- Given the `:disabled` pseudo-class, then it MUST match any element that is "actually disabled", whose exhaustive list is a disabled `button`, `input`, `select` or `textarea`, an `optgroup` or `option` that is disabled or whose nearest ancestor `select` is disabled, a disabled `fieldset`, and a disabled form-associated custom element (WHATWG HTML: §4.15 Disabled elements; §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes) MUST.
- Given the native radio button group definition, then two radios are in the same group only when both are `type="radio"`, they share a form owner or both have none, they are in the same tree, and both have non-empty `name` attributes with equal values; a headless radio group MUST reimplement all four conditions itself (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.16 Radio Button state) MUST.
- Given a radio whose checkedness becomes true, then every other radio in the same group MUST be set to false, and this MUST be re-enforced whenever the radio's `name` is set, changed or removed, its form owner changes, a type change is signalled, or it becomes connected (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.16, mutual-exclusion algorithm) MUST.
- Given a radio activation, then legacy-pre-activation MUST record the previously checked radio and set this one checked, and legacy-canceled-activation MUST restore the previously checked radio, or set this one false if that radio is gone (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.16) MUST.
- Given any radio in a group marked required and every radio in that group unchecked, then the element MUST be suffering from being missing; requiredness is evaluated group-wide, not per radio (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.16) MUST.
- Given arrow-key move-and-check behavior for native radios, then the HTML spec defines NO algorithm for it: it is a platform convention, so the APG rule that arrows move focus and change selection is a de facto convention rather than a spec requirement (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3, which defers to platform conventions).

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="radio"`, then it MUST have an accessible name from visible text, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-toggle-field-name.json).
- Given `role="radio"`, then `aria-checked` is a REQUIRED attribute, so omitting it on any radio fails at critical impact (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js radio `requiredAttrs`; lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json).
- Given a native `input[type="radio"]` with a stray `aria-checked` disagreeing with its real checkedness, then it MUST be removed or corrected; the message is "Remove aria-checked, or set it to \"${data.checkState}\" to match the real radio state" at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-conditional-attr.json, fail.radio).
- Given `role="radio"` in the axe role table, then its role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-posinset`, `aria-setsize` and `aria-required`, it has NO `requiredContext`, so axe does NOT require a `radiogroup` ancestor even though the APG does, a name is required, the name may come from content, and children are presentational (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, radio).
- Given `role="radiogroup"` in the axe role table, then it is a composite role whose allowed attributes are `aria-readonly`, `aria-required`, `aria-activedescendant`, `aria-expanded` and `aria-orientation`, it has NO required owned elements, and `accessibleNameRequired` is false, marked a spec difference (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, radiogroup).
- Given a native `input[type="radio"]`, then the `label` and `label-title-only` and `form-field-multiple-labels` rules apply exactly as for checkbox (axe: lib/rules/label.json, label-title-only.json, form-field-multiple-labels.json).
- Given `role="radio"`, then it MUST NOT contain a focusable widget-role descendant, because `radio` has `childrenPresentational: true` (axe: lib/rules/nested-interactive.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js radio).
- Given a radio group implemented on `div` or `span` elements with `tabindex="0"`, then `radio` is a widget-type role and `radiogroup` is a composite-type role, so both satisfy `focus-order-semantics` (axe: lib/checks/aria/has-widget-role-evaluate.js; lib/standards/aria-roles.js).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a native HTML radio group with nothing selected, some browsers place focus on the last radio when Shift+Tab enters the group, whereas the APG pattern always specifies the first radio, so the library's behavior deliberately differs from some native behavior (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, note after the keyboard table).
- Given radio buttons in a toolbar, then they are frequently styled to look like toggle buttons, so visual appearance MUST NOT be used to infer which role or keyboard model applies (aria-practices: content/patterns/radio/radio-group-pattern.html, note after the toolbar keyboard table).
- Given the keyboard-navigation practice, then a radio group is a composite widget whose tab stop is the checked radio, in the same family as tabs, listbox and tree, and unlike menubar and toolbar which put the tab stop on the first item (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, keyboard navigation inside components).
- Given a disabled radio inside a radio group, then the APG convention is `aria-disabled="true"` where discoverability matters, keeping the control focusable, and the native `disabled` attribute where a user can infer the disabled control from its neighbors (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, focusability of disabled controls).

## switch

### Roles and attributes

- Given a switch, then it MUST have `role="switch"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a switch, then it MUST have an accessible label from exactly one of: visible text content inside the `switch` element, a visible label referenced by `aria-labelledby`, or `aria-label` (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a switch that is on, then `aria-checked` MUST be `"true"`; when off, `aria-checked` MUST be `"false"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a switch built on `input[type="checkbox"]`, then it MUST use the native `checked` attribute instead of `aria-checked` (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a switch, then `aria-checked` MUST NOT take the value `mixed`, because a switch is strictly binary, unlike checkbox and toggle button (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a group of switches with a visible label, then either wrap them in `role="group"` with `aria-labelledby`, or wrap them in an HTML `fieldset` whose label is in a `legend` (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given descriptive static text relevant to a switch or switch group, then the switch or group MUST reference it with `aria-describedby` (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a switch, then its label MUST NOT change when its state changes (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, about this pattern, marked "Important").
- Given a switch built from a non-native element, then it MUST carry `tabindex="0"` (aria-practices: test/tests/switch_switch.js, switch-tabindex assertion).
- Given a switch whose visual design shows literal "on" and "off" text inside the control, then those text nodes MUST carry `aria-hidden="true"` so they do not duplicate the state already conveyed by `aria-checked` (aria-practices: test/tests/switch_switch.js and switch_switch-button.js and switch_switch-checkbox.js, aria-hidden assertion).
- Given a switch built on a native `button`, then no `tabindex` is needed and the role is added to the `button` element itself (aria-practices: test/tests/switch_switch-button.js, switch-role assertion, which requires tag `button`).
- Given a `role="switch"` element, then all of its descendants are forced presentational by the user agent (aria-practices: content/practices/hiding-semantics/hiding-semantics-practice.html, roles that require presentational children).

### Keyboard

- Given focus is on a switch, when Space is pressed, then its state MUST change (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus is on a switch, when Enter is pressed, then its state MAY change; the APG marks Enter "(Optional)" for switch (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a switch reading `aria-checked="false"`, when Space is pressed then pressed again, then `aria-checked` MUST read `"true"` then `"false"` (aria-practices: test/tests/switch_switch.js, key-space assertion).
- Given a switch reading `aria-checked="false"`, when Enter is pressed then pressed again, then `aria-checked` MUST read `"true"` then `"false"`, and the APG example handles Enter and Space in one branch (aria-practices: test/tests/switch_switch.js, key-space assertion titled "key Enter turns switch on and off"; content/patterns/switch/examples/js/switch.js handleKeydown).
- Given a switch built on `input[type="checkbox"]`, when Space is pressed, then the native `checked` IDL property MUST flip and the implementation MUST NOT call preventDefault on the key event, because the browser supplies the toggle (aria-practices: test/tests/switch_switch-checkbox.js, key-space assertion via assertInputChecked; content/patterns/switch/examples/js/switch-checkbox.js has no keydown handler).
- Given a group of switches, when Tab is pressed repeatedly, then focus MUST visit each switch in DOM order; switches do not use roving tabindex (aria-practices: test/tests/switch_switch-button.js and switch_switch-checkbox.js, key-tab assertions).

### Pointer

- Given a switch, when it is clicked, then its state MUST change exactly as on Space (aria-practices: test/tests/switch_switch.js, switch-aria-checked assertion).
- Given a switch built on `input[type="checkbox"]`, when it is clicked, then the native `checked` IDL property MUST flip with no scripted handler involved (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/examples/js/switch-checkbox.js, which wires only focus and blur).

### State

- Given a switch, when it is clicked with a pointer, then `aria-checked` MUST flip in the same way as for a key press (aria-practices: test/tests/switch_switch.js, switch-aria-checked assertion).
- Given two switches in one group, then toggling one MUST NOT change the other, i.e. switches are independent unlike radios (aria-practices: test/tests/switch_switch-button.js, switch-aria-checked assertion, which toggles both independently).

### Forms

- Given a control that mimics a native form control without using one, then it MUST be made form-associated deliberately: only `button`, `fieldset`, `input`, `object`, `output`, `select`, `textarea`, `img` and form-associated custom elements are form-associated elements, and only `button`, `input`, `select`, `textarea` and form-associated custom elements are submittable (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.2 Categories, form-associated elements, listed elements and submittable elements lists) MUST.
- Given a control with a `form` attribute, then that attribute's value MUST be the id of a `form` element in the control's tree, and only listed elements may carry it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3 Association of controls and forms) MUST.
- Given a form-associated element with no `form` attribute, then its form owner MUST be its nearest ancestor `form` element, and the owner MUST be recomputed whenever the element is inserted or removed, its `form` attribute changes, or a matching id appears or disappears (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3, reset the form owner algorithm) MUST.
- Given a headless control that needs form participation, then the supported mechanism is a form-associated custom element: set `static formAssociated = true`, call `attachInternals()` once, report the value with `setFormValue(value, state)`, and report validity with `setValidity(flags, message, anchor)`; a second `attachInternals()` call MUST throw `NotSupportedError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13 Custom elements, ElementInternals) MUST.
- Given `setFormValue(null)`, then the element MUST be excluded from the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setFormValue) MUST.
- Given `setValidity` called with any flag true, then the message argument MUST be non-empty or the call MUST throw `TypeError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setValidity) MUST.
- Given a form-associated custom element, then it MUST handle `formAssociatedCallback` when its form owner changes, `formResetCallback` when the owner is reset, `formDisabledCallback` with the new boolean when its disabled state changes, and `formStateRestoreCallback` with a reason of `"autocomplete"` or `"restore"` when the user agent restores its value (WHATWG HTML: §4.13.6, form lifecycle callbacks).
- Given a control that exposes constraint validation, then `willValidate` MUST return whether it is a candidate for constraint validation, `checkValidity()` MUST fire a cancelable `invalid` event and return false when constraints are unmet, `reportValidity()` MUST additionally report the problem to the user and MAY focus the element, and `setCustomValidity(error)` MUST normalize newlines in the message before storing it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3 The constraint validation API) MUST.
- Given a validity state, then the full set of flags is `valueMissing`, `typeMismatch`, `patternMismatch`, `tooLong`, `tooShort`, `rangeUnderflow`, `rangeOverflow`, `stepMismatch`, `badInput`, `customError` and `valid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3, ValidityState).
- Given a submitter with `formnovalidate`, or a form with `novalidate`, then its no-validate state is true and interactive constraint validation MUST be skipped on submission (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.6) MUST.
- Given a form that is reset, then a `reset` event MUST be fired that bubbles and is cancelable, and only if it is not cancelled MUST each resettable element's own reset algorithm run; changes made by those algorithms MUST NOT count as user changes, so they MUST NOT fire `input` events (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.23 Resetting a form) MUST.
- Given `:user-invalid`, then it MUST match only elements whose user validity is true, which is set when the element has been through a change or blur with a changed value or when submission has been attempted, and which is therefore distinct from `:invalid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes).
- Given a control the library renders as disabled without using a native disabled element, then `aria-disabled="true"` alone changes only the accessibility tree: the browser supplies no click suppression, no focus exclusion and no constraint-validation exemption, so every one of those behaviors MUST be implemented in script if they are wanted (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 and §4.15, contrasted with ARIA's `aria-disabled`) MUST.
- Given a native form control that is disabled, then the user agent MUST prevent any click events queued on the user interaction task source from being dispatched on it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 Enabling and disabling form controls: the disabled attribute) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST NOT be a focusable area at all, so it is neither click-focusable nor tab-reachable nor focusable by `.focus()`; "the element is not actually disabled" is a precondition of being a focusable area (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST be barred from constraint validation and MUST NOT contribute an entry to the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5; §4.10.22.4 Constructing the entry list) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then being disabled MUST NOT prevent programmatic modification: setting `value` or `checked` from script still works, so a "disabled" state in a headless library MUST NOT be relied on to freeze state (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5, explicit note).
- Given a `fieldset` with `disabled`, then every form control descendant MUST be disabled except those inside the fieldset's first `legend` child, and this propagates transitively through nested fieldsets (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.15 The fieldset element) MUST.
- Given the `:disabled` pseudo-class, then it MUST match any element that is "actually disabled", whose exhaustive list is a disabled `button`, `input`, `select` or `textarea`, an `optgroup` or `option` that is disabled or whose nearest ancestor `select` is disabled, a disabled `fieldset`, and a disabled form-associated custom element (WHATWG HTML: §4.15 Disabled elements; §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes) MUST.

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="switch"`, then it MUST have an accessible name from visible text, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-toggle-field-name.json).
- Given `role="switch"`, then `aria-checked` is a REQUIRED attribute, so omitting it fails at critical impact; note that a native `input[type="checkbox"][role="switch"]` relies on native checkedness instead, which is the axe-relevant reason the APG checkbox-based switch example sets no `aria-checked` (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js switch `requiredAttrs`; lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json).
- Given `role="switch"` in the axe role table, then its superclass is `checkbox`, its role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-expanded`, `aria-readonly` and `aria-required`, a name is required, the name may come from content, and children are presentational (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, switch).
- Given `role="switch"`, then it MUST NOT contain a focusable widget-role descendant (axe: lib/rules/nested-interactive.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js switch `childrenPresentational`).
- Given `role="switch"` on a `div`, then `aria-allowed-role` checks whether the host element supports that role at minor impact, and `focus-order-semantics` is satisfied because `switch` is a widget-type role (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-role.json; lib/checks/aria/has-widget-role-evaluate.js).
- Given a native `input[type="checkbox"][role="switch"]`, then `label`, `label-title-only` and `form-field-multiple-labels` apply as for any native checkbox (axe: lib/rules/label.json, label-title-only.json, form-field-multiple-labels.json).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a binary choice whose natural wording is on/off, then `switch` produces better screen reader output than `checkbox`, for example "Lights switch on" rather than "Lights checkbox checked"; given a binary choice in a checklist framing, `checkbox` reads better (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/switch-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a switch built on `input[type="checkbox"]` with `role="switch"`, then no explicit `aria-checked` management is needed and the APG example ships no keydown handler at all, only a focus class toggle (aria-practices: content/patterns/switch/examples/js/switch-checkbox.js).

## slider

### Roles and attributes

- Given the focusable thumb of a slider, then it MUST have `role="slider"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a slider, then `aria-valuenow` MUST be set to a decimal value representing the current value (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a slider, then `aria-valuemin` MUST be set to a decimal value representing the minimum allowed value (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a slider, then `aria-valuemax` MUST be set to a decimal value representing the maximum allowed value (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a slider whose `aria-valuenow` is not user-friendly, for example a numeric day-of-week, then `aria-valuetext` MUST be set to a human-readable string (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a slider with a visible label, then that label MUST be referenced by `aria-labelledby` on the slider element; otherwise `aria-label` MUST supply the name (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a vertically oriented slider, then it MUST have `aria-orientation="vertical"`; the default for `slider` is `horizontal` (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, roles/states/properties; test/tests/slider_slider-temperature.js, aria-orientation assertion).
- Given a slider, then `aria-valuemin` and `aria-valuemax` only need to be set when the range differs from the default 0 to 100, but `aria-valuenow` is always required for `slider` (aria-practices: content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html, range widget table).
- Given a slider, then the thumb MUST carry `tabindex="0"` so it is in the page tab sequence (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-seek.js, slider-tabindex assertion).
- Given a slider drawn in SVG, then the wrapping `svg` MUST carry `role="none"` and the decorative rail `rect` elements MUST carry `aria-hidden="true"`, with `role="slider"` on the `g` that represents the thumb (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-seek.js, svg-none, aria-hidden-rect and slider-role assertions).
- Given a slider whose thumb is a `div` with a decorative inline `svg`, then the `svg` MUST carry `aria-hidden="true"` (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-rating.js, svg-aria-hidden assertion).
- Given a set of related sliders such as RGB channels, then they SHOULD be wrapped in `role="group"` with `aria-labelledby`, and each slider keeps its own `aria-labelledby` (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-color-viewer.js, group-role and aria-labelledby assertions).
- Given a `role="slider"` element, then all of its descendants are forced presentational by the user agent (aria-practices: content/practices/hiding-semantics/hiding-semantics-practice.html, roles that require presentational children).
- Given `aria-valuetext` is set, then assistive technology stops reporting `aria-valuenow`, so `aria-valuetext` MUST only be used where the bare number is insufficient (aria-practices: content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html, using aria-valuetext).

### Keyboard

- Given focus on a slider thumb, when Right Arrow is pressed, then the value MUST increase by one step (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a slider thumb, when Up Arrow is pressed, then the value MUST increase by one step (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a slider thumb, when Left Arrow is pressed, then the value MUST decrease by one step (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a slider thumb, when Down Arrow is pressed, then the value MUST decrease by one step (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a slider thumb, when Home is pressed, then the value MUST be set to the first allowed value in the range (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a slider thumb, when End is pressed, then the value MUST be set to the last allowed value in the range (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a slider thumb, when Page Up is pressed, then the value MAY increase by an amount larger than the Up Arrow step (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given focus on a slider thumb, when Page Down is pressed, then the value MAY decrease by an amount larger than the Down Arrow step (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given a vertical slider, Up and Right Arrow both increase and Down and Left Arrow both decrease, i.e. the APG examples do not remap axes for vertical orientation (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-temperature.js, key-up-arrow and key-right-arrow assertions on the vertical temperature slider).
- Given a slider whose step is fractional, when an arrow key is pressed, then `aria-valuenow` MUST be formatted to a fixed precision so repeated presses do not accumulate float drift; the APG temperature example uses `toFixed(1)` with a 0.1 step (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-temperature.js, key-right-arrow assertion asserting the exact string "25.1").
- Given a slider at its maximum, when an increasing key is pressed, then the value MUST stay at the maximum and MUST NOT wrap to the minimum (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-temperature.js and slider_slider-color-viewer.js, key-right-arrow assertions driving 200 to 260 extra presses).
- Given a slider at its minimum, when a decreasing key is pressed, then the value MUST stay at the minimum and MUST NOT wrap to the maximum (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-color-viewer.js, key-left-arrow assertion).
- Given a slider, when a key the slider handles is pressed, then the implementation MUST call `preventDefault` and `stopPropagation`, and MUST NOT do so for keys it does not handle (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/examples/js/slider-seek.js, onSliderKeydown flag pattern).
- Given the APG page-step conventions, then the big step is chosen per widget: 15 of 300 for the media seek slider, 2.0 against a 0.1 arrow step for the temperature slider, 2 of 10 for the rating slider, and 10 of 255 for each colour channel (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/examples/js/slider-seek.js, slider-temperature.js, slider-rating.js, slider-color-viewer.js).

### Pointer

- Given a slider thumb, when `pointerdown` fires on it, then the thumb MUST receive DOM focus, and the handler MUST call `preventDefault` and `stopPropagation` (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/examples/js/slider-seek.js, onSliderPointerDown).
- Given a drag in progress, when `pointermove` fires, then the value MUST be recomputed from the pointer coordinate along the rail and clamped to the range (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/examples/js/slider-seek.js, onPointerMove and moveSliderTo).
- Given a drag in progress, when `pointerup` fires anywhere in the document, then the drag MUST end; the up listener is on `document`, not on the thumb, so releasing outside the control still ends the drag (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/examples/js/slider-seek.js, document pointerup listener).
- Given a click on the slider rail away from the thumb, then the thumb MUST jump to the clicked position and MUST receive focus (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/examples/js/slider-seek.js, onRailClick).
- Given the APG slider examples, then they use Pointer Events only and register no touch-specific listeners, so touch support comes from pointer event coalescing (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/examples/js/slider-seek.js, slider-temperature.js, slider-color-viewer.js).
- Given a vertical slider, when dragging, then the value MUST be computed from the pointer Y coordinate with the axis inverted so a higher position is a higher value (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/examples/js/slider-temperature.js, moveSliderTo).
- Given a slider with discrete hit targets, as in the rating example, then pointer interaction MAY read a quantised `data-value` off the hit target rather than interpolating a coordinate, and focus is set on click and on pointerup rather than on pointerdown (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/examples/js/slider-rating.js, onTargetClick and onPointerUp).

### Focus management

- Given a slider, then focus MUST be placed on the thumb, that is on the object a pointer user would drag, not on the rail or a wrapper (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, note).
- Given a slider thumb, then it MUST be a stop in the page tab sequence via `tabindex="0"`; a slider is not a composite widget and does not use roving tabindex (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-seek.js, slider-tabindex assertion).
- Given a group of related sliders such as RGB channels, then each thumb MUST be its own tab stop; the `role="group"` wrapper groups them semantically without reducing the number of tab stops (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-color-viewer.js, tabindex assertion requiring `tabindex="0"` on all three).
- Given a multi-thumb slider, then the tab order MUST stay constant regardless of value and visual position, so a thumb that passes a sibling MUST NOT change where Tab goes (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider-multithumb/slider-multithumb-pattern.html, keyboard interaction).
- Given a slider thumb, when `pointerdown` fires on it or the rail is clicked, then it MUST receive DOM focus, so pointer and keyboard interaction stay consistent (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/examples/js/slider-seek.js, onSliderPointerDown and onRailClick).

### State

- Given any slider value change, then `aria-valuenow` MUST be updated and every dependent visible display MUST be updated in the same synchronous step; the colour viewer test asserts the rgb text input, the hex text input and the swatch background all match after each key press (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-color-viewer.js, testDisplayMatchesValue helper).
- Given a slider whose `aria-valuetext` includes a total or a scale description, then the APG examples use a terse form while the slider has focus and a verbose form on blur, so a conformance test MUST capture valuetext at a defined focus state (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/examples/js/slider-seek.js getValueTextMinutesSeconds flag, and slider-rating.js addTotalRectsToRatingLabel bound to blur).
- Given the media seek slider, then `aria-valuetext` MUST read as minutes and seconds with singular forms when the count is one, the minutes segment omitted when zero, and "0 Seconds" when the value is zero (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-seek.js, aria-valuetext assertion).
- Given the rating slider, then decreasing keys floor at `valuemin + 1` while Home alone reaches the true minimum, because the example treats "no rating" as reachable only by reset (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/examples/js/slider-rating.js, moveSliderTo clamp and Home handler; test/tests/slider_slider-rating.js, key-home and key-page-down assertions).

### Forms

- Given a control that mimics a native form control without using one, then it MUST be made form-associated deliberately: only `button`, `fieldset`, `input`, `object`, `output`, `select`, `textarea`, `img` and form-associated custom elements are form-associated elements, and only `button`, `input`, `select`, `textarea` and form-associated custom elements are submittable (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.2 Categories, form-associated elements, listed elements and submittable elements lists) MUST.
- Given a control with a `form` attribute, then that attribute's value MUST be the id of a `form` element in the control's tree, and only listed elements may carry it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3 Association of controls and forms) MUST.
- Given a form-associated element with no `form` attribute, then its form owner MUST be its nearest ancestor `form` element, and the owner MUST be recomputed whenever the element is inserted or removed, its `form` attribute changes, or a matching id appears or disappears (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3, reset the form owner algorithm) MUST.
- Given a headless control that needs form participation, then the supported mechanism is a form-associated custom element: set `static formAssociated = true`, call `attachInternals()` once, report the value with `setFormValue(value, state)`, and report validity with `setValidity(flags, message, anchor)`; a second `attachInternals()` call MUST throw `NotSupportedError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13 Custom elements, ElementInternals) MUST.
- Given `setFormValue(null)`, then the element MUST be excluded from the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setFormValue) MUST.
- Given `setValidity` called with any flag true, then the message argument MUST be non-empty or the call MUST throw `TypeError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setValidity) MUST.
- Given a form-associated custom element, then it MUST handle `formAssociatedCallback` when its form owner changes, `formResetCallback` when the owner is reset, `formDisabledCallback` with the new boolean when its disabled state changes, and `formStateRestoreCallback` with a reason of `"autocomplete"` or `"restore"` when the user agent restores its value (WHATWG HTML: §4.13.6, form lifecycle callbacks).
- Given a control that exposes constraint validation, then `willValidate` MUST return whether it is a candidate for constraint validation, `checkValidity()` MUST fire a cancelable `invalid` event and return false when constraints are unmet, `reportValidity()` MUST additionally report the problem to the user and MAY focus the element, and `setCustomValidity(error)` MUST normalize newlines in the message before storing it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3 The constraint validation API) MUST.
- Given a validity state, then the full set of flags is `valueMissing`, `typeMismatch`, `patternMismatch`, `tooLong`, `tooShort`, `rangeUnderflow`, `rangeOverflow`, `stepMismatch`, `badInput`, `customError` and `valid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3, ValidityState).
- Given a submitter with `formnovalidate`, or a form with `novalidate`, then its no-validate state is true and interactive constraint validation MUST be skipped on submission (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.6) MUST.
- Given a form that is reset, then a `reset` event MUST be fired that bubbles and is cancelable, and only if it is not cancelled MUST each resettable element's own reset algorithm run; changes made by those algorithms MUST NOT count as user changes, so they MUST NOT fire `input` events (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.23 Resetting a form) MUST.
- Given `:user-invalid`, then it MUST match only elements whose user validity is true, which is set when the element has been through a change or blur with a changed value or when submission has been attempted, and which is therefore distinct from `:invalid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes).
- Given a control the library renders as disabled without using a native disabled element, then `aria-disabled="true"` alone changes only the accessibility tree: the browser supplies no click suppression, no focus exclusion and no constraint-validation exemption, so every one of those behaviors MUST be implemented in script if they are wanted (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 and §4.15, contrasted with ARIA's `aria-disabled`) MUST.
- Given a native form control that is disabled, then the user agent MUST prevent any click events queued on the user interaction task source from being dispatched on it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 Enabling and disabling form controls: the disabled attribute) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST NOT be a focusable area at all, so it is neither click-focusable nor tab-reachable nor focusable by `.focus()`; "the element is not actually disabled" is a precondition of being a focusable area (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST be barred from constraint validation and MUST NOT contribute an entry to the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5; §4.10.22.4 Constructing the entry list) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then being disabled MUST NOT prevent programmatic modification: setting `value` or `checked` from script still works, so a "disabled" state in a headless library MUST NOT be relied on to freeze state (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5, explicit note).
- Given a `fieldset` with `disabled`, then every form control descendant MUST be disabled except those inside the fieldset's first `legend` child, and this propagates transitively through nested fieldsets (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.15 The fieldset element) MUST.
- Given the `:disabled` pseudo-class, then it MUST match any element that is "actually disabled", whose exhaustive list is a disabled `button`, `input`, `select` or `textarea`, an `optgroup` or `option` that is disabled or whose nearest ancestor `select` is disabled, a disabled `fieldset`, and a disabled form-associated custom element (WHATWG HTML: §4.15 Disabled elements; §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes) MUST.
- Given `input[type="range"]`, then its default minimum is 0, its default maximum is 100, its step scale factor is 1 and its default step is 1 (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.13 Range state).
- Given a range control whose value is not a valid floating-point number, then the value sanitization algorithm MUST set it to the best representation of the default value, which is the minimum plus half the difference between minimum and maximum, or the minimum when the maximum is less than the minimum (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.13) MUST.
- Given a range control suffering from underflow, then the user agent MUST set its value to the minimum; given overflow with a maximum not less than the minimum, it MUST set the value to the maximum; a range control therefore clamps rather than merely flagging, unlike `type="number"` (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.13) MUST.
- Given a range control suffering from a step mismatch, then the user agent MUST round the value to the nearest step-aligned number within the range, preferring the one nearest positive infinity when two are equidistant (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.13) MUST.
- Given a range control, then there MUST be no way to set its value to the empty string, so a headless slider has no empty state to model (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.13) MUST.
- Given a step mismatch, then it exists when the element has an allowed value step and the numeric value minus the step base is not an integral multiple of that step; `step="any"` means there is no allowed value step and the mismatch can never apply (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.3.8 The step attribute).

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="slider"`, then it MUST have an accessible name from `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`, and there MUST NOT be a mismatch with a wrapping `label`; note the rule does NOT accept visible text content as a name source for input fields, unlike the toggle-field rule, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-input-field-name.json, any checks aria-label, aria-labelledby, non-empty-title, none check no-implicit-explicit-label).
- Given `role="slider"`, then `aria-valuenow` is a REQUIRED attribute, so omitting it fails at critical impact; `aria-valuemin`, `aria-valuemax` and `aria-orientation` are allowed but NOT required, because they are implicit (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js slider `requiredAttrs` and its source comment; lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json).
- Given `role="slider"` in the axe role table, then its role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-valuemax`, `aria-valuemin`, `aria-orientation`, `aria-readonly`, `aria-required` and `aria-valuetext`, its superclasses are `input` and `range`, a name is required, and children are presentational (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, slider).
- Given `role="slider"`, then it MUST NOT contain a focusable widget-role descendant (axe: lib/rules/nested-interactive.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js slider `childrenPresentational`).
- Given a slider built on `input[type="range"]`, then it MUST have a label per the `label` rule at critical impact, SHOULD have a visible label rather than a `title`-only label at serious impact, and MUST NOT have multiple `label` elements at moderate impact (axe: lib/rules/label.json, label-title-only.json, form-field-multiple-labels.json).
- Given a slider thumb built on a `div` with `tabindex="0"`, then `slider` is a widget-type role so `focus-order-semantics` passes (axe: lib/checks/aria/has-widget-role-evaluate.js).
- Given `aria-valuenow`, `aria-valuemin` or `aria-valuemax` set to a non-numeric string, then `aria-valid-attr-value` fails at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json).
- Given a decorative `svg` inside a slider marked `role="none"` or `role="presentation"`, then it MUST NOT also carry a `tabindex` or a global ARIA attribute (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a slider, then focus MUST be placed on the thumb, that is on the element a pointer user would drag, not on the track or a wrapper (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, note).
- Given a slider where reversing the key direction would be more intuitive, for example a vertical control where Up Arrow lowers the value, then the direction MAY be reversed (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, note).
- Given a touch-based assistive technology, then slider operation depends on that AT synthesising key events for increase and decrease, which is a newer convention that is not universally implemented, so slider widgets SHOULD be tested on touch devices before shipping (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider/slider-pattern.html, warning).
- Given a multi-thumb slider, then each focusable thumb MUST have `role="slider"` and its own `aria-valuenow`, `aria-valuemin` and `aria-valuemax` (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider-multithumb/slider-multithumb-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a multi-thumb slider, then every thumb MUST be in the page tab sequence and MUST support the full single-slider key set (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider-multithumb/slider-multithumb-pattern.html, keyboard interaction).
- Given a multi-thumb slider, then the tab order MUST stay constant regardless of thumb value and visual position, so a thumb that moves past a sibling MUST NOT change where Tab goes (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider-multithumb/slider-multithumb-pattern.html, keyboard interaction).
- Given a multi-thumb slider whose thumbs constrain each other, then when one thumb's value changes, the dependent sibling's `aria-valuemin` or `aria-valuemax` MUST be updated to the new value (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider-multithumb/slider-multithumb-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a two-thumb range slider, then the upper thumb's `aria-valuemin` MUST always equal the lower thumb's current `aria-valuenow`, and the lower thumb's `aria-valuemax` MUST always equal the upper thumb's current `aria-valuenow` (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-multithumb.js, aria-valuemin and aria-valuemax assertions; content/patterns/slider-multithumb/examples/js/slider-multithumb.js moveSliderTo).
- Given a two-thumb range slider, when the lower thumb is driven upward past the upper thumb, then it MUST stop at the upper thumb's value; the thumbs may become equal but MUST NOT cross (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-multithumb.js, key-right-arrow assertion).
- Given the lower thumb, when End is pressed, then it MUST rise only to the upper thumb's current value, not to the absolute maximum (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-multithumb.js, key-end assertion).
- Given the upper thumb, when Home is pressed, then it MUST fall only to the lower thumb's current value, not to the absolute minimum (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-multithumb.js, key-home assertion).
- Given a two-thumb slider, then there is no enforced minimum gap between thumbs; they can converge on the same value (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider-multithumb/examples/js/slider-multithumb.js, moveSliderTo clamp).
- Given a click on the rail of a two-thumb slider, then the thumb nearer the click position MUST be the one that moves (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider-multithumb/examples/js/slider-multithumb.js, onRailClick).
- Given a drag on a two-thumb slider, then `pointermove` and `pointerup` MUST be listened for on `document` so a drag that leaves the control still tracks and still terminates (aria-practices: content/patterns/slider-multithumb/examples/js/slider-multithumb.js, onPointermove and onPointerup).
- Given a multi-thumb slider drawn in SVG, then each decorative rail and range `g` MUST carry `aria-hidden="true"` and the wrapping `svg` MUST carry `role="none"` (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-multithumb.js, svg-none and aria-hidden-g assertions).
- Given a multi-thumb slider where thumbs have no visible label of their own, then each thumb MUST have a non-empty `aria-label`; the APG multithumb example uses `aria-label` rather than `aria-labelledby` (aria-practices: test/tests/slider_slider-multithumb.js, aria-label assertion via assertAriaLabelExists).

## number-field

### Roles and attributes

- Given the focusable element serving as a number field, then it MUST have `role="spinbutton"`, and it is typically an element that supports text input (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a spinbutton, then `aria-valuenow` MUST be set to a decimal value representing the current value (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a spinbutton with a known minimum, then `aria-valuemin` MUST be set; if there is no known minimum, it MUST be omitted (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a spinbutton with a known maximum, then `aria-valuemax` MUST be set; if there is no known maximum, it MUST be omitted (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a spinbutton, then unlike every other range role it has NO default limits, so an unset `aria-valuemin` or `aria-valuemax` exposes no limit rather than 0 or 100, and an unset `aria-valuenow` exposes no value (aria-practices: content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html, range widget table and "range properties with spin buttons").
- Given a spinbutton whose `aria-valuenow` is not user-friendly, then `aria-valuetext` MUST be set to a readable string (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a spinbutton with a visible label, then that label MUST be referenced by `aria-labelledby`; otherwise `aria-label` MUST supply the name (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, roles/states/properties; test/tests/spinbutton_datepicker.js, spinbutton-aria-label assertion).
- Given a spinbutton whose current value is outside the allowed range, then `aria-invalid` MUST be `"true"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a spinbutton with a validation message, then `aria-errormessage` MUST reference the element containing that message (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_quantity.js, spinbutton-aria-errormessage assertion).
- Given increment and decrement buttons next to a spinbutton, then they MUST have `tabindex="-1"` so the text field is the only tab stop (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_quantity.js and spinbutton_datepicker.js, button-tabindex assertions).
- Given increment and decrement buttons, then they MUST reference the spinbutton with `aria-controls` and MUST have their own accessible name, supplied by `title` in the quantity example and by `aria-label` in the datepicker example (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_quantity.js button-aria-controls and button-title assertions; test/tests/spinbutton_datepicker.js button-aria-label assertion).
- Given a symbolic glyph inside an increment or decrement button, then it MUST carry `aria-hidden="true"` so it does not pollute the accessible name (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_quantity.js, span-aria-hidden assertion).
- Given adjacent "previous value" and "next value" peek displays next to a spinbutton, then they MUST carry `aria-hidden="true"` because they duplicate information already exposed (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_datepicker.js, spinbutton-aria-hidden assertion).
- Given a set of related spinbuttons such as day, month and year, then they SHOULD be wrapped in `role="group"` with `aria-labelledby` (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_datepicker.js, group-role and group-aria-labelledby assertions).

### Keyboard

- Given focus on a spinbutton, when Up Arrow is pressed, then the value MUST increase (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a spinbutton, when Down Arrow is pressed, then the value MUST decrease (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a spinbutton with a minimum value, when Home is pressed, then the value MUST be set to that minimum (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a spinbutton with a maximum value, when End is pressed, then the value MUST be set to that maximum (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a spinbutton, when Page Up is pressed, then the value MAY increase by a larger step than Up Arrow (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given a spinbutton, when Page Down is pressed, then the value MAY decrease by a larger step than Up Arrow (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given a spinbutton whose text field allows direct editing, then all standard single-line text editing keys for the platform MUST keep working, so the implementation MUST NOT capture key events used for input, cursor movement, selection or text manipulation (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, keyboard table and note, marked IMPORTANT).
- Given a spinbutton whose value accepts only certain characters, when a disallowed printable character is typed, then neither the field contents nor the spinbutton value MUST change (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, keyboard table, printable characters row).
- Given the APG quantity spinbutton, then only Up Arrow, Down Arrow, Home and End are handled and Page Up and Page Down are not implemented, so the optional big-step keys are genuinely optional (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/examples/js/quantity-spinbutton.js, handleKey).
- Given the APG date spinbuttons, then Up and Down change by 1 and Page Up and Page Down change by 5 for day, month and year alike (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/examples/js/spinbutton-date.js, key handling).
- Given a spinbutton, when a key it handles is pressed, then the handler MUST call `preventDefault` so the arrow key does not also scroll the page or move the text cursor (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/examples/js/quantity-spinbutton.js and spinbutton-date.js, handleKey).

### Pointer

- Given increment and decrement buttons, when one is clicked, then the value MUST change by a single step, not the big step (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/examples/js/spinbutton-date.js, handleIncreaseClick and handleDecreaseClick).
- Given a spinbutton with a paired `output` announcer, when the increment or decrement button is clicked, then the `output` MUST be updated with the new value; the APG quantity example deliberately announces on button click but not on arrow keys, because the field value itself is already announced when it changes (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_quantity.js, increment-button and decrement-button assertions; content/patterns/spinbutton/examples/js/quantity-spinbutton.js announce).
- Given an `output` announcer that was just populated, then it MUST self-clear after a configured delay, 2000 ms in the APG quantity example via a `data-self-destruct` attribute (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_quantity.js, increment-button assertion; content/patterns/spinbutton/examples/js/quantity-spinbutton.js).

### Focus management

- Given a spinbutton, then focus MUST remain on the text field throughout operation, including while the increment and decrement buttons are used (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, note).
- Given a spinbutton, then the increment and decrement buttons MUST NOT be reachable by Tab, because arrow keys already provide the same function from the field (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, about this pattern; test/tests/spinbutton_quantity.js button-tabindex assertion).

### State

- Given a spinbutton at its maximum, when an increasing key is pressed, then the value MUST clamp at the maximum with no wrap, when the widget is configured to clamp (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_quantity.js, spinbutton-up-arrow assertion).
- Given a spinbutton at its minimum, when a decreasing key is pressed, then the value MUST clamp at the minimum with no wrap, when the widget is configured to clamp (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_quantity.js, spinbutton-down-arrow assertion).
- Given a spinbutton configured to wrap, when the value would exceed the maximum, then it MUST wrap to the minimum, and when it would fall below the minimum it MUST wrap to the maximum; the APG date example wraps day and month and explicitly calls `noWrap()` on year (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/examples/js/spinbutton-date.js, setValue; test/tests/spinbutton_datepicker.js, spinbutton-page-up assertions).
- Given a spinbutton that wraps, when a big step overshoots the maximum by a non-exact amount, then it MUST still wrap to the minimum rather than clamping; the APG day spinbutton at 27 with a step of 5 and a maximum of 31 lands on 1 (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_datepicker.js, spinbutton-page-up assertion on day).
- Given a spinbutton whose maximum depends on another spinbutton, as day depends on month and year, then the maximum MUST be recomputed on every change of the dependency, and if the current value now exceeds the new maximum, the value MUST be forced down to that maximum (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/examples/js/datepicker-spinbuttons.js, updateSpinButtons).
- Given a spinbutton whose value was typed directly rather than stepped, then the value MUST NOT be silently clamped; it is kept as typed and flagged with `aria-invalid="true"` when outside the range, and `aria-invalid` MUST be absent, not `"false"`, when the value is valid (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_quantity.js, standard-single-line-editing-keys assertion via assertAttributeDNE).
- Given a spinbutton where non-numeric text such as "abc" or a bare space is typed, then the value MUST resolve to the minimum (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_quantity.js, standard-single-line-editing-keys assertion).
- Given a spinbutton, when the value reaches the minimum, then the decrement button MUST have `aria-disabled="true"`; when it reaches the maximum, the increment button MUST have `aria-disabled="true"`; otherwise the attribute MUST be absent rather than set to `"false"` (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_quantity.js, spinbutton-home and spinbutton-end assertions; content/patterns/spinbutton/examples/js/quantity-spinbutton.js updateButtonStates).
- Given a spinbutton whose emptied field loses focus, then the value MUST reset to the minimum where a minimum exists (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/examples/js/quantity-spinbutton.js, blur handler).
- Given a spinbutton whose values map onto names, as month does, then `aria-valuenow` carries the index and `aria-valuetext` carries the name; the APG month spinbutton uses `aria-valuemin="0"` and `aria-valuemax="11"` with names in `aria-valuetext` (aria-practices: test/tests/spinbutton_datepicker.js, spinbutton-aria-valuetext assertion).

### Forms

- Given a control that mimics a native form control without using one, then it MUST be made form-associated deliberately: only `button`, `fieldset`, `input`, `object`, `output`, `select`, `textarea`, `img` and form-associated custom elements are form-associated elements, and only `button`, `input`, `select`, `textarea` and form-associated custom elements are submittable (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.2 Categories, form-associated elements, listed elements and submittable elements lists) MUST.
- Given a control with a `form` attribute, then that attribute's value MUST be the id of a `form` element in the control's tree, and only listed elements may carry it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3 Association of controls and forms) MUST.
- Given a form-associated element with no `form` attribute, then its form owner MUST be its nearest ancestor `form` element, and the owner MUST be recomputed whenever the element is inserted or removed, its `form` attribute changes, or a matching id appears or disappears (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3, reset the form owner algorithm) MUST.
- Given a headless control that needs form participation, then the supported mechanism is a form-associated custom element: set `static formAssociated = true`, call `attachInternals()` once, report the value with `setFormValue(value, state)`, and report validity with `setValidity(flags, message, anchor)`; a second `attachInternals()` call MUST throw `NotSupportedError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13 Custom elements, ElementInternals) MUST.
- Given `setFormValue(null)`, then the element MUST be excluded from the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setFormValue) MUST.
- Given `setValidity` called with any flag true, then the message argument MUST be non-empty or the call MUST throw `TypeError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setValidity) MUST.
- Given a form-associated custom element, then it MUST handle `formAssociatedCallback` when its form owner changes, `formResetCallback` when the owner is reset, `formDisabledCallback` with the new boolean when its disabled state changes, and `formStateRestoreCallback` with a reason of `"autocomplete"` or `"restore"` when the user agent restores its value (WHATWG HTML: §4.13.6, form lifecycle callbacks).
- Given a control that exposes constraint validation, then `willValidate` MUST return whether it is a candidate for constraint validation, `checkValidity()` MUST fire a cancelable `invalid` event and return false when constraints are unmet, `reportValidity()` MUST additionally report the problem to the user and MAY focus the element, and `setCustomValidity(error)` MUST normalize newlines in the message before storing it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3 The constraint validation API) MUST.
- Given a validity state, then the full set of flags is `valueMissing`, `typeMismatch`, `patternMismatch`, `tooLong`, `tooShort`, `rangeUnderflow`, `rangeOverflow`, `stepMismatch`, `badInput`, `customError` and `valid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3, ValidityState).
- Given a submitter with `formnovalidate`, or a form with `novalidate`, then its no-validate state is true and interactive constraint validation MUST be skipped on submission (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.6) MUST.
- Given a form that is reset, then a `reset` event MUST be fired that bubbles and is cancelable, and only if it is not cancelled MUST each resettable element's own reset algorithm run; changes made by those algorithms MUST NOT count as user changes, so they MUST NOT fire `input` events (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.23 Resetting a form) MUST.
- Given `:user-invalid`, then it MUST match only elements whose user validity is true, which is set when the element has been through a change or blur with a changed value or when submission has been attempted, and which is therefore distinct from `:invalid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes).
- Given a control the library renders as disabled without using a native disabled element, then `aria-disabled="true"` alone changes only the accessibility tree: the browser supplies no click suppression, no focus exclusion and no constraint-validation exemption, so every one of those behaviors MUST be implemented in script if they are wanted (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 and §4.15, contrasted with ARIA's `aria-disabled`) MUST.
- Given a native form control that is disabled, then the user agent MUST prevent any click events queued on the user interaction task source from being dispatched on it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 Enabling and disabling form controls: the disabled attribute) MUST.
