- Given a disabled control, then it MUST NOT be a focusable area at all, so it is neither click-focusable nor tab-reachable nor focusable by `.focus()`; "the element is not actually disabled" is a precondition of being a focusable area (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST be barred from constraint validation and MUST NOT contribute an entry to the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5; §4.10.22.4 Constructing the entry list) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then being disabled MUST NOT prevent programmatic modification: setting `value` or `checked` from script still works, so a "disabled" state in a headless library MUST NOT be relied on to freeze state (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5, explicit note).
- Given a `fieldset` with `disabled`, then every form control descendant MUST be disabled except those inside the fieldset's first `legend` child, and this propagates transitively through nested fieldsets (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.15 The fieldset element) MUST.
- Given the `:disabled` pseudo-class, then it MUST match any element that is "actually disabled", whose exhaustive list is a disabled `button`, `input`, `select` or `textarea`, an `optgroup` or `option` that is disabled or whose nearest ancestor `select` is disabled, a disabled `fieldset`, and a disabled form-associated custom element (WHATWG HTML: §4.15 Disabled elements; §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes) MUST.
- Given `input[type="number"]`, then the user agent MUST NOT allow the user to set the value to a non-empty string that is not a valid floating-point number (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.12 Number state) MUST NOT.
- Given a number control whose value is not a valid floating-point number, then the value sanitization algorithm MUST set it to the empty string, so unlike range there IS an empty state (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.12) MUST.
- Given `type="number"`, then its step scale factor is 1 and its default step is 1 unless the step base has a non-integer value (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.12).
- Given a number control whose user interface describes input the user agent cannot convert to a valid floating-point number, then the control MUST be suffering from bad input (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.12) MUST.
- Given `stepUp(n)` or `stepDown(n)`, then it MUST throw `InvalidStateError` when the methods do not apply to the type or when there is no allowed value step, MUST no-op when the minimum exceeds the maximum or when no in-range step-aligned value exists, and MUST compute a delta of the allowed value step multiplied by n, negated for `stepDown` (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.4 Common input element APIs) MUST.
- Given `stepDown` whose result would be greater than the value before stepping, or `stepUp` whose result would be less, then the method MUST return without changing the value, which is what prevents stepping in the wrong direction from an out-of-range start (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.4) MUST.
- Given a number control's step mismatch, then the correction is only a MAY for `type="number"`, in contrast to the MUST-round rule for `type="range"` (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.5.1.12 compared with §4.10.5.1.13).

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="spinbutton"`, then it MUST have an accessible name from `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`, at serious impact; visible text content is NOT accepted as a name source (axe: lib/rules/aria-input-field-name.json).
- Given `role="spinbutton"` in the axe role table, then it has NO required attributes: `aria-valuenow`, `aria-valuemax` and `aria-valuemin` are allowed but not required, because they are implicit; its superclasses are `composite`, `input` and `range`; a name is required; children are NOT presentational, which is why increment and decrement buttons may legally live inside it (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, spinbutton).
- Given `role="spinbutton"` with nested increment and decrement buttons, then `nested-interactive` does NOT apply, because the rule only matches roles whose `childrenPresentational` is true and `spinbutton` is not one (axe: lib/rules/nested-interactive-matches.js; lib/standards/aria-roles.js spinbutton).
- Given a number field built on a native `input`, then `label` applies at critical impact, `label-title-only` at serious, and `form-field-multiple-labels` at moderate (axe: lib/rules/label.json, label-title-only.json, form-field-multiple-labels.json).
- Given `aria-errormessage` on a spinbutton, then the referenced element MUST exist, MUST NOT be hidden, and MUST use a technique that announces the message such as `aria-live`, `aria-describedby` or `role="alert"`; multiple ids in `aria-errormessage` is explicitly flagged as not widely supported, all at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-errormessage with its fail.unsupported and fail.hidden messages).
- Given a number field with an `autocomplete` attribute, then the value MUST be correctly formatted; a non-standard value is reported as incomplete, at serious impact, and the state terms `none`, `false`, `true`, `disabled`, `enabled`, `undefined`, `null`, `xoff` and `xon` are treated specially (axe: lib/rules/autocomplete-valid.json, check autocomplete-valid with its `stateTerms` and `ignoredValues` options).
- Given `role="spinbutton"` on a `div` with `tabindex="0"`, then `spinbutton` is a widget-type role so `focus-order-semantics` passes (axe: lib/checks/aria/has-widget-role-evaluate.js).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given text editing inside a spinbutton, then the most robust implementation relies on the browser's own editing behavior for `input[type="text"]` or a `contenteditable` element rather than reimplementing it (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, note) SHOULD.
- Given a spinbutton with a large range, then it SHOULD support both a small and a large step, for example 1 minute on Up Arrow and 10 minutes on Page Up (aria-practices: content/patterns/spinbutton/spinbutton-pattern.html, about this pattern).

## tabs

### Roles and attributes

- Given the container for a set of tabs, then it MUST have `role="tablist"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given each tab, then it MUST have `role="tab"` and MUST be contained within the `tablist` element (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given each content panel, then it MUST have `role="tabpanel"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a tablist with a visible label, then the `tablist` MUST have `aria-labelledby` referencing it; otherwise `aria-label` MUST supply the name (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given each `tab`, then it MUST have `aria-controls` referencing its `tabpanel` (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given the active tab, then it MUST have `aria-selected="true"` and every other tab MUST have `aria-selected="false"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given each `tabpanel`, then it MUST have `aria-labelledby` referencing its `tab` (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a tab with an associated popup menu, then it MUST have `aria-haspopup` set to `menu` or `true` (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a vertically oriented tablist, then it MUST have `aria-orientation="vertical"`; the default for `tablist` is `horizontal` (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given the APG tabs examples, then `role="tablist"` sits on a `div` and each `role="tab"` sits on a native `button` (aria-practices: test/tests/tabs_tabs-automatic.js, tablist-role and tab-role assertions).
- Given roving tabindex on tabs, then the selected tab MUST have NO `tabindex` attribute at all rather than `tabindex="0"`, and every unselected tab MUST have `tabindex="-1"`; the APG examples call `removeAttribute('tabindex')` on the selected tab (aria-practices: test/tests/tabs_tabs-automatic.js and tabs_tabs-manual.js, tab-tabindex assertions; content/patterns/tabs/examples/js/tabs-automatic.js setSelectedTab).
- Given a `role="tab"` element, then all of its descendants are forced presentational, so a heading inside a tab label is not exposed (aria-practices: content/practices/hiding-semantics/hiding-semantics-practice.html, roles that require presentational children, with `tab` named explicitly).
- Given `ul` and `li` markup reused as a tablist, then each intervening `li` MUST carry `role="presentation"` so the list-item semantics do not break the tablist-to-tab parent relationship (aria-practices: content/practices/hiding-semantics/hiding-semantics-practice.html, introduction example).

### Keyboard

- Given focus moving into the tab list with Tab, then focus MUST land on the active tab, not on the first tab (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus inside the tab list, when Tab is pressed, then focus MUST move to the next element in the page tab sequence outside the tablist, which is the tabpanel unless the first meaningful element inside the panel is focusable (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a tab in a horizontal tab list, when Left Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the previous tab, wrapping from the first tab to the last, and MAY also activate the newly focused tab (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a tab in a horizontal tab list, when Right Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the next tab, wrapping from the last tab to the first, and MAY also activate the newly focused tab (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a tab, when Space or Enter is pressed, then the tab MUST activate if it was not already activated automatically on focus (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a tab, when Home is pressed, then focus MAY move to the first tab and MAY also activate it (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given focus on a tab, when End is pressed, then focus MAY move to the last tab and MAY also activate it (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given a tab with an associated popup menu, when Shift + F10 is pressed, then that menu MUST open (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a deletable tab, when Delete is pressed, then that tab and its panel MAY be closed, focus MUST then be set on the tab that followed the deleted tab, and the newly focused tab MAY be activated (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given the deleted tab was the last tab in the list, when it is deleted, then focus MUST be set on the tab that preceded it (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, keyboard table, Delete row).
- Given an application that allows every tab to be deleted, when the last remaining tab is deleted, then focus MUST be moved to another element that provides a logical work flow (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, keyboard table, Delete row).
- Given Delete is supported, then the delete function SHOULD also, or instead, be available from a context menu (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, keyboard table, Delete row).
- Given a tablist with `aria-orientation="vertical"`, then Down Arrow MUST behave as Right Arrow and Up Arrow MUST behave as Left Arrow (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, note).
- Given a horizontal tablist, then it MUST NOT listen for Up Arrow or Down Arrow, so those keys keep their normal page-scrolling behavior while focus is inside the tab list (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, note).
- Given the APG tabs examples, then Ctrl+Page Up and Ctrl+Page Down are not implemented (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/examples/js/tabs-automatic.js and tabs-manual.js, no such key handling).
- Given automatic activation, when Right Arrow, Left Arrow, Home or End moves focus, then the newly focused tab MUST become `aria-selected="true"` and its panel MUST be displayed in the same step (aria-practices: test/tests/tabs_tabs-automatic.js, key-right-arrow, key-left-arrow, key-home and key-end assertions).
- Given manual activation, when Right Arrow, Left Arrow, Home or End moves focus, then only focus moves: the newly focused tab's panel MUST NOT be displayed and `aria-selected` MUST NOT change (aria-practices: test/tests/tabs_tabs-manual.js, key-right-arrow and key-left-arrow assertions, which assert the panel is explicitly not displayed).
- Given manual activation, when Enter is pressed on a focused tab, then its panel MUST be displayed and its `aria-selected` MUST become `"true"` (aria-practices: test/tests/tabs_tabs-manual.js, key-enter-or-space assertion).
- Given manual activation, when Space is pressed on a focused tab, then its panel MUST be displayed and its `aria-selected` MUST become `"true"` (aria-practices: test/tests/tabs_tabs-manual.js, key-enter-or-space assertion).
- Given automatic activation, when Home is pressed from any tab, then focus and selection MUST both move to the first tab; given manual activation, only focus moves (aria-practices: test/tests/tabs_tabs-automatic.js key-home assertion vs test/tests/tabs_tabs-manual.js key-home assertion).
- Given a tabs widget, then automatic activation SHOULD be the default as long as panels display without noticeable latency, which normally requires panel content to be preloaded, because automatic activation slows focus movement otherwise (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, note).
- Given a tabs widget where activating a tab triggers a network request or a page refresh, then selection MUST NOT follow focus, because the latency per arrow press makes the widget unusable for keyboard and screen reader users (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, deciding when to make selection automatically follow focus).
- Given selection does not follow focus, then the user changes selection by pressing Enter or Space (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, deciding when to make selection automatically follow focus).

### Pointer

- Given a tab, when it is clicked, then it MUST become selected, its panel MUST be displayed, every other tab MUST become `aria-selected="false"`, and the roving `tabindex` MUST move to the clicked tab in both the automatic and manual examples (aria-practices: test/tests/tabs_tabs-automatic.js and tabs_tabs-manual.js, tab-aria-selected and tab-tabindex assertions, which drive selection by click).
- Given manual activation, then click activation MUST still select the tab, because manual activation only decouples selection from ARROW-key focus movement, not from explicit activation (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/examples/js/tabs-manual.js, in which `setSelectedTab` is invoked only from the click handler).

### Focus management

- Given a tabpanel that contains no focusable elements, or whose first element with content is not focusable, then that tabpanel SHOULD have `tabindex="0"` so it enters the page tab sequence (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, note).
- Given the automatic-activation APG example whose panels contain no leading focusable element, then every `tabpanel` MUST have `tabindex="0"` and Tab from the selected tab MUST land on the panel itself (aria-practices: test/tests/tabs_tabs-automatic.js, tabpanel-tabindex and key-tab assertions).
- Given the manual-activation APG example whose panels begin with a link, then the panel itself is NOT given `tabindex="0"` and Tab from the selected tab MUST land on that first link (aria-practices: test/tests/tabs_tabs-manual.js, key-tab assertion targeting `#tabpanel-N a`).
- Given a tabs widget, then the whole tab list MUST occupy exactly one stop in the page tab sequence (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, fundamental keyboard navigation conventions).
- Given a tab clicked with a pointer, then the roving `tabindex` MUST be updated the same way as for a keyboard move, so a later Tab into the tablist lands on the clicked tab (aria-practices: test/tests/tabs_tabs-automatic.js, tab-tabindex assertion, which drives state with clicks; content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, note on pointer interactions).
- Given a tabs widget with per-tab action buttons, then the actions belonging to the focused tab wrapper join the roving group and are un-focusabled again on `focusout` when focus leaves the wrapper (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/examples/js/tabs-actions.js, makeTabAndActionsFocusable and focusout handler).
- Given an element with a negative `tabindex`, then the user agent MUST consider it a focusable area but SHOULD omit it from tabindex-ordered focus navigation, so `tabindex="-1"` is programmatically and click focusable but not Tab-reachable; this is the spec basis for roving tabindex (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3 The tabindex attribute) MUST for focusability, SHOULD for the omission.
- Given `tabindex="0"`, then the element MUST be included in the sequential focus navigation order at its DOM position (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3) MUST.
- Given positive `tabindex` values, then all of them come before every element with an omitted, invalid, zero or negative value, ordered ascending by value with ties broken by tree order; a headless library MUST NOT emit positive values (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3, processing model) MUST NOT.
- Given the `tabIndex` IDL attribute with no content attribute present, then it MUST default to 0 for `a`, `area`, `button`, `frame`, `iframe`, `input`, `object`, `select`, `textarea`, SVG and MathML `a`, and a `summary` that is its parent `details`'s summary, and to -1 otherwise (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3) MUST.
- Given an element to be a focusable area, then ALL of these MUST hold: its tabindex value is non-null or the user agent determines it focusable; it is either not a shadow host or has a shadow root whose `delegatesFocus` is false; it is not actually disabled; it is not inert; and it is being rendered, delegating rendering to its children, or used as canvas fallback content (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
- Given an element can be focusable without being either sequentially focusable or click focusable, then a headless library MUST treat "focusable", "Tab-reachable" and "click-focusable" as three separate properties rather than one (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2, explicit clarification).
- Given a shadow host whose shadow root has `delegatesFocus: true`, then getting the focusable area MUST return the host's focus delegate: the first descendant with `autofocus`, otherwise the first focusable area in tree order, recursing into further delegating hosts, otherwise null (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.4 Focus, get the focusable area and focus delegate) MUST.
- Given the focusing steps, then they MUST return early when the resolved target's DOM anchor is inert, MUST no-op when the target is already the currently focused area, and MUST otherwise run the focus update steps, firing `blur` on the old chain and `focus` on the new chain with `relatedTarget` set (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.4, focusing steps and focus update steps) MUST.
- Given `element.focus(options)`, then `preventScroll` defaults to false, so scrolling into view is the DEFAULT and MUST be suppressed explicitly with `{ preventScroll: true }` (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.6 The focus() method) MUST.
- Given `element.blur()`, then the user agent SHOULD run the unfocusing steps but MAY selectively or uniformly do nothing, so a headless library MUST NOT rely on `blur()` to move focus anywhere in particular (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.6) SHOULD/MAY.
- Given a focus navigation scope, then each Document, shadow host, slot and popover trigger element owns its own tabindex-ordered focus navigation scope, and Tab traversal walks the flattened recursive composition of those scopes, skipping negative-tabindex elements (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.5 Sequential focus navigation; §6.6.2).

### State

- Given a tabs widget, then exactly one tab MUST have `aria-selected="true"` and exactly one panel MUST be displayed at any moment (aria-practices: test/tests/tabs_tabs-automatic.js, tab-aria-selected assertion, which checks all four selections exhaustively).
- Given a deletable tabs widget, then deleting the last remaining tab MUST be refused; the APG actions example returns false rather than deleting (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/examples/js/tabs-actions.js, deleteTab).
- Given a reorderable tabs widget, then moving a tab past the first or last position MUST be refused, and a move MUST relocate the tab and its panel together in the DOM (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/examples/js/tabs-actions.js, relocateTab).

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="tab"`, then it MUST have an accessible name from visible text, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`; this is WCAG 4.1.2 A (axe: lib/rules/aria-tab-name.json).
- Given `role="tablist"`, then it MUST own at least one `role="tab"` child; a missing required child fails at critical impact, though `tablist` is in the `reviewEmpty` list, so a completely empty tablist is reported as incomplete ("Expecting ARIA child role to be added") rather than a hard failure (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-children.json, check aria-required-children with its reviewEmpty option including "tablist"; lib/standards/aria-roles.js tablist `requiredOwned`).
- Given `role="tab"`, then it MUST be contained by a `role="tablist"`; a missing required parent fails at critical impact with "Required ARIA parent role not present" (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-parent.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js tab `requiredContext`).
- Given a `role="tablist"` whose children include roles other than `tab`, then `aria-required-children` fails with "Element has children which are not allowed", and setting `aria-busy="true"` does NOT excuse disallowed children, only missing ones (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-children.json, fail.unallowed and fail.aria-busy-fail messages).
- Given `role="tab"`, then it MUST NOT contain a focusable widget-role descendant, because `tab` has `childrenPresentational: true`; a per-tab close button inside the `tab` element itself is therefore a serious-impact violation and MUST be a sibling instead (axe: lib/rules/nested-interactive.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js tab).
- Given `role="tab"` in the axe role table, then its role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-posinset`, `aria-selected`, `aria-setsize` and `aria-expanded`, its superclasses are `sectionhead` and `widget`, the name may come from content, children are presentational, and `accessibleNameRequired` is NOT set even though the `aria-tab-name` rule enforces a name (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, tab).
- Given `role="tablist"` in the axe role table, then it is a composite role whose allowed attributes are `aria-level`, `aria-multiselectable`, `aria-orientation`, `aria-activedescendant` and `aria-expanded`, with no required context and no required attributes (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, tablist).
- Given `role="tabpanel"` in the axe role table, then it is a structure role whose only role-specific allowed attribute is `aria-expanded`, its superclass is `section`, and `accessibleNameRequired` is false, marked a spec difference (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, tabpanel).
- Given a `tabpanel` given `tabindex="0"`, then `focus-order-semantics` is satisfied via the scrollable-region branch, not the widget-role branch, because `tabpanel` is a structure-type role and `tabpanel: true` appears in the valid-scrollable-semantics map (axe: lib/checks/aria/valid-scrollable-semantics-evaluate.js).
- Given hidden tab panels implemented with `aria-hidden="true"` rather than `hidden` or removal, then their focusable content MUST be made non-focusable or removed, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json).
- Given a scrollable tab list or panel container, then it MUST either contain a focusable element or itself be focusable, so Safari users can scroll it with the keyboard; this is WCAG 2.1.1 and 2.1.3 A at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/scrollable-region-focusable.json, checks focusable-content, focusable-element).
- Given `li` wrappers in a tablist marked `role="presentation"`, then they MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a tabs widget, then only one panel is displayed at a time and the tab list sits along one edge of the displayed panel, most commonly the top (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given the APG vocabulary, then "tabs" or "tabbed interface" means the whole set, "tab list" is the set of tab elements inside the `tablist`, "tab" is the label that can be activated, and "tabpanel" is the associated content container (aria-practices: content/patterns/tabs/tabs-pattern.html, terms).
- Given a tabs widget with per-tab action buttons, then those buttons MUST live outside the `tab` element itself, because `tab` forces its descendants presentational and axe flags focusable descendants of a `tab` at serious impact (aria-practices: content/practices/hiding-semantics/hiding-semantics-practice.html; axe: lib/rules/nested-interactive.json).
- Given a tab whose panel is displayed by a network request, then selection MUST NOT follow focus; see the Keyboard section (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, deciding when to make selection automatically follow focus).

## accordion

### Roles and attributes

- Given each accordion header title, then it MUST be contained in an element with `role="button"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/accordion-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given each accordion header button, then it MUST be wrapped in an element with `role="heading"` carrying an `aria-level` appropriate to the page's information architecture; a native HTML heading element with its implicit role and level MAY be used instead (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/accordion-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given an accordion header, then the `button` MUST be the only element inside the `heading`, so any other visually persistent controls MUST live outside the heading (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/accordion-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a visible accordion panel, then its header button MUST have `aria-expanded="true"`; given a hidden panel, `aria-expanded="false"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/accordion-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given an accordion header button, then it MUST have `aria-controls` set to the id of the panel content element (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/accordion-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a visible accordion panel in an implementation that does not allow collapsing, then its header button MUST have `aria-disabled="true"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/accordion-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given an accordion panel container, then it MAY have `role="region"` with `aria-labelledby` referencing the button that controls it (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/accordion-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given an accordion with more than roughly six simultaneously expandable panels, then `role="region"` SHOULD be avoided to prevent landmark proliferation (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/accordion-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given accordion panels that contain heading elements or a nested accordion, then `role="region"` is especially helpful for screen reader perception of structure (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/accordion-pattern.html, roles/states/properties) SHOULD.
- Given the APG accordion example, then every accordion button's parent element MUST be an `h3` (aria-practices: test/tests/accordion_accordion.js, h3-element assertion).
- Given the APG accordion example, then the element referenced by each button's `aria-controls` MUST carry `role="region"` and `aria-labelledby` (aria-practices: test/tests/accordion_accordion.js, region-role and region-aria-labelledby assertions).

### Keyboard

- Given focus on the header of a collapsed panel, when Enter or Space is pressed, then that panel MUST expand, and in a single-open implementation any other expanded panel MUST collapse (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/accordion-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on the header of an expanded panel, when Enter or Space is pressed, then the panel MUST collapse if the implementation supports collapsing; implementations that require one panel to stay open MUST NOT support a collapse function (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/accordion-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given an accordion, when Tab is pressed, then focus MUST move to the next focusable element, because every focusable element in the accordion is in the page Tab sequence; the accordion is not a composite widget and does not use roving tabindex (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/accordion-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given an accordion, when Shift+Tab is pressed, then focus MUST move to the previous focusable element (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/accordion-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given the APG accordion example, when Enter is pressed on a collapsed header, then its panel MUST become displayed with `aria-expanded="true"`, and other open panels MUST NOT close, i.e. the example allows multiple panels open (aria-practices: test/tests/accordion_accordion.js, key-enter-or-space assertion; content/patterns/accordion/examples/js/accordion.js, one independent instance per header).
- Given the APG accordion example with only one panel open, when Enter is pressed on that panel's header, then it MUST collapse; the example has no `aria-disabled` lockout (aria-practices: test/tests/accordion_accordion.js, key-enter-or-space assertion; content/patterns/accordion/examples/js/accordion.js, no aria-disabled logic).
- Given the APG accordion example, when Space is pressed on a header, then the same expand and collapse behavior as Enter MUST apply (aria-practices: test/tests/accordion_accordion.js, key-enter-or-space assertion).

### Pointer

- Given an accordion header button, when it is clicked, then the panel MUST toggle exactly as on Enter or Space, because the native button routes key activation through the same `click` handler (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/examples/js/accordion.js; test/tests/accordion_accordion.js key-tab assertion, which closes a panel by clicking).

### Focus management

- Given an accordion with one panel expanded, when Tab is pressed repeatedly, then focus MUST visit each header button and, interleaved at the right position, every focusable element inside the expanded panel only (aria-practices: test/tests/accordion_accordion.js, key-tab assertion).
- Given an accordion with all panels collapsed, when Tab is pressed repeatedly, then focus MUST visit only the header buttons (aria-practices: test/tests/accordion_accordion.js, key-tab assertion).
- Given an accordion, when Shift+Tab is pressed repeatedly from the last header, then the reverse order MUST mirror the forward order, including the inputs of an expanded panel (aria-practices: test/tests/accordion_accordion.js, key-shift-tab assertion).

### State

- Given a collapsed accordion panel, then it MUST be removed from the accessibility tree and from the tab sequence, not merely visually hidden; the APG example toggles the native `hidden` attribute (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/examples/js/accordion.js, toggle).
- Given an accordion header toggled to a state it is already in, then the toggle MUST be a no-op (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/examples/js/accordion.js, toggle early return).
- Given an accordion, then the initially expanded panel MUST read `aria-expanded="true"` on load while all others read `"false"` (aria-practices: test/tests/accordion_accordion.js, button-aria-expanded assertion).

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given an accordion header implemented as a native `button`, then `button-name` applies at critical impact; implemented with `role="button"`, `aria-command-name` applies at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/button-name.json, aria-command-name.json).
- Given an accordion header button carrying `aria-expanded`, then that attribute is in the `button` role`'`s allowed list so `aria-allowed-attr` passes; on a role that does not allow it, the same attribute fails at critical impact (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js button `allowedAttrs`; lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json).
- Given an accordion header with `aria-level` on the heading wrapper, then a value greater than 6 is reported as incomplete because such levels are not supported in all screen reader and browser combinations, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-level).
- Given an accordion header button, then it MUST NOT contain a focusable widget-role descendant, which is the axe-side reason the APG requires visually persistent controls such as a per-section menu button to live outside the heading (axe: lib/rules/nested-interactive.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js button `childrenPresentational`).
- Given `role="region"` on an accordion panel, then in the axe role table it is a landmark-type role whose only role-specific allowed attribute is `aria-expanded` and whose `accessibleNameRequired` is false, marked a spec difference (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, region).
- Given accordion panels hidden with `aria-hidden="true"` instead of `hidden` or `inert`, then their focusable content MUST be made non-focusable or removed, at serious impact; this is why the APG example toggles the native `hidden` attribute instead (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json).
- Given an accordion built on native `details` and `summary`, then the `summary` MUST have discernible text from visible text, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/summary-name.json, check has-visible-text).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

## collapsible

### Roles and attributes

- Given a disclosure, then the element that shows and hides the content MUST have `role="button"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/disclosure-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given visible disclosure content, then the `button` MUST have `aria-expanded="true"`; given hidden content, `aria-expanded="false"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/disclosure-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a disclosure, then the `button` MAY have `aria-controls` referencing the element that contains all the content shown or hidden (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/disclosure-pattern.html, roles/states/properties, marked optional).
- Given a disclosure, then it has exactly two parts: a button and a single region of content whose visibility the button controls (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/disclosure-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given every APG disclosure example, then `aria-controls` is present and MUST resolve to an existing element, so the library SHOULD treat it as required even though the pattern marks it optional (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_faq.js, disclosure_card.js, disclosure_image-description.js and disclosure_navigation.js, button-aria-controls assertions).

### Keyboard

- Given focus on the disclosure button, when Enter is pressed, then the disclosure control MUST activate and the content's visibility MUST toggle (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/disclosure-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on the disclosure button, when Space is pressed, then the disclosure control MUST activate and the content's visibility MUST toggle (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/disclosure-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a collapsed disclosure, when Enter is pressed twice, then `aria-expanded` MUST read `"true"` then `"false"` and the content MUST become displayed then hidden (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_faq.js, key-enter-or-space assertion).
- Given a collapsed disclosure, when Space is pressed twice, then the same round trip MUST occur as for Enter (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_faq.js, key-enter-or-space assertion).
- Given a disclosure built on a native `button`, then Enter and Space activation comes from the browser through a `click` event and the implementation MUST NOT add its own key handler for them (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/examples/js/disclosure-card.js, which wires only click/mousedown/mouseup).
- Given a disclosure navigation menu, then only one top-level menu MAY be open at a time: opening one MUST close any other that is open (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/examples/js/disclosureMenu.js, toggleExpand).
- Given focus on a top-level disclosure nav button, when Right Arrow or Down Arrow is pressed and that button's own menu is closed, then focus MUST move to the next top-level item (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_navigation.js, key-arrows assertion).
- Given focus on a top-level disclosure nav button whose own menu is open, when Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move into that menu onto its first link (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_navigation.js, key-arrows assertion).
- Given focus on the last top-level disclosure nav item, when Right Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST stay put: disclosure navigation does NOT wrap, unlike tabs and menubar (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_navigation.js, key-arrows assertion; content/patterns/disclosure/examples/js/disclosureMenu.js controlFocusByKey, which clamps with `min` and `max`).
- Given focus on the first top-level disclosure nav item, when Left Arrow or Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST stay put (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_navigation.js, key-arrows assertion).
- Given focus inside an open disclosure nav menu, when Down Arrow or Right Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the next link, clamping at the last link without wrapping (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_navigation.js, key-arrows assertion).
- Given focus inside an open disclosure nav menu, when Up Arrow or Left Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the previous link, clamping at the first link (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_navigation.js, key-arrows assertion).
- Given focus on a top-level disclosure nav item, when Home is pressed, then focus MUST move to the first top-level item; when End is pressed, to the last (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_navigation.js, key-home-end assertion).
- Given focus inside an open disclosure nav menu, when Home or End is pressed, then focus MUST move to the first or last link within that menu (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_navigation.js, key-home-end assertion).
- Given an open disclosure nav menu, when Escape is pressed with focus on a link inside it, then that menu MUST close and its button's `aria-expanded` MUST become `"false"`, with focus returning to the top-level button (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_navigation.js, key-escape assertion; content/patterns/disclosure/examples/js/disclosureMenu.js onMenuKeyDown).
- Given an open disclosure nav menu, when focus leaves the whole navigation, then that menu MUST close; the APG example listens for `focusout` on the nav root and checks that `relatedTarget` is not contained (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/examples/js/disclosureMenu.js, onBlur).
- Given a hybrid disclosure nav where each item has both a top-level link and a disclosure button, then all of those links and buttons MUST be in the page tab sequence, interleaved in DOM order (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_navigation_hybrid.js, key-tab assertion).
- Given a nav link marking the current page, then exactly one link across the whole navigation MUST have `aria-current="page"` at any time (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_navigation.js, link-aria-current assertion).
- Given a disclosure nav, then arrow-key navigation MAY be switched off entirely, leaving plain Tab navigation; the APG example exposes a `useArrowKeys` flag (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/examples/js/disclosureMenu.js, useArrowKeys).

### Pointer

- Given a disclosure card whose whole surface is clickable, when the click lands on a nested focusable element other than the disclosure button, or on a `label`, then the disclosure MUST NOT toggle (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/examples/js/disclosure-card.js, click handler guards).
- Given a disclosure card, when a click ends a text selection, then the disclosure MUST NOT toggle; the APG example checks `window.getSelection()` before toggling (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/examples/js/disclosure-card.js, click handler).
- Given a disclosure card, when it is toggled by clicking the card body, then focus MUST be moved to the disclosure button so the keyboard state stays consistent with the pointer state (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/examples/js/disclosure-card.js, which calls `button.focus()`).
- Given an element with an activation behavior, then activation is always caused by a `click` event, and when the user triggers it by any other means such as keyboard or voice, the default action of that interaction event MUST be to fire a `click` event at the element; the user agent SHOULD allow such non-pointer triggering (WHATWG HTML: §6.5 Activation behavior of elements) MUST for the click-firing, SHOULD for the allowance.
- Given a headless control that synthesizes activation, then the HTML spec defines no Enter or Space key binding for buttons at all: key activation is a platform convention the user agent supplies for native elements and which the library MUST therefore implement itself (WHATWG HTML: §6.5, which defers key triggering to the UA; §4.16.3 gives the Space-on-button `:active` example as non-normative prose).
- Given `element.click()`, then it MUST return early if the element is a disabled form control, MUST return early if a click is already in progress on that element, and MUST otherwise fire a synthetic pointer event named `click` with the not-trusted flag set, so `isTrusted` is false and the method is reentrancy-guarded per element (WHATWG HTML: §6.5, the click() steps) MUST.

### Focus management

- Given a disclosure button, then it MUST be an ordinary stop in the page tab sequence, reachable by forward Tab from the preceding element (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_image-description.js, key-tab assertion).
- Given a set of disclosures whose content holds no focusable elements, then expanding them MUST NOT add tab stops (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_faq.js, key-tab assertion, which checks the order before and after expanding all four).

### State

- Given a collapsed disclosure, then the content MUST be unreachable by Tab; the APG image-description example uses `display: none`, the card example uses the `inert` IDL property, and the accordion uses the `hidden` attribute (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/examples/js/disclosure-button.js, disclosure-card.js and content/patterns/accordion/examples/js/accordion.js).
- Given a collapsed disclosure card, then its `.details` container MUST have `inert` set, and expanding MUST clear `inert` (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_card.js, button-aria-expanded and key-enter-or-space assertions).
- Given an expanded disclosure card containing a link, a checkbox and a button, then Tab order MUST be header button, link, checkbox, button, per card (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_card.js, key-tab assertion).
- Given a set of independent disclosures, then expanding one MUST NOT collapse the others; the APG FAQ example creates one instance per button with no shared state (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/examples/js/disclosure-button.js; test/tests/disclosure_faq.js button-aria-expanded assertion, which expands all four).

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given a disclosure trigger implemented as a native `button`, then `button-name` applies at critical impact; with `role="button"`, `aria-command-name` applies at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/button-name.json, aria-command-name.json).
- Given a disclosure built on native `details` and `summary`, then the `summary` MUST have discernible text, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/summary-name.json).
- Given a disclosure trigger, then it MUST NOT contain a focusable widget-role descendant (axe: lib/rules/nested-interactive.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js button `childrenPresentational`).
- Given collapsed disclosure content hidden with `aria-hidden="true"`, then its focusable descendants MUST be made non-focusable or removed, at serious impact; the `inert` attribute used by the APG card example is the cleaner fix because axe`'`s `isFocusable` treats inert content as not focusable (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json; lib/commons/dom/is-focusable.js via focusDisabled).
- Given a disclosure button carrying `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`, then `aria-controls` MUST resolve to an existing element or the result is reported as incomplete rather than a pass, and the referenced id MUST be unique (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json check aria-valid-attr-value incomplete.noId; lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json).
- Given a disclosure whose visible label differs from an added `aria-label`, then the visible text MUST be part of the accessible name (axe: lib/rules/label-content-name-mismatch.json).
- Given a scrollable disclosure content region, then it MUST contain a focusable element or be focusable itself, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/scrollable-region-focusable.json).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a collapsed disclosure, then the button is conventionally styled with a right-pointing arrow or triangle, rotating to point down when expanded; this is presentation only and MUST NOT be exposed to assistive technology (aria-practices: content/patterns/disclosure/disclosure-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a widget that needs headings, a group-level show/hide model, or a required-open panel, then it is an accordion rather than a disclosure; disclosure has no heading wrapper requirement and no cross-panel state (aria-practices: content/patterns/accordion/accordion-pattern.html vs content/patterns/disclosure/disclosure-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).

## dialog

### Roles and attributes

- Given a modal dialog container, then it MUST have `role="dialog"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a modal dialog, then every element required to operate it MUST be a descendant of the element with `role="dialog"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a modal dialog, then the container MUST have `aria-modal="true"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a modal dialog, then it MUST have either `aria-labelledby` referring to a visible dialog title, or a label from `aria-label` (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a modal dialog, then `aria-describedby` MAY point at the element or elements describing its primary purpose, so a screen reader announces the description alongside the title and the initially focused element (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given dialog content that includes lists, tables or multiple paragraphs that need to be perceived structurally, then `aria-describedby` SHOULD be omitted, because the description is announced as a single unbroken string (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, roles/states/properties and note).
- Given a dialog marked `aria-modal="true"`, then the application MUST actually prevent all users from interacting with content outside it AND the visual styling MUST obscure that outside content; marking a dialog modal without both is severely harmful to assistive technology users (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, note, marked "only when both").
- Given a legacy dialog implementation that uses `aria-hidden` to make outside content inert, then `aria-hidden="true"` MUST be set on each element containing part of the inert layer, and the dialog element MUST NOT be a descendant of any element with `aria-hidden="true"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, note).
- Given `aria-modal="true"` is used, then it replaces the `aria-hidden`-on-background technique and the APG examples set no `aria-hidden` on siblings at all (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, note; content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js, which never toggles aria-hidden).
- Given the dialog container element, then it MUST NOT be made focusable; the APG example carries an explicit source comment saying not to make the `role="dialog"` element focusable, and no dialog container in the example markup carries `tabindex` (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js comment; test/tests/dialog-modal_dialog.js markup structure).
- Given the APG modal dialog example, then `role="dialog"` sits on a `div` and every one of the four dialogs MUST carry `aria-modal="true"` and a resolvable `aria-labelledby` (aria-practices: test/tests/dialog-modal_dialog.js, dialog-role, aria-modal and aria-labelledby assertions).

### Keyboard

- Given an open modal dialog, when Tab is pressed, then focus MUST move to the next tabbable element inside the dialog (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on the last tabbable element inside a modal dialog, when Tab is pressed, then focus MUST wrap to the first tabbable element inside the dialog (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, keyboard table; test/tests/dialog-modal_dialog.js key-tab assertion).
- Given an open modal dialog, when Shift+Tab is pressed, then focus MUST move to the previous tabbable element inside the dialog (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on the first tabbable element inside a modal dialog, when Shift+Tab is pressed, then focus MUST wrap to the last tabbable element inside the dialog (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, keyboard table; test/tests/dialog-modal_dialog.js key-shift-tab assertion).
- Given an open modal dialog, when Escape is pressed, then the dialog MUST close (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given an open modal dialog, when Escape is pressed while focus is on any focusable element inside it, then the dialog MUST close; the APG test verifies this from every focusable element in turn (aria-practices: test/tests/dialog-modal_dialog.js, key-escape assertion).
- Given a modal dialog containing exactly one focusable element, when Tab or Shift+Tab is pressed, then focus MUST stay on that element (aria-practices: test/tests/dialog-modal_dialog.js, key-tab and key-shift-tab assertions for dialog4).
- Given a modal dialog, then Tab and Shift+Tab MUST NOT move focus outside the dialog, and unlike a non-modal dialog there MUST be no way to move keyboard focus outside without closing the dialog (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given the term "tabbable element" in this pattern, then it means any element with a `tabindex` value of zero or greater; values greater than 0 are strongly discouraged (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, keyboard interaction preamble).

### Pointer

- Given a modal dialog, then backdrop click dismissal is NOT part of the pattern and the APG example wires no click handler on the backdrop element, so a library that adds it is going beyond the baseline (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js).
- Given content outside an active dialog, then an attempt to interact with it MAY cause the dialog to close, which is one of the two conventional visual and behavioral signals that the outside content is inert (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a dialog-style popup date picker, then a capturing `pointerup` listener on `document.body` MUST close it when the click lands outside both the toggle button and the dialog, and that path MUST NOT restore focus to the toggle (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/datepicker-dialog.js, handleBackgroundMouseUp).
- Given a modal dialog implemented with the native `dialog` element and `closedby="any"`, then light dismiss requires a matching `pointerdown` and `pointerup` pair on the same non-dialog target, so a drag that starts inside the dialog and ends outside MUST NOT dismiss it (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.5 Dialog light dismiss) MUST NOT.

### Focus management

- Given a dialog that opens, then focus MUST move to an element contained in the dialog; generally the first focusable element, but the most appropriate placement depends on the nature and size of the content (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, keyboard table and note).
- Given dialog content with semantic structures that must be perceived to be understood, then a static element at the start of the content SHOULD be given `tabindex="-1"` and focused initially, and `aria-describedby` SHOULD be omitted from the dialog container in that case (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, note).
- Given dialog content large enough that focusing the first interactive element would scroll the start of the content out of view, then a static element at the top such as the title SHOULD be given `tabindex="-1"` and focused initially (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, note).
- Given a dialog that confirms an action that is not easily reversible, such as deleting data or completing a financial transaction, then focus SHOULD be set on the least destructive action (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, note).
- Given a dialog limited to providing information or continuing processing, then focus SHOULD be set on the element most likely to be used, such as an OK or Continue button (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, note).
- Given a dialog that closes, then focus MUST return to the element that invoked it (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, note).
- Given a dialog that closes whose invoking element no longer exists, then focus MUST be set on another element that provides a logical work flow (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, note).
- Given a dialog whose completed task feeds directly into a subsequent step, and where users are very unlikely to need to re-invoke the dialog, then focus MAY be moved to that next step instead of back to the invoker, for example into the first cell of newly added grid rows (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, note).
- Given any dialog, then its tab sequence SHOULD include a visible element with `role="button"` that closes it, such as a close icon or cancel button; the APG marks this "strongly recommended" (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, note).
- Given a dialog whose initial focus target is a static element, then that element MUST be given `tabindex="-1"` so it is programmatically focusable without entering the tab sequence, and the initial Tab from it MUST land on the first real tabbable element (aria-practices: test/tests/dialog-modal_dialog.js, key-tab assertion for dialog2 and its `<p tabindex="-1" id="dialog2_para1">`).
- Given a dialog whose initial focus should be an element other than the first in DOM order, then the initial focus target MUST be passed explicitly rather than derived from DOM order; the APG example passes a `focusFirst` id (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js constructor; test/tests/dialog-modal_dialog.js dialog3 notes).
- Given a dialog implementation, then the element to restore focus to on close MUST be captured at open time and supplied explicitly rather than inferred; the APG example takes a required `focusAfterClosed` argument (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js, close).
- Given an element with a negative `tabindex`, then the user agent MUST consider it a focusable area but SHOULD omit it from tabindex-ordered focus navigation, so `tabindex="-1"` is programmatically and click focusable but not Tab-reachable; this is the spec basis for roving tabindex (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3 The tabindex attribute) MUST for focusability, SHOULD for the omission.
- Given `tabindex="0"`, then the element MUST be included in the sequential focus navigation order at its DOM position (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3) MUST.
- Given positive `tabindex` values, then all of them come before every element with an omitted, invalid, zero or negative value, ordered ascending by value with ties broken by tree order; a headless library MUST NOT emit positive values (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3, processing model) MUST NOT.
- Given the `tabIndex` IDL attribute with no content attribute present, then it MUST default to 0 for `a`, `area`, `button`, `frame`, `iframe`, `input`, `object`, `select`, `textarea`, SVG and MathML `a`, and a `summary` that is its parent `details`'s summary, and to -1 otherwise (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3) MUST.
- Given an element to be a focusable area, then ALL of these MUST hold: its tabindex value is non-null or the user agent determines it focusable; it is either not a shadow host or has a shadow root whose `delegatesFocus` is false; it is not actually disabled; it is not inert; and it is being rendered, delegating rendering to its children, or used as canvas fallback content (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
- Given an element can be focusable without being either sequentially focusable or click focusable, then a headless library MUST treat "focusable", "Tab-reachable" and "click-focusable" as three separate properties rather than one (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2, explicit clarification).
- Given a shadow host whose shadow root has `delegatesFocus: true`, then getting the focusable area MUST return the host's focus delegate: the first descendant with `autofocus`, otherwise the first focusable area in tree order, recursing into further delegating hosts, otherwise null (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.4 Focus, get the focusable area and focus delegate) MUST.
- Given the focusing steps, then they MUST return early when the resolved target's DOM anchor is inert, MUST no-op when the target is already the currently focused area, and MUST otherwise run the focus update steps, firing `blur` on the old chain and `focus` on the new chain with `relatedTarget` set (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.4, focusing steps and focus update steps) MUST.
- Given `element.focus(options)`, then `preventScroll` defaults to false, so scrolling into view is the DEFAULT and MUST be suppressed explicitly with `{ preventScroll: true }` (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.6 The focus() method) MUST.
- Given `element.blur()`, then the user agent SHOULD run the unfocusing steps but MAY selectively or uniformly do nothing, so a headless library MUST NOT rely on `blur()` to move focus anywhere in particular (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.6) SHOULD/MAY.
- Given a focus navigation scope, then each Document, shadow host, slot and popover trigger element owns its own tabindex-ordered focus navigation scope, and Tab traversal walks the flattened recursive composition of those scopes, skipping negative-tabindex elements (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.5 Sequential focus navigation; §6.6.2).

### State

- Given a dialog, then its open state MUST be reflected in the DOM: the native element uses the `open` boolean attribute, and a `dialog` without it SHOULD NOT be shown (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4 The dialog element, the open attribute).
- Given a dialog closed with a return value, then `returnValue` MUST be set before the `close` event is fired, and `close()` with no argument MUST set it to null (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, close the dialog algorithm) MUST.
- Given a modal dialog, then an "is modal" flag MUST be tracked separately from the open state, because only a modal dialog is added to and removed from the top layer (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, show a modal dialog and close the dialog) MUST.
- Given stacked dialogs, then the stack MUST be tracked explicitly so Escape closes only the top one and closing a child reactivates the parent's focus trap; the APG example keeps a module-level open-dialog list (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js, OpenDialogList).
- Given a dialog that is constructed, then the APG example blanks every `input` value inside it, so a conformance suite MUST decide deliberately whether opening a dialog resets its form state (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js, clearDialog).

### Dismissal and layering

- Given a modal dialog, then windows under it MUST be inert: users MUST NOT be able to interact with content outside the active dialog (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given inert content outside an active dialog, then it is typically visually obscured or dimmed, and in some implementations an attempt to interact with it closes the dialog (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, about this pattern) MAY.
- Given multiple stacked modal dialogs, then only the topmost dialog's focus trap MUST be active; opening a new dialog MUST deactivate the parent's trap, and closing it MUST reactivate the parent's (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js, OpenDialogList stack with addListeners and removeListeners).
- Given Escape with several dialogs open, then only the topmost dialog MUST close (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js, closeCurrentDialog on the top of the stack).
- Given a dialog opens, then body scrolling MUST be suppressed, and it MUST only be restored when no dialogs remain open (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js, which adds a class to `document.body` and removes it only when the open list is empty).
- Given a dialog that replaces another in place, then the replacement MUST inherit the outgoing dialog's focus-restoration target unless a new one is given (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js, replace).
- Given the APG dialog implementation, then backdrop click does NOT dismiss: the example wires no click handler on the backdrop element (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js).
- Given a dialog-popup combobox such as the APG date picker, then clicking outside both the toggle button and the dialog MUST close the dialog, and in that case focus MUST NOT be moved back to the toggle button (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/datepicker-dialog.js, handleBackgroundMouseUp calling `close(false)`).
- Given `showModal()`, then it MUST throw if the element is not connected, not fully active, or already showing as a popover; MUST set the dialog's "is modal" to true; MUST set the node document to be blocked by that modal dialog; and MUST add the element to the top layer (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4 The dialog element, show a modal dialog) MUST.
