- Given a document blocked by a modal dialog, then every node connected to that document MUST become inert except the dialog element and its flat tree descendants; a document is blocked when the dialog is the topmost `dialog` element in the document's top layer (WHATWG HTML: §6.3.1 Modal dialogs and inert subtrees) MUST.
- Given an inert node, then hit-testing MUST act as if `pointer-events: none`, text selection MUST act as if `user-select: none`, an editable node MUST behave as non-editable, and the user agent SHOULD ignore it for find-in-page; inert nodes are not exposed to accessibility APIs (WHATWG HTML: §6.3 Inert subtrees) MUST for the first three, SHOULD for find-in-page.
- Given an inert element, then it MUST NOT be a focusable area, so it cannot be focused by click, by Tab, or by `.focus()`; the focusing steps also return early when the new target's DOM anchor is inert (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area; §6.6.4 focusing steps) MUST.
- Given the `inert` attribute, then it makes the element and all flat-tree descendants that do not otherwise escape inertness inert; a modal dialog escapes its ancestors' inertness but can still be made inert by putting `inert` on the dialog itself, which is the "expressly inert" case (WHATWG HTML: §6.3.2 The inert attribute).
- Given individual form controls, then `inert` SHOULD NOT be used on them; `disabled` is the right attribute (WHATWG HTML: §6.3.2, authoring guidance).
- Given `show()`, then it MUST return early if the dialog is already open and non-modal, MUST throw `InvalidStateError` if it is open and modal, MUST fire a cancelable `beforetoggle` with `oldState` "closed" and `newState` "open" and abort if that is cancelled, MUST queue a `toggle` event task, MUST add the `open` attribute, MUST record the previously focused element, MUST hide popovers up to the topmost popover ancestor, and MUST then run the dialog focusing steps (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, the show() method steps) MUST.
- Given the dialog focusing steps, then the focus target MUST be resolved in this priority order: the dialog itself if it carries `autofocus`, otherwise the dialog's focus delegate, otherwise the dialog element itself (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, dialog focusing steps) MUST.
- Given `close(returnValue)`, then it MUST fire `beforetoggle` with `oldState` "open" and `newState` "closed", remove the `open` attribute, request removal from the top layer when the dialog is modal, set `returnValue`, run the focusing steps for the previously focused element WITHOUT scrolling the viewport, and then queue a `close` event on the user interaction task source (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, close the dialog algorithm) MUST.
- Given `requestClose(returnValue)`, then it MUST first fire a cancelable `cancel` event, and only if that is not prevented MUST it close the dialog exactly as `close()` does, including firing `close`; it MUST ignore the `closedby` attribute entirely, so even `closedby="none"` still closes (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, requestClose) MUST.
- Given the `closedby` attribute, then its states are `any`, `closerequest` and `none`, and both the missing and invalid value defaults are Auto, which behaves as Close Request when the dialog was shown with `showModal()` and as None otherwise (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, the closedby attribute).
- Given a dialog whose computed closed-by state is Any, then clicking outside it MUST light-dismiss it: a `pointerdown` and a matching `pointerup` on the same non-dialog target MUST request the topmost dialog's close watcher to close with false (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.5 Dialog light dismiss) MUST.
- Given a `dialog` element, then `tabindex` MUST NOT be specified on it, which is the spec-level reason the APG example warns against making the dialog container focusable (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4) MUST NOT.
- Given a `dialog` without the `open` attribute, then it SHOULD NOT be shown to the user (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, the open attribute).
- Given a button with `commandfor` pointing at a dialog, then only the `close`, `request-close` and `show-modal` command states are valid for that target, and they MUST map to closing, requesting close, and showing a modal dialog respectively (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, is valid command steps and command steps for dialog elements) MUST.
- Given `autofocus` inside a `dialog`, then the element MUST be focused when the dialog becomes shown rather than at page load, because the nearest ancestor autofocus scoping root is the dialog (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.7 The autofocus attribute) MUST.
- Given the popover attribute states, then they are `auto`, which closes other popovers and has light dismiss and responds to close requests, `manual`, which does neither, and `hint`, which closes other hint popovers but not other auto popovers and does have light dismiss; the missing value default is No Popover, the invalid value default is Manual, and the empty value default is Auto (WHATWG HTML: §6.12 The popover attribute).
- Given `showPopover()`, then it MUST check popover validity, fire a cancelable `beforetoggle` with `oldState` "closed" and `newState` "open", re-check validity because handlers may have mutated the DOM, hide sibling popovers in the relevant stack, establish a close watcher, add the element to the top layer, run the popover focusing steps, and queue a `toggle` event (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, show popover algorithm) MUST.
- Given a stack of popovers, then the previously focused element MUST be recorded only for the first, bottom popover in the stack, so focus returns once rather than at every level (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, show popover algorithm, explicit note) MUST.
- Given `hidePopover()`, then it MUST recursively hide descendant hint and auto stack members, fire a cancelable `beforetoggle` with `oldState` "open" and `newState` "closed", remove the element from the top layer, clear the popover trigger, set visibility state to hidden, and queue a `toggle` event (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, hide popover algorithm) MUST.
- Given a popover being hidden, then focus MUST be returned to the previously focused element only when `focusPreviousElement` is true AND the document's currently focused area is a shadow-including inclusive descendant of the popover, and that focusing MUST NOT scroll the viewport (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, hide popover algorithm) MUST.
- Given the popover focusing steps, then they check the `autofocus` attribute descendant and then an autofocus delegate, and unlike the dialog focusing steps they have NO fallback that focuses the popover element itself (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, popover focusing steps compared with §4.11.4, dialog focusing steps).
- Given `togglePopover(options)`, then a `force` of true MUST prevent hiding a showing popover, a `force` of false MUST prevent showing a hidden one, and the return value MUST be whether the final visibility state is showing (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, togglePopover).
- Given a popover in the Auto or Hint state, then clicking outside it MUST light-dismiss it, matched by a `pointerdown` and `pointerup` pair and resolved against the popover stack via the topmost clicked popover algorithm (WHATWG HTML: §6.12.2 Popover light dismiss) MUST.
- Given `beforetoggle`, then it is cancelable and cancelling it MUST abort the show or hide; given `toggle`, then it is queued on the DOM manipulation task source, is deduplicated so a pending task keeps its original `oldState`, and is NOT cancelable (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, ToggleEvent handling) MUST.
- Given a popover invoker, then `get the popover target element` MUST return null when the node is not a button, when it is disabled, or when it is a submit button with a form owner, and a `show` action on an already-showing popover or a `hide` on an already-hidden one MUST be a no-op (WHATWG HTML: §6.12.1 The popover target attributes) MUST.
- Given a shown popover, then its implicit anchor element MUST be set to the invoking source element and MUST be cleared to null on hide; this is what powers CSS anchor positioning and there is no separate JS API for it (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, show popover algorithm) MUST.
- Given a shown popover, then it MUST be added to the top layer; on hide, an auto or hint popover MUST be removed asynchronously so exit animations can run, while a manual popover MUST be removed immediately (WHATWG HTML: §6.12) MUST.
- Given `:popover-open`, then it MUST match any element whose `popover` attribute is not in the No Popover state and whose popover visibility state is showing (WHATWG HTML: §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes) MUST.

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="dialog"`, then it SHOULD have an accessible name from `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`; the rule is tagged best-practice rather than WCAG, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-dialog-name.json).
- Given `role="dialog"` in the axe role table, then it is a window-type role whose role-specific allowed attributes are exactly `aria-expanded` and `aria-modal`, its superclass is `window`, and an accessible name IS required by the role table even though the name rule is best-practice; it has no required owned elements, required context or required attributes (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, dialog).
- Given background content made inert with `aria-hidden="true"` while a modal dialog is open, then that content MUST NOT contain any focusable element; axe reports "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and separately offers `focusable-modal-open`, which reports incomplete with "Check that focusable elements are not tabbable in the current state" when a modal is open (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable).
- Given nested `aria-hidden="true"` wrappers around background content, then only the outermost one is evaluated, because the rule`'`s matcher walks up and returns false if any ancestor is already `aria-hidden="true"` (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given a dialog container given `tabindex="0"`, then `focus-order-semantics` is satisfied only through the scrollable-region branch, because `dialog` is a window-type rather than widget-type role and `dialog: true` appears in the valid-scrollable-semantics map (axe: lib/checks/aria/valid-scrollable-semantics-evaluate.js; lib/checks/aria/has-widget-role-evaluate.js).
- Given a dialog with a scrollable body, then that scroll container MUST contain a focusable element or be focusable itself, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/scrollable-region-focusable.json).
- Given `aria-modal` on any role other than `dialog` or `alertdialog`, then `aria-allowed-attr` fails at critical impact, because those are the only two roles in the axe table whose allowed attributes include it (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js; lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json).
- Given a dialog rendered inside an `iframe` with `tabindex="-1"`, then the frame MUST NOT have `tabindex="-1"` while it contains focusable content; this is WCAG 2.1.1 A at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/frame-focusable-content.json).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a focus trap, then it MAY be implemented without intercepting Tab at all: the APG example places two `tabindex="0"` sentinel elements immediately before and after the dialog and installs a capturing `focus` listener on `document` that redirects focus back inside whenever it escapes (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js, preNode/postNode and trapFocus).
- Given a sentinel-based focus trap, then the wrap direction MUST be inferred: if the first descendant is already the last focused element, focus goes to the last descendant instead, which is how Shift+Tab wrap is produced (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js, trapFocus).
- Given a dialog implementation, then the set of focusable descendants MUST be discovered by walking the DOM and attempting focus rather than by matching a hard-coded selector list; the APG example uses `attemptFocus` with an `isFocusable` predicate and verifies `document.activeElement` afterwards (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js, focusFirstDescendant and attemptFocus).
- Given the APG examples, then modal dialogs are built on a plain `div`, not the native `dialog` element, with a source comment noting that the native element's `::backdrop` pseudo-element works similarly (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/js/dialog.js).
- Given the APG dialog test suite, then there is no automated assertion that focus returns to the invoking element on close; that behavior is documented in prose and in the example implementation only, so a conformance suite MUST add its own test for it (aria-practices: test/tests/dialog-modal_dialog.js, which has no such ariaTest).
- Given the `alertdialog` role, then it is a special-case dialog role for dialogs that divert attention to a brief, important message, and its usage is described in the alert dialog pattern (aria-practices: content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html, about this pattern).

## alert-dialog

### Roles and attributes

- Given a dialog that interrupts the user's workflow to communicate an important message and acquire a response, then the element containing all of its parts including the message and the buttons MUST have `role="alertdialog"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/alertdialog-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given an alert dialog, then the container MUST have `aria-modal="true"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/alertdialog-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given an alert dialog with a visible label, then it MUST have `aria-labelledby` referring to the element containing the title; otherwise it MUST have `aria-label` (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/alertdialog-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given an alert dialog, then it MUST have `aria-describedby` referring to the element containing the alert message; unlike plain `dialog`, this is required rather than optional (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/alertdialog-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given `role="alertdialog"`, then it lets assistive technologies and browsers distinguish alert dialogs from other dialogs so they can give them special treatment, such as playing a system alert sound (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/alertdialog-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given an alert dialog, then the same "only when both" constraint on `aria-modal` applies as for `dialog`: application code MUST prevent all interaction outside it and visual styling MUST obscure the outside content (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/alertdialog-pattern.html, note).
- Given a legacy alert dialog using `aria-hidden` for the inert layer, then the same two caveats as for `dialog` apply (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/alertdialog-pattern.html, note).
- Given the APG alert dialog example, then `role="alertdialog"` sits on a `div` and carries `aria-modal="true"`, a resolvable `aria-labelledby` and a resolvable `aria-describedby` (aria-practices: test/tests/alertdialog_alertdialog.js, alertdialog-role, aria-modal, aria-labelledby and aria-describedby assertions).
- Given an alert dialog container, then it MUST NOT carry `tabindex`; the APG example markup has no `tabindex` anywhere inside the alert dialog (aria-practices: test/tests/alertdialog_alertdialog.js markup notes; content/patterns/alertdialog/examples/alertdialog.html).

### Keyboard

- Given an alert dialog, then it MUST implement the full modal dialog keyboard interaction: Tab and Shift+Tab cycle within the dialog and Escape closes it (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/alertdialog-pattern.html, keyboard interaction, which defers to the modal dialog pattern).
- Given an alert dialog with a No and a Yes button, when Tab is pressed from the last button, then focus MUST wrap to the first button (aria-practices: test/tests/alertdialog_alertdialog.js, key-tab assertion).
- Given an open alert dialog, when Escape is pressed, then it MUST close (aria-practices: test/tests/alertdialog_alertdialog.js, key-escape assertion).
- Given a widget with an alert dialog, then a keyboard shortcut MAY perform the same action as the primary button; the APG example wires Ctrl+S on Windows and Command+S on macOS to save (aria-practices: test/tests/alertdialog_alertdialog.js, key-control-s assertion; content/patterns/alertdialog/examples/js/alertdialog.js keydownHandler).

### Pointer

- Given an alert dialog, then backdrop click dismissal is NOT implemented: the APG example wires no click handler on the backdrop, so a conformance suite MUST decide deliberately whether outside-click dismissal is appropriate for a dialog that demands a response (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/examples/js/alertdialog.js).

### Focus management

- Given an alert dialog that confirms a destructive action, then initial focus MUST land on the least destructive button; the APG example focuses the "No" button, not "Yes" (aria-practices: test/tests/alertdialog_alertdialog.js, key-tab assertion; content/patterns/dialog-modal/dialog-modal-pattern.html note on focus placement).
- Given the APG alert dialog, then on close focus returns to the text area the action applies to, not to the button that opened the dialog, because the invoker is passed explicitly as the restore target (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/examples/js/alertdialog.js, openAlertDialog passing `target` as focusAfterClosed).
- Given a trigger button whose action is currently unavailable, then it MUST use `aria-disabled="true"` rather than the native `disabled` attribute so it stays in the Tab order and remains discoverable, and the open handler MUST refuse to open when `aria-disabled` is `"true"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/examples/js/alertdialog.js, openAlertDialog guard; content/patterns/alertdialog/examples/alertdialog.html accessibility features).

### Dismissal and layering

- Given an alert dialog, then only one MAY be open at a time; the APG example tracks a single open dialog rather than a stack, and unconditionally restores body scrolling on close (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/examples/js/alertdialog.js, `aria.openedDialog`).
- Given an open alert dialog, then Escape handling MUST close the single open dialog directly (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/examples/js/alertdialog.js, keyup listener).
- Given an alert dialog, then no `aria-hidden` is toggled on sibling content; modality is conveyed by `aria-modal="true"` plus the focus trap alone (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/examples/js/alertdialog.js).
- Given an alert dialog built on the native `dialog` element, then every rule of the dialog element applies unchanged: `showModal()` blocks the document with a modal dialog and makes everything else inert, `close()` restores focus to the previously focused element without scrolling and then fires `close`, and `requestClose()` fires a cancelable `cancel` first (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4 The dialog element; §6.3.1 Modal dialogs and inert subtrees) MUST.
- Given an alert dialog that MUST NOT be dismissible by clicking outside, then `closedby="none"` suppresses light dismiss, but `requestClose()` ignores `closedby` entirely and still closes, so a headless alert dialog MUST NOT rely on `closedby` to make the dialog unclosable (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, the closedby attribute and requestClose) MUST NOT.
- Given an alert dialog shown with `showModal()`, then its computed closed-by state defaults to Close Request, so Escape closes it via the close watcher, firing a cancelable `cancel` then `close` (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, computed closed-by state and canceling dialogs) MUST.
- Given `autofocus` on the least destructive button inside an alert dialog, then that element MUST receive focus when the dialog is shown, because the dialog focusing steps check `autofocus` before the focus delegate (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, dialog focusing steps; §6.6.7 The autofocus attribute) MUST.

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="alertdialog"`, then it SHOULD have an accessible name from `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`; the same best-practice rule covers both dialog roles, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-dialog-name.json, selector `[role="dialog"], [role="alertdialog"]`).
- Given `role="alertdialog"` in the axe role table, then it is a window-type role whose superclasses are `alert` and `dialog`, whose role-specific allowed attributes are exactly `aria-expanded` and `aria-modal`, and for which an accessible name is required (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, alertdialog).
- Given `aria-describedby` on an alert dialog, then the referenced id MUST resolve; an unresolvable id reference is reported as incomplete, "ARIA attribute element ID does not exist on the page", rather than as a pass (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, incomplete.noId).
- Given an alert dialog whose confirm and cancel buttons use `aria-disabled="true"` to stay focusable, then `aria-hidden-focus` is not triggered, because `aria-disabled` neither hides the element nor removes it from the focus order; only `aria-hidden` and the native `disabled` attribute affect axe`'`s focusability model (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json; lib/commons/dom/is-focusable.js).
- Given the trigger button of an alert dialog, then `button-name` applies at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/button-name.json).
- Given an alert dialog container given `tabindex="0"`, then `focus-order-semantics` passes through the scrollable-region branch, since `alertdialog: true` is in the valid-scrollable-semantics map (axe: lib/checks/aria/valid-scrollable-semantics-evaluate.js).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given an alert dialog, then its focus trap uses the same sentinel-node and capturing-`focus`-listener mechanism as the modal dialog, with the stack support removed (aria-practices: content/patterns/alertdialog/examples/js/alertdialog.js).
- Given the APG alert dialog example, then a separate `role="alert"` live region exists next to the control it describes, outside the alert dialog, for non-interrupting status such as "Saved successfully"; `alertdialog` and `alert` are distinct components in the same widget (aria-practices: test/tests/alertdialog_alertdialog.js, alert-role assertion).
- Given an alert dialog example, then the APG test suite has no Shift+Tab assertion even though the documented keyboard support table includes Shift+Tab wrap, so a conformance suite MUST add it (aria-practices: test/tests/alertdialog_alertdialog.js).

## alert

### Roles and attributes

- Given an element that displays a brief, important message, then it MUST have `role="alert"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/alert/alert-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given the APG alert example, then exactly one `role="alert"` element exists and it is a `div` (aria-practices: test/tests/alert_alert.js, alert-role assertion).

### Keyboard

- Given an alert, then there is no keyboard interaction: the APG keyboard section reads "Not applicable" (aria-practices: content/patterns/alert/alert-pattern.html, keyboard interaction).

### Pointer

- Given an alert, then there is no pointer interaction defined; the element is a live region, not a control (aria-practices: content/patterns/alert/alert-pattern.html, keyboard interaction "Not applicable" and the absence of any pointer rules).

### Focus management

- Given an alert is rendered, then it MUST NOT affect keyboard focus, because an alert must not interrupt the user's ability to continue working; use the alert dialog pattern when interrupting the workflow is necessary (aria-practices: content/patterns/alert/alert-pattern.html, about this pattern, marked crucial).

### State

- Given an alert that must be announced, then it MUST be dynamically rendered into the document after page load; screen readers do not inform users of alerts present before page load completes (aria-practices: content/patterns/alert/alert-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given the APG alert example, then triggering the alert injects a fresh `role="alert"` element rather than unhiding an existing one, which is why a conformance test can only assert visibility of the selector at each point in time and not node identity (aria-practices: test/tests/alert_alert.js, alert-role assertion; content/patterns/alert/examples/js/alert.js addAlert).

### Dismissal and layering

- Given an alert, then it MUST NOT disappear automatically, because an alert that vanishes too quickly can fail WCAG 2.0 success criterion 2.2.3 (aria-practices: content/patterns/alert/alert-pattern.html, about this pattern).

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="alert"`, then axe defines NO accessible-name rule for it: there is no `aria-alert-name` rule, and in the role table `accessibleNameRequired` is not set (axe: lib/rules contains no alert-name rule; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, alert).
- Given `role="alert"` in the axe role table, then it is a structure-type role whose only role-specific allowed attribute is `aria-expanded` and whose superclass is `section`; it has no required owned elements, required context or required attributes (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, alert).
- Given a `role="alert"` container given `tabindex="0"`, then `focus-order-semantics` is satisfied through the scrollable-region branch, because `alert: true` appears in the valid-scrollable-semantics map, not through the widget-role branch (axe: lib/checks/aria/valid-scrollable-semantics-evaluate.js).
- Given a `role="alert"` element carrying an `aria-*` attribute other than `aria-expanded` or a global attribute, then `aria-allowed-attr` fails at critical impact (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js alert; lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json).
- Given an alert used as the announcement target of `aria-errormessage`, then it MUST be visible and MUST use a supported announcement technique; `role="alert"` is one of the techniques axe names explicitly (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-errormessage pass message and fail.hidden).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given repeated alerts, then interruption frequency MUST be kept low, because frequent interruptions inhibit usability for people with visual and cognitive disabilities and make WCAG 2.0 success criterion 2.2.4 harder to meet (aria-practices: content/patterns/alert/alert-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a dynamically rendered alert, then most screen readers announce it automatically and some operating systems may play an alert sound (aria-practices: content/patterns/alert/alert-pattern.html, about this pattern).

## tooltip

### Roles and attributes

- Given the element serving as the tooltip container, then it MUST have `role="tooltip"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/tooltip/tooltip-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given the element that triggers a tooltip, then it MUST reference the tooltip element with `aria-describedby` (aria-practices: content/patterns/tooltip/tooltip-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given the tooltip pattern, then it is explicitly marked work in progress without task force consensus, tracked in APG issue 128, so the library SHOULD treat these rules as provisional (aria-practices: content/patterns/tooltip/tooltip-pattern.html, about this pattern, marked NOTE).

### Keyboard

- Given a visible tooltip, when Escape is pressed, then the tooltip MUST be dismissed (aria-practices: content/patterns/tooltip/tooltip-pattern.html, keyboard interaction).

### Pointer

- Given a tooltip invoked by a pointing cursor moving over the trigger, then it MUST remain open as long as the cursor is over either the trigger or the tooltip itself (aria-practices: content/patterns/tooltip/tooltip-pattern.html, note).
- Given a tooltip, then it typically appears after a small delay and disappears on mouse out (aria-practices: content/patterns/tooltip/tooltip-pattern.html, about this pattern).

### Focus management

- Given a tooltip widget, then it MUST NOT receive focus (aria-practices: content/patterns/tooltip/tooltip-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a visible tooltip, then focus MUST stay on the triggering element (aria-practices: content/patterns/tooltip/tooltip-pattern.html, note).
- Given a tooltip invoked when the trigger receives focus, then it MUST be dismissed when the trigger no longer has focus, on blur (aria-practices: content/patterns/tooltip/tooltip-pattern.html, note).
- Given a hover-like surface that must contain focusable elements, then it MUST NOT be a tooltip; use a non-modal dialog instead (aria-practices: content/patterns/tooltip/tooltip-pattern.html, about this pattern).

### State

- Given a tooltip, then it has no state beyond visible or hidden: the pattern defines no `aria-expanded` on the trigger and no selection or checked state (aria-practices: content/patterns/tooltip/tooltip-pattern.html, roles/states/properties, which lists only `role="tooltip"` and `aria-describedby`).

### Dismissal and layering

- Given a tooltip implemented with the `popover` attribute, then `popover="hint"` is the state designed for it: it closes other hint popovers when opened but does NOT close open auto popovers, and it has light dismiss and responds to close requests (WHATWG HTML: §6.12 The popover attribute, hint state).
- Given a hint popover being shown while nested under another hint ancestor, then an Auto popover may be downgraded to the Hint effective type, which changes which stack is closed (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, show popover algorithm, effectiveType).
- Given a tooltip popover, then it MUST NOT be focused on show: the popover focusing steps check `autofocus` and an autofocus delegate only, with no fallback to the popover element itself, which matches the APG rule that tooltips do not receive focus (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, popover focusing steps).
- Given a tooltip popover being hidden, then focus MUST be restored to the previously focused element only when the currently focused area is inside the popover, which for a tooltip is normally never, so the trigger keeps focus throughout (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, hide popover algorithm) MUST.
- Given a tooltip popover, then `beforetoggle` is cancelable and `toggle` is not, so a "tooltip about to show" hook can suppress the show while a "tooltip shown" hook cannot (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, ToggleEvent handling).

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="tooltip"`, then it MUST have an accessible name from visible text, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`; this is WCAG 4.1.2 A at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-tooltip-name.json).
- Given `role="tooltip"` in the axe role table, then it is a structure-type role whose only role-specific allowed attribute is `aria-expanded`, its superclass is `section`, the name may come from content, and `accessibleNameRequired` is NOT set in the table even though the name rule is tagged `wcag2a` (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, tooltip).
- Given a `role="tooltip"` element given `tabindex="0"`, then `focus-order-semantics` is satisfied because `tooltip` is the single entry in that rule`'`s `valid-scrollable-semantics` `roles` option, not because it is a widget role; a tooltip is therefore the one non-widget role the rule explicitly allows in the focus order (axe: lib/rules/focus-order-semantics.json, check valid-scrollable-semantics with `options.roles: ["tooltip"]`).
- Given a tooltip hidden with `aria-hidden="true"` that contains a focusable element, then that content MUST be made non-focusable or removed, at serious impact; this is the axe-side reason the APG forbids focusable content inside a tooltip (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json).
- Given a trigger that references its tooltip with `aria-describedby`, then that id MUST resolve and MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact for the uniqueness rule (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json; lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json).
- Given a control labelled only by a `title` attribute, then it SHOULD have a visible label instead; a tooltip-as-only-label is a best-practice failure at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/label-title-only.json, check title-only).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given the APG repository at this commit, then the tooltip pattern ships no example and no test file; work on an example is tracked by APG issue 127, so there are no test-derived assertions for tooltip (aria-practices: content/patterns/tooltip/tooltip-pattern.html, example section; content/patterns/tooltip contains only the pattern page).
- Given a toolbar-style hover label, then the APG toolbar example shows a workable pattern: show the label immediately on `mouseover` and on `focus`, hide it immediately on `blur`, hide it after an 800 ms delay on `mouseleave` while re-checking whether hover was regained, and hide every open label on any document-level Escape (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FormatToolbarItem.js, showPopupLabel/hidePopupLabel/handleHideAllPopupLabels).

## menu

### Roles and attributes

- Given the element that opens a menu, then it MUST have `role="button"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/menu-button-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a menu button, then it MUST have `aria-haspopup` set to `menu` or `true` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/menu-button-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a displayed menu, then its button MUST have `aria-expanded="true"`; when the menu is hidden, `aria-expanded="false"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/menu-button-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given the element containing the menu items, then it MUST have `role="menu"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/menu-button-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a menu button, then it MAY have `aria-controls` referring to the element with `role="menu"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/menu-button-pattern.html, roles/states/properties, marked optional).
- Given a `role="menu"` element, then it MUST have either `aria-labelledby` referring to the menuitem or button that controls its display, or a label from `aria-label` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a horizontally oriented menu, then it MUST have `aria-orientation="horizontal"`; the default for `menu` is `vertical` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given menu items, then each MUST be a child element of the containing `menu` and MUST have one of the roles `menuitem`, `menuitemcheckbox` or `menuitemradio` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a checked `menuitemcheckbox` or `menuitemradio`, then `aria-checked` MUST be `"true"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a disabled menu item, then `aria-disabled` MUST be `"true"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given groups of menu items, then they MAY be divided with an element of `role="separator"`, and this technique SHOULD be used when a menu contains a set of `menuitemradio` items (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a separator, then it SHOULD have `aria-orientation` consistent with its visual orientation (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given focus management inside a menu, then exactly one of two approaches MUST be used: the menu container has `tabindex` of `-1` or `0` plus `aria-activedescendant` pointing at the focused item, OR each item has `tabindex="-1"` with roving tabindex, except that in a menubar the first item has `tabindex="0"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given `ul` and `li` markup reused for a menu of links, then the `li` wrappers MUST carry `role="none"` and the `role="menuitem"` MUST sit on the `a` element (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_links.js, none-role and menuitem-role assertions).
- Given `role="menuitemcheckbox"` or `role="menuitemradio"`, then all descendants are forced presentational, so a visual checkmark glyph MUST be marked `aria-hidden="true"` (aria-practices: content/practices/hiding-semantics/hiding-semantics-practice.html, roles that require presentational children; test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js menuitemcheckbox-aria-hidden and menuitemradio-aria-hidden assertions).
- Given a decorative expand-indicator glyph on a parent menuitem, then it MUST carry `aria-hidden="true"` (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js, menubar-menuitem-aria-hidden assertion).
- Given the `aria-activedescendant` menu-button variant, then the `menu` element MUST carry `tabindex="-1"` and MUST already carry `aria-activedescendant` pointing at the first item before the menu is ever opened (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions-active-descendant.js, menu-tabindex and menu-aria-activedescendant assertions).

### Keyboard

- Given focus on a menu button, when Enter is pressed, then the menu MUST open and focus MUST move to the first menu item (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/menu-button-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a menu button, when Space is pressed, then the menu MUST open and focus MUST move to the first menu item (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/menu-button-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a menu button, when Down Arrow is pressed, then the menu MAY open with focus on the first menu item (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/menu-button-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given focus on a menu button, when Up Arrow is pressed, then the menu MAY open with focus on the last menu item (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/menu-button-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given a menu that opens, then authors MUST ensure focus moves to an item inside it, because Tab and Shift+Tab do not move focus into a menu (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard interaction preamble).
- Given focus on a menuitem in a menu, when Tab or Shift+Tab is pressed, then focus MUST move out of the menu and all menus and submenus MUST close (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a menuitem with a submenu, when Enter is pressed, then the submenu MUST open with focus on its first item (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a leaf menuitem, when Enter is pressed, then the item MUST activate and the menu MUST close (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a `menuitemcheckbox`, when Space is pressed, then its state MAY change without closing the menu (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given focus on an unchecked `menuitemradio`, when Space is pressed, then that item MAY become checked and any other checked `menuitemradio` in the same group MUST be unchecked, without closing the menu (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given focus on a menuitem with a submenu, when Space is pressed, then the submenu MAY open with focus on its first item (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given focus on a leaf menuitem, when Space is pressed, then the item MAY activate and the menu close (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given focus in a menu, when Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the next item, optionally wrapping from last to first (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a menu, when Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the previous item, optionally wrapping from first to last (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a menu on a menuitem with a submenu, when Right Arrow is pressed, then the submenu MUST open with focus on its first item (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a submenu of an item in a menu, when Left Arrow is pressed, then the submenu MUST close and focus MUST return to the parent menuitem (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a menu opened from a menu button rather than a menubar, when Right Arrow is pressed on an item with no submenu, then nothing MUST happen (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table, Right Arrow note).
- Given arrow-key wrapping is not supported, when Home is pressed, then focus MUST move to the first item in the current menu; when End is pressed, to the last (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a menu, when a printable character is typed, then focus MAY move to the next item in that menu whose label begins with that character (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given focus in a menu, when Escape is pressed, then the menu containing focus MUST close and focus MUST return to the element or context from which it was opened, such as the menu button or the parent menuitem (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a closed menu button, when Enter, Space or Down Arrow is pressed, then the menu MUST be displayed and focus MUST be on menu item index 0 (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions.js, menu-button-key-open assertion).
- Given a closed menu button, when Up Arrow is pressed, then the menu MUST be displayed and focus MUST be on the last menu item (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions.js, menu-button-key-up-arrow assertion).
- Given an open menu, when Enter is pressed on any item, then that item's action MUST be performed, the menu MUST close, and focus MUST return to the menu button (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions.js, menu-key-enter assertion, verified for every item).
- Given an open menu, when Escape is pressed on any item, then no action MUST be performed, the menu MUST close, and focus MUST return to the menu button (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions.js, menu-key-escape assertion, verified for every item).
- Given focus on the last item of an open menu, when Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST wrap to the first item (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions.js, menu-key-down-arrow assertion).
- Given focus on the first item of an open menu, when Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST wrap to the last item (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions.js, menu-key-up-arrow assertion).
- Given focus on any item of an open menu, when Home is pressed, then focus MUST move to the first item; when End is pressed, to the last (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions.js, menu-key-home and menu-key-end assertions, verified from every position).
- Given focus on a menu item, when a character with no matching item is typed, then focus MUST stay put (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions.js, menu-key-character assertion, 'x' at index 0 stays at 0).
- Given focus on a menu item, when a character matching a later item is typed, then focus MUST move to the next item whose label starts with that character, searching forward from the item after the current one and wrapping to the start (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions.js and menu-button_links.js, menu-key-character assertions; content/patterns/menu-button/examples/js/menu-button-actions.js setFocusByFirstCharacter).
- Given a menu typeahead search that finds no match after the current item, then the search MUST wrap and continue from the first item (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_links.js, menu-character assertion, where 'w' at the last match wraps to index 0).
- Given menu typeahead in the APG menu-button examples, then it is single-character only: there is no multi-character buffer and no reset timer, unlike the listbox and combobox implementations (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/examples/js/menu-button-actions.js, setFocusByFirstCharacter).
- Given focus in a menu, when Shift+Tab is pressed, then the menu MUST close and focus MUST move to the button (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/examples/js/menu-button-actions.js, onMenuitemKeydown shift branch).
- Given focus in a menu, when Tab is pressed, then the menu MUST close and the default Tab behavior MUST be allowed to proceed, so the handler MUST NOT call `preventDefault` for Tab (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/examples/js/menu-button-actions.js, onMenuitemKeydown Tab case, which does not set the handled flag).
- Given an `aria-activedescendant` menu, when Enter, Space or Down Arrow opens it from the button, then `aria-activedescendant` MUST point at the first item and DOM focus MUST be on the `menu` element (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions-active-descendant.js, button-down-arrow-or-space-or-enter assertion).
- Given an `aria-activedescendant` menu, when Up Arrow opens it from the button, then `aria-activedescendant` MUST point at the last item (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions-active-descendant.js, button-up-arrow assertion).
- Given an `aria-activedescendant` menu, when Enter is pressed, then the action for the item named by `aria-activedescendant` MUST be performed, the menu MUST close, and focus MUST return to the button (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions-active-descendant.js, menu-enter assertion).
- Given a menu closing in the `aria-activedescendant` variant, then `aria-activedescendant` MUST be set to the empty string rather than removed (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/examples/js/menu-button-actions-active-descendant.js, closePopup).
- Given a menu of links, when Space is pressed on a link menuitem, then the APG example navigates immediately and does NOT call `preventDefault`, so the browser's default Space behavior is not suppressed; this is a known rough edge rather than a rule to copy (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/examples/js/menu-button-links.js, onMenuitemKeydown Space case).
- Given a menu of links, then Enter activation relies on the native anchor behavior of the focused `a` element rather than on a scripted handler, and the corresponding APG test is disabled as flaky under issue 1937 (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_links.js, commented-out menu-enter test; content/patterns/menu-button/examples/js/menu-button-links.js).

### Pointer

- Given an open menu, when the pointer moves over a menu item, then that item MUST become the focused item immediately with no hover delay; the roving-tabindex variant calls `focus()` and the `aria-activedescendant` variant updates `aria-activedescendant` without moving DOM focus (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/examples/js/menu-button-actions.js and menu-button-actions-active-descendant.js, onMenuitemMouseover).
- Given an open menu, when the pointer presses down outside the widget, then the menu MUST close and focus MUST return to the button; the APG example listens for `mousedown` on `window` in the capture phase (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/examples/js/menu-button-actions.js, onBackgroundMousedown).
- Given an open menu, when a menu item is clicked, then the menu MUST close, focus MUST return to the button, and the item's action MUST be performed (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/examples/js/menu-button-actions.js, onMenuitemClick).
- Given a closed menu button, when it is clicked, then the menu MUST open with focus on the first item; when clicked again while open, the menu MUST close and focus MUST return to the button (aria-practices: content/patterns/menu-button/examples/js/menu-button-actions.js, onButtonClick).
- Given an element with an activation behavior, then activation is always caused by a `click` event, and when the user triggers it by any other means such as keyboard or voice, the default action of that interaction event MUST be to fire a `click` event at the element; the user agent SHOULD allow such non-pointer triggering (WHATWG HTML: §6.5 Activation behavior of elements) MUST for the click-firing, SHOULD for the allowance.
- Given a headless control that synthesizes activation, then the HTML spec defines no Enter or Space key binding for buttons at all: key activation is a platform convention the user agent supplies for native elements and which the library MUST therefore implement itself (WHATWG HTML: §6.5, which defers key triggering to the UA; §4.16.3 gives the Space-on-button `:active` example as non-normative prose).
- Given `element.click()`, then it MUST return early if the element is a disabled form control, MUST return early if a click is already in progress on that element, and MUST otherwise fire a synthetic pointer event named `click` with the not-trusted flag set, so `isTrusted` is false and the method is reentrancy-guarded per element (WHATWG HTML: §6.5, the click() steps) MUST.

### Focus management

- Given a `menu` or `menubar`, then it is a composite widget, so Tab and Shift+Tab MUST NOT move focus among its items (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard interaction preamble).
- Given a menu that opens, then keyboard focus MUST be placed on the first item, unless the widget deliberately opens on the last item or on the checked item (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard interaction preamble).
- Given disabled menu items, then they MUST remain focusable but MUST NOT be activatable (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, note).
- Given a separator in a menu, then it MUST NOT be focusable or interactive (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, note).
- Given a menu opened from a context action such as a shortcut key in an editor, then Escape or Enter MAY return focus to that invoking context rather than to a menu button (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, note).
- Given `aria-owns` on a menu container that pulls in elements which are not DOM children, then those elements appear in the reading order after any DOM children, in reference order, and the focus-management script MUST make the visual focus order match that reading order (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, note).
- Given a menu whose items are selected radios, then opening the menu MAY place focus on the currently checked item rather than the first item; the APG toolbar font menu does exactly this (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-menubutton-enter-or-space-or-down-or-up assertion; content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FontMenuButton.js setFocusToCheckedItem).
- Given an element with a negative `tabindex`, then the user agent MUST consider it a focusable area but SHOULD omit it from tabindex-ordered focus navigation, so `tabindex="-1"` is programmatically and click focusable but not Tab-reachable; this is the spec basis for roving tabindex (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3 The tabindex attribute) MUST for focusability, SHOULD for the omission.
- Given `tabindex="0"`, then the element MUST be included in the sequential focus navigation order at its DOM position (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3) MUST.
- Given positive `tabindex` values, then all of them come before every element with an omitted, invalid, zero or negative value, ordered ascending by value with ties broken by tree order; a headless library MUST NOT emit positive values (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3, processing model) MUST NOT.
- Given the `tabIndex` IDL attribute with no content attribute present, then it MUST default to 0 for `a`, `area`, `button`, `frame`, `iframe`, `input`, `object`, `select`, `textarea`, SVG and MathML `a`, and a `summary` that is its parent `details`'s summary, and to -1 otherwise (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3) MUST.
- Given an element to be a focusable area, then ALL of these MUST hold: its tabindex value is non-null or the user agent determines it focusable; it is either not a shadow host or has a shadow root whose `delegatesFocus` is false; it is not actually disabled; it is not inert; and it is being rendered, delegating rendering to its children, or used as canvas fallback content (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
- Given an element can be focusable without being either sequentially focusable or click focusable, then a headless library MUST treat "focusable", "Tab-reachable" and "click-focusable" as three separate properties rather than one (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2, explicit clarification).
- Given a shadow host whose shadow root has `delegatesFocus: true`, then getting the focusable area MUST return the host's focus delegate: the first descendant with `autofocus`, otherwise the first focusable area in tree order, recursing into further delegating hosts, otherwise null (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.4 Focus, get the focusable area and focus delegate) MUST.
- Given the focusing steps, then they MUST return early when the resolved target's DOM anchor is inert, MUST no-op when the target is already the currently focused area, and MUST otherwise run the focus update steps, firing `blur` on the old chain and `focus` on the new chain with `relatedTarget` set (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.4, focusing steps and focus update steps) MUST.
- Given `element.focus(options)`, then `preventScroll` defaults to false, so scrolling into view is the DEFAULT and MUST be suppressed explicitly with `{ preventScroll: true }` (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.6 The focus() method) MUST.
- Given `element.blur()`, then the user agent SHOULD run the unfocusing steps but MAY selectively or uniformly do nothing, so a headless library MUST NOT rely on `blur()` to move focus anywhere in particular (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.6) SHOULD/MAY.
- Given a focus navigation scope, then each Document, shadow host, slot and popover trigger element owns its own tabindex-ordered focus navigation scope, and Tab traversal walks the flattened recursive composition of those scopes, skipping negative-tabindex elements (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.5 Sequential focus navigation; §6.6.2).

### State

- Given a parent menuitem, then `aria-expanded` MUST read `"true"` exactly while its child menu is visible and `"false"` otherwise (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a menu button, then `aria-expanded` MUST read `"true"` while the menu is displayed and `"false"` while it is hidden (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions.js, menu-button-aria-expanded assertion).
- Given `menuitemcheckbox` items, then more than one MAY be checked simultaneously and toggling one MUST NOT affect the others (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js, menuitemcheckbox-aria-checked assertion).
- Given a group of `menuitemradio` items, then exactly one MUST be checked and checking a new one MUST uncheck the previous (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js, menuitemradio-aria-checked assertion; content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-editor.js setRadioButton).

### Dismissal and layering

- Given a menu whose close is requested while the pointer is hovering it or its controlling button, or while it still contains focus, then the close MAY be deferred; the APG toolbar font menu only closes unconditionally when the close is forced (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FontMenu.js, close).
- Given a hover-driven menu, when the pointer leaves the menu or an item, then a delayed close MUST be scheduled and MUST be cancellable if hover or focus returns; the APG toolbar font menu uses 300 ms (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FontMenu.js and FontMenuItem.js, handleMouseout).
- Given a menu closed by Enter, Space, Escape or Tab, then the close MUST be immediate and forced, bypassing the hover and focus guards (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FontMenuItem.js, handleKeydown calling `close(true)`).
- Given a menu implemented with the `popover` attribute, then `popover="auto"` is the correct state: opening one auto popover closes the others, and it has light dismiss and responds to close requests (WHATWG HTML: §6.12 The popover attribute, auto state).
- Given nested submenus as popovers, then the topmost popover ancestor algorithm walks both DOM nesting AND invoker relationships established by `popovertarget`, so a submenu popover triggered from inside a parent popover is treated as its descendant and is not closed when the parent opens (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, topmost popover ancestor).
- Given a click outside an open menu popover, then light dismiss MUST close popovers down to, but not including, the nearest open ancestor popover of the clicked element (WHATWG HTML: §6.12.2 Popover light dismiss, hide popovers until) MUST.
- Given a menu button with `popovertarget` pointing at the menu, then the invoker MUST be a button, MUST NOT be disabled, and MUST NOT be a submit button with a form owner, or the target resolves to null and nothing happens (WHATWG HTML: §6.12.1 The popover target attributes) MUST.
- Given a menu popover opened from an invoker, then the popover's implicit anchor element MUST be set to that invoker, which is what CSS anchor positioning uses to place the menu (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, show popover algorithm) MUST.
- Given a modal dialog opened while a menu popover is open, then `show()` and `showModal()` MUST hide popovers up to the topmost popover ancestor before focusing the dialog, so an open menu does not survive over a modal (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, the show() method steps, step calling hide popovers until) MUST.

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="menuitem"`, then it MUST have an accessible name from visible text, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-command-name.json, selector including `[role="menuitem"]`).
- Given `role="menuitemcheckbox"` or `role="menuitemradio"`, then it MUST have an accessible name and there MUST NOT be a mismatch with a wrapping `label`, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-toggle-field-name.json).
- Given `role="menu"`, then it MUST own at least one of `group`, `menuitemradio`, `menuitem`, `menuitemcheckbox`, `menu` or `separator`; a missing required child fails at critical impact, but `menu` is in the `reviewEmpty` list so an entirely empty menu is reported as incomplete instead (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-children.json with its reviewEmpty option including "menu"; lib/standards/aria-roles.js menu `requiredOwned`, marked a spec difference for including nested `menu` and `separator`).
- Given `role="menuitem"`, `role="menuitemcheckbox"` or `role="menuitemradio"`, then it MUST be contained by `menu`, `menubar` or `group`, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-parent.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js `requiredContext` for all three menu item roles).
- Given `role="menuitemcheckbox"` or `role="menuitemradio"`, then `aria-checked` is a REQUIRED attribute, so omitting it fails at critical impact (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js `requiredAttrs`; lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json).
- Given `role="menuitemcheckbox"` or `role="menuitemradio"`, then it MUST NOT contain a focusable widget-role descendant, because both have `childrenPresentational: true`; plain `role="menuitem"` does NOT have that flag, so `nested-interactive` does not apply to it (axe: lib/rules/nested-interactive.json; lib/rules/nested-interactive-matches.js; lib/standards/aria-roles.js).
- Given `role="menu"` in the axe role table, then it is a composite role whose role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-activedescendant`, `aria-expanded` and `aria-orientation`, its superclass is `select`, and `accessibleNameRequired` is not set (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, menu).
- Given `role="menuitem"` in the axe role table, then its role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-posinset`, `aria-setsize` and `aria-expanded`, its superclass is `command`, a name is required and may come from content (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, menuitem).
- Given `role="menuitemradio"` in the axe role table, then its superclasses are `menuitemcheckbox` and `radio`, and its role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-expanded`, `aria-posinset`, `aria-readonly` and `aria-setsize` (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, menuitemradio).
- Given a menu button that is a native `button`, then `button-name` applies at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/button-name.json).
- Given `aria-controls` on a menu button that also carries `aria-haspopup`, then an unresolvable reference is reported as incomplete with the specific message "Unable to determine if aria-controls referenced ID exists on the page while using aria-haspopup", so lazily rendered menus are surfaced as needing review rather than as failures (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, incomplete.controlsWithinPopup).
- Given a scrollable menu, then it MUST contain a focusable element or be focusable itself, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/scrollable-region-focusable.json).
- Given `li` wrappers marked `role="none"` around `a` elements carrying `role="menuitem"`, then those wrappers MUST NOT carry `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby`, because `none` prohibits both, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js noneRole `prohibitedAttrs`).
- Given a menu implemented with roving tabindex, then no item MUST have a positive `tabindex`, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
