- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a menu item that launches a dialog, then appending an ellipsis to its label is the conventional signal (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, about this pattern) MAY.
- Given a submenu's `menu` element, then it MUST be contained inside the same `menu` element as its parent menuitem and MUST be the sibling immediately following that parent menuitem (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a parent menuitem, then it MUST have `aria-haspopup` set to `menu` or `true`, and `aria-expanded` set to `"false"` when its child menu is hidden and `"true"` when visible (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given the choice between opening a menu from a menu button and from a menubar, then except where explicitly noted, menus opened from a menu button behave identically to menus opened from a menubar (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard interaction preamble).

## menubar

### Roles and attributes

- Given a visually persistent menu, then its container MUST have `role="menubar"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, about this pattern and roles/states/properties).
- Given a menubar with a visible label, then it MUST have `aria-labelledby` referring to that label; otherwise `aria-label` MUST supply the name (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a vertically oriented menubar, then it MUST have `aria-orientation="vertical"`; the default for `menubar` is `horizontal` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a menubar using roving tabindex, then the first item MUST have `tabindex="0"` while every other item in the whole widget has `tabindex="-1"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties; test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js menubar-menuitem-tabindex assertion).
- Given `ul` and `li` markup for a menubar, then every direct `li` child of the menubar and of every `menu` MUST carry `role="none"`, except those that are separators (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js, menubar-role-none and menu-role-none assertions).
- Given a menubar of navigation links, then `role="menuitem"` sits on the `a` elements (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js, menuitem-role assertion, which requires tag `a`).
- Given a menubar of editor commands, then `role="menuitem"` MAY sit on `span` elements for top-level items and on `li` elements for submenu items (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js, menubar-menuitem-role and submenu-menuitem-role assertions).
- Given groups of `menuitemradio` items, then each group MUST be wrapped in an element with `role="group"` carrying `aria-label`, with `role="separator"` elements between groups (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js, group-role, group-aria-label and separator-role assertions).
- Given every submenu `menu` in a menubar, then it MUST carry `tabindex="-1"` and `aria-label` (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js, menu-role and menu-aria-label assertions).
- Given menu items in a menubar that are not disabled, then `aria-disabled` MUST read `"false"` explicitly rather than being absent, in the APG editor example (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js, submenu-menuitem-aria-disabled assertion).

### Keyboard

- Given Tab moving focus into a menubar for the first time, then focus MUST be set on the first `menuitem` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given Tab moving focus into a menubar that has previously contained focus, then focus MAY be set on the `menuitem` that last had focus; otherwise it MUST be set on the first `menuitem` that is not disabled (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a menuitem in a menubar, when Tab or Shift+Tab is pressed, then focus MUST move out of the menubar and all menus and submenus MUST close (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a menubar, when Right Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the next item, optionally wrapping from last to first (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a menubar, when Left Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the previous item, optionally wrapping from first to last (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a menubar menuitem with a submenu, when Down Arrow is pressed, then the submenu MUST open with focus on its first item (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus on a menubar menuitem with a submenu, when Up Arrow is pressed, then the submenu MAY open with focus on its last item (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given focus in a menu opened from a menubar, on an item with no submenu, when Right Arrow is pressed, then the submenu and any parent menus MUST close, focus MUST move to the next item in the menubar, and if that item has a submenu, that submenu SHOULD be opened without moving focus into it, or MAY be opened with focus on its first item (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table, with "Recommended" marking the first option).
- Given focus in a submenu of a menubar item, when Left Arrow is pressed, then that submenu MUST close, focus MUST move to the previous menubar item, and if that item has a submenu it SHOULD be opened without moving focus into it, or MAY be opened with focus on its first item (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a menubar whose items are vertical and whose menus are horizontal, then Down Arrow MUST behave as Right Arrow and vice versa, and Up Arrow MUST behave as Left Arrow and vice versa (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, note).
- Given a navigation menubar item that both performs a function and opens a submenu, then Enter and Space SHOULD perform the navigation while Down Arrow in a horizontal menubar opens the submenu, though the APG advises avoiding this design altogether (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, note).
- Given a top-level menubar item with a submenu, when Enter or Space is pressed, then the submenu MUST be displayed and focus MUST move to its first item (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js menubar-space-or-enter assertion; test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js menubar-key-space-and-enter assertion).
- Given the last top-level menubar item, when Right Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST wrap to the first top-level item (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js menubar-right-arrow assertion; test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js menubar-key-right-arrow assertion).
- Given the first top-level menubar item, when Left Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST wrap to the last top-level item (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js menubar-left-arrow assertion).
- Given a top-level menubar item with a submenu, when Up Arrow is pressed, then the submenu MUST open with focus on its last item (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js menubar-up-arrow assertion; test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js menubar-key-up-arrow assertion).
- Given a top-level menubar item with a submenu, when Down Arrow is pressed, then the submenu MUST open with focus on its first item (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js menubar-down-arrow assertion).
- Given focus anywhere in the menubar, when Home is pressed, then focus MUST move to the first top-level item; when End is pressed, to the last (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js menubar-home and menubar-end assertions).
- Given focus on a top-level menubar item, when a printable character is typed, then focus MUST move to the next top-level item whose label starts with that character, searching cyclically and staying put when nothing matches (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js and menubar_menubar-editor.js, menubar-character and menubar-key-character assertions).
- Given a menubar whose top-level typeahead is scoped to top-level items only, then a character matching a submenu item MUST NOT move focus into a submenu (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js, menubar-character assertion).
- Given focus in an open submenu, when a printable character is typed, then the search MUST be scoped to that submenu or popout only (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js and menubar_menubar-editor.js, submenu-character assertions).
- Given a leaf menuitem in a submenu, when Enter or Space is pressed, then its action MUST be performed; in the navigation menubar all menus close and DOM focus moves to the new content heading (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js, submenu-space-or-enter assertion).
- Given a leaf menuitem in the editor menubar, when Enter is pressed, then the submenu MUST close and focus MUST return to the associated top-level menubar item (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js, submenu-enter assertion).
- Given a `menuitemradio` or `menuitemcheckbox` in the editor menubar, when Space is pressed, then the state MUST change, the submenu MUST stay open, and focus MUST remain on that same item; when Enter is pressed on the same item, the submenu MUST close (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js, submenu-space and submenu-enter assertions).
- Given a leaf `menuitem` in the editor menubar, when Space is pressed, then the submenu MUST close and focus MUST return to the top-level item, i.e. Space behaves like Enter for plain items but not for toggle items (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js, submenu-space assertion).
- Given focus in an open submenu, when Escape is pressed, then the immediate open level MUST close and focus MUST return one level up, to the parent submenu item or the top-level item as appropriate, not all the way to the root (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js submenu-escape assertion; test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js submenu-escape assertion).
- Given focus on a submenu item with no nested popout, when Right Arrow is pressed, then the current menu MUST close, the next top-level menubar item's submenu MUST open, and focus MUST move there, wrapping from the last top-level menu to the first (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js and menubar_menubar-editor.js, submenu-right-arrow assertions).
- Given focus on a submenu item that itself has a popout, when Right Arrow is pressed, then that nested popout MUST open and focus MUST move into it (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js, second submenu-right-arrow assertion).
- Given focus inside a nested popout, when Left Arrow is pressed, then the popout MUST close and focus MUST return to the parent submenu item, whose `aria-expanded` MUST become `"false"` (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js, submenu-left-arrow assertion).
- Given focus on a submenu item, when Left Arrow is pressed and there is no nested popout, then the current menu MUST close, the previous top-level item's submenu MUST open, and focus MUST move there, wrapping from the first to the last (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js and menubar_menubar-editor.js, submenu-left-arrow assertions).
- Given focus inside a submenu, when Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the next item in that same menu, wrapping from last to first; the wrap MUST NOT cross into another menu (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js and menubar_menubar-editor.js, submenu-down-arrow assertions).
- Given focus inside a submenu, when Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the previous item in that same menu, wrapping from first to last (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js and menubar_menubar-editor.js, submenu-up-arrow assertions).
- Given focus inside a submenu, when Home or End is pressed, then focus MUST move to the first or last item of that submenu (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js and menubar_menubar-editor.js, submenu-home and submenu-end assertions).
- Given focus in a menubar, then Page Up MUST behave as Home and Page Down MUST behave as End; both APG menubar examples map them that way (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-editor.js and menubar-navigation.js, onKeydown).
- Given a menubar, when Tab is pressed, then the open popup MUST close and the default Tab behavior MUST proceed, so the handler MUST NOT call `preventDefault` for Tab (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-editor.js, onKeydown Tab case).

### Pointer

- Given a menubar with nothing open, when the pointer moves over a top-level item, then nothing MUST happen: hover-to-open MUST be gated on a popup already being open or the menubar already having focus (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-editor.js onMenuitemPointerover, which checks `isAnyPopupOpen()`; menubar-navigation.js onMenuitemPointerover, which checks `hasFocus()` or an open popup).
- Given a menubar with one submenu already open, when the pointer moves over a different top-level item with a submenu, then the open submenu MUST close and the hovered item's submenu MUST open (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-editor.js, onMenuitemPointerover).
- Given an open menubar, when the pointer presses down outside the whole menubar, then every open popup MUST close; the APG examples listen for `pointerdown` on `window` in the capture phase (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-editor.js and menubar-navigation.js, onBackgroundPointerdown).
- Given a leaf menuitem in a menubar, when it is clicked, then the action MUST be performed and every open menu MUST close; click always closes everything, unlike Escape which closes one level (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-navigation.js, onMenuitemClick).
- Given a parent menuitem in a menubar, when it is clicked, then its submenu MUST toggle open or closed (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-editor.js, onMenuitemClick).
- Given a menubar, then submenu open and close MUST be synchronous with no hover-intent timers; neither APG menubar example uses `setTimeout` (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-editor.js and menubar-navigation.js).
- Given an element with an activation behavior, then activation is always caused by a `click` event, and when the user triggers it by any other means such as keyboard or voice, the default action of that interaction event MUST be to fire a `click` event at the element; the user agent SHOULD allow such non-pointer triggering (WHATWG HTML: §6.5 Activation behavior of elements) MUST for the click-firing, SHOULD for the allowance.
- Given a headless control that synthesizes activation, then the HTML spec defines no Enter or Space key binding for buttons at all: key activation is a platform convention the user agent supplies for native elements and which the library MUST therefore implement itself (WHATWG HTML: §6.5, which defers key triggering to the UA; §4.16.3 gives the Space-on-button `:active` example as non-normative prose).
- Given `element.click()`, then it MUST return early if the element is a disabled form control, MUST return early if a click is already in progress on that element, and MUST otherwise fire a synthetic pointer event named `click` with the not-trusted flag set, so `isTrusted` is false and the method is reentrancy-guarded per element (WHATWG HTML: §6.5, the click() steps) MUST.

### Focus management

- Given a menubar, then it MUST occupy exactly one stop in the page tab sequence and arrow keys MUST move focus among its items (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, keyboard interaction preamble; content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, fundamental keyboard navigation conventions).
- Given a menubar receiving focus, then focus MUST be placed on the first item, which is the "first element" tab-stop convention shared with toolbar and distinct from the "selected element" convention used by radio group, tabs, listbox and tree (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, keyboard navigation inside components).
- Given a menubar using roving tabindex, then moving focus MUST set `tabindex="-1"` on the element that had `tabindex="0"`, set `tabindex="0"` on the new target, and then call `focus()` on it, in that order (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, managing focus within components using a roving tabindex).
- Given a menubar that loses focus, then if the design calls for a specific item to be focused next time, that item's `tabindex` MUST be set to `0` and the previous holder's to `-1` before the composite loses focus (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, managing focus within components using a roving tabindex).
- Given focus moving to a menubar item, then every other open popup that does NOT contain the target MUST be closed, while the target's own ancestor chain stays open (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-editor.js, setFocusToMenuitem and closePopupAll).
- Given a leaf navigation menuitem activated in a navigation menubar, then all menus MUST close and DOM focus MUST move to the newly loaded content heading, which the APG example makes focusable with `tabIndex = -1` first (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-navigation.js, updateContent).
- Given a navigation menubar in its "expanded" mode, then Left Arrow and Right Arrow at the top level MUST also open the newly focused item's popup, so the user flips through top-level menus with their dropdowns staying open (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-navigation.js, onKeydown Left and Right cases gated on an open popup or the expanded data attribute).
- Given Right Arrow pressed on a leaf item inside a popout in the navigation menubar, then every popout MUST be closed before focus advances, not just the immediate level (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-navigation.js, onKeydown Right case calling closePopout).

### State

- Given a menubar, then at most one top-level submenu MUST be open at a time: opening one MUST set its item's `aria-expanded` to `"true"` and set every other top-level item's to `"false"` (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js, menubar-menuitem-aria-expanded assertion, verified exhaustively for every item).
- Given an ancestor chain of open menus, then closing other popups MUST NOT close the ancestors of the newly focused item; the APG example only closes popups that do not contain the target (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-editor.js, closePopupAll with its `doesNotContain` guard).
- Given `menuitemcheckbox` items in a group, then more than one MAY be checked at once, and toggling one MUST NOT change the others (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js, menuitemcheckbox-aria-checked assertion, which checks two independently).
- Given a group of `menuitemradio` items, then exactly one MUST have `aria-checked="true"` and every other in the group MUST read `"false"` (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js, menuitemradio-aria-checked assertion, verified for five groups).
- Given a menu whose plain menuitems act as increment and decrement commands over a radio group, then reaching the smallest or largest option MUST set `aria-disabled="true"` on the corresponding command item (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-editor.js, submenu-menuitem-aria-disabled assertion; content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-editor.js updateFontSizeMenu).
- Given a menu's orientation is unset, then `menubar` MUST default to horizontal and `menu` MUST default to vertical, and the key handler MUST branch on the computed orientation rather than the attribute's presence (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-editor.js, getMenuOrientation).

### Dismissal and layering

- Given a menu whose close is requested while the pointer is hovering it or its controlling button, or while it still contains focus, then the close MAY be deferred; the APG toolbar font menu only closes unconditionally when the close is forced (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FontMenu.js, close).
- Given a hover-driven menu, when the pointer leaves the menu or an item, then a delayed close MUST be scheduled and MUST be cancellable if hover or focus returns; the APG toolbar font menu uses 300 ms (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FontMenu.js and FontMenuItem.js, handleMouseout).
- Given a menu closed by Enter, Space, Escape or Tab, then the close MUST be immediate and forced, bypassing the hover and focus guards (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FontMenuItem.js, handleKeydown calling `close(true)`).
- Given a menu implemented with the `popover` attribute, then `popover="auto"` is the correct state: opening one auto popover closes the others, and it has light dismiss and responds to close requests (WHATWG HTML: §6.12 The popover attribute, auto state).
- Given nested submenus as popovers, then the topmost popover ancestor algorithm walks both DOM nesting AND invoker relationships established by `popovertarget`, so a submenu popover triggered from inside a parent popover is treated as its descendant and is not closed when the parent opens (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, topmost popover ancestor).
- Given a click outside an open menu popover, then light dismiss MUST close popovers down to, but not including, the nearest open ancestor popover of the clicked element (WHATWG HTML: §6.12.2 Popover light dismiss, hide popovers until) MUST.
- Given a menu button with `popovertarget` pointing at the menu, then the invoker MUST be a button, MUST NOT be disabled, and MUST NOT be a submit button with a form owner, or the target resolves to null and nothing happens (WHATWG HTML: §6.12.1 The popover target attributes) MUST.
- Given a menu popover opened from an invoker, then the popover's implicit anchor element MUST be set to that invoker, which is what CSS anchor positioning uses to place the menu (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, show popover algorithm) MUST.
- Given a modal dialog opened while a menu popover is open, then `show()` and `showModal()` MUST hide popovers up to the topmost popover ancestor before focusing the dialog, so an open menu does not survive over a modal (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, the show() method steps, step calling hide popovers until) MUST.

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="menubar"`, then it MUST own at least one of `group`, `menuitemradio`, `menuitem`, `menuitemcheckbox`, `menu` or `separator`, at critical impact, with `menubar` also in the `reviewEmpty` list so an empty menubar is reported as incomplete (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-children.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js menubar `requiredOwned`).
- Given `role="menubar"` in the axe role table, then it is a composite role whose superclass is `menu`, and whose role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-activedescendant`, `aria-expanded` and `aria-orientation` (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, menubar).
- Given a `role="menubar"` whose children include roles outside that required-owned set, then `aria-required-children` fails with "Element has children which are not allowed" (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-children.json, fail.unallowed).
- Given every menu item role in a menubar, then the naming, required-parent, required-attribute and nested-interactive rules listed for the menu component apply identically (axe: lib/rules/aria-command-name.json, aria-toggle-field-name.json, aria-required-parent.json, aria-required-attr.json, nested-interactive.json).
- Given `role="separator"` in a menubar, then `aria-valuenow` is a REQUIRED attribute in the axe role table, which applies to a FOCUSABLE separator; a non-focusable menu separator that omits it will still be flagged by `aria-required-attr` at critical impact, so a conformance suite MUST decide deliberately whether to set it (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js separator `requiredAttrs`; lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json).
- Given `role="group"` in a menubar, then in the axe role table it is a structure-type role whose role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-activedescendant` and `aria-expanded`, with no required owned elements and `accessibleNameRequired` not set (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, group).
- Given a menubar using roving tabindex, then exactly one item may have `tabindex="0"` and no item may have a positive `tabindex`, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json).
- Given a scrollable menubar overflow container, then it MUST contain a focusable element or be focusable itself (axe: lib/rules/scrollable-region-focusable.json).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a menubar, then it is typically horizontal and is used to offer quick access to a consistent set of commands, the web equivalent of a desktop application's window menu bar (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/menu-and-menubar-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a menubar built by discovering its own structure, then the APG examples walk the DOM from the menubar root, deriving a menu id from the role plus the normalized `aria-label`, and collecting per-menu first-character tables plus first and last item references; any item with `aria-haspopup="true"` is registered as a popup owner (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-editor.js, initMenu and getMenuitems).
- Given a navigation menubar, then the APG example distinguishes three container kinds: the menubar itself, a depth-one popup, and a deeper popout, and the Left and Right Arrow behavior differs between popup and popout (aria-practices: content/patterns/menubar/examples/js/menubar-navigation.js, isPopup and isPopout).
- Given a menubar built on links, then the APG example wraps site navigation in landmark structure, a `header` with `role="banner"`, a `nav` with `aria-label`, a `section` with `aria-labelledby` and a `footer` with `role="contentinfo"`, and the tests assert each of those (aria-practices: test/tests/menubar_menubar-navigation.js, banner-role, navigation-role, navigation-aria-label, region-role, region-aria-labelledby and contentinfo-role assertions).

## listbox

### Roles and attributes

- Given an element that contains or owns all the options, then it MUST have `role="listbox"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given each option, then it MUST have `role="option"` and MUST be contained in or owned by either the `listbox` element or a `role="group"` inside the listbox (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a listbox with grouped options, then every option group MUST contain at least one option and MUST have an accessible name from `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a listbox that is not part of another widget such as a combobox, then it MUST have either a visible label referenced by `aria-labelledby` or a value for `aria-label` (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a listbox that allows more than one option to be selected, then it MUST have `aria-multiselectable="true"`; otherwise `aria-multiselectable` is `"false"` or its default false is implied (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a listbox, then selection state MUST be expressed with either `aria-selected` or `aria-checked` on the options, never both; if `aria-selected` is used then `aria-checked` MUST NOT be specified on any option, and vice versa (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a selected option, then it MUST have the chosen selection attribute set to `"true"`; given a selectable but unselected option, that attribute MUST be `"false"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a listbox without `aria-multiselectable="true"`, then no more than one option MUST be selected at a time (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a listbox whose full option set is not in the DOM because of dynamic loading as the user scrolls, then each option MUST carry appropriate `aria-setsize` and `aria-posinset` (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given options arranged horizontally, then the `listbox` MUST have `aria-orientation="horizontal"`; the default for `listbox` is `vertical` (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a design-system convention choice, then using `aria-selected` for single-select widgets and `aria-checked` for multi-select widgets is the recommended convention, absent a reason to do otherwise, and the convention MUST be applied consistently across a site or app (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, note).
- Given a listbox using both `aria-selected` and `aria-checked`, then all of the following MUST hold: the two states differ in meaning and purpose, the interface makes each state's meaning apparent, and the interface provides a separate method for controlling each; the APG strongly recommends avoiding such a design (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, note).
- Given a standalone listbox, then the `listbox` element MUST carry `tabindex="0"` and the options MUST NOT be individual tab stops; focus inside is managed with `aria-activedescendant` (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_scrollable.js, listbox-tabindex and listbox-aria-activedescendant assertions).
- Given a listbox that is the popup of a collapsible button, then the `listbox` MUST carry `tabindex="-1"` and receive focus programmatically when the button opens it (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_collapsible.js, listbox-tabindex assertion).
- Given a single-select listbox using `aria-selected`, then unselected options MUST have the attribute absent rather than set to `"false"`; the APG implementation removes the attribute on defocus (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_scrollable.js option-aria-selected assertion; content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js defocusItem).
- Given a multi-select listbox, then every option MUST carry an explicit `aria-selected="false"` from the start, not an absent attribute (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, option-aria-selected assertion for the multi-select example).
- Given a decorative checkmark glyph inside an option, then it MUST carry `aria-hidden="true"` (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_scrollable.js, listbox_grouped.js and listbox_rearrangeable.js, span-aria-hidden assertions).
- Given a `role="option"` element, then all of its descendants are forced presentational, which is why a listbox cannot present a list of interactive elements; use the grid pattern for that (aria-practices: content/practices/hiding-semantics/hiding-semantics-practice.html, roles that require presentational children; content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given the collapsible-listbox trigger button, then it MUST have `aria-haspopup="listbox"`, a resolvable `aria-labelledby`, and `aria-expanded="true"` while open; the APG example removes `aria-expanded` entirely rather than setting it to `"false"` when closed (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_collapsible.js button-aria-haspopup and button-aria-expanded assertions; content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox-collapsible.js hideListbox).

### Keyboard

- Given a single-select listbox receiving focus with no option selected, then focus MUST be set on the first option, and that option MAY be automatically selected (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a single-select listbox receiving focus with an option already selected, then focus MUST be set on that selected option (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a multi-select listbox receiving focus with no option selected, then focus MUST be set on the first option and the selection state MUST NOT change (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given a multi-select listbox receiving focus with one or more options selected, then focus MUST be set on the first selected option in the list (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a vertical listbox, when Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the next option, and in a single-select listbox selection MAY move with focus (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a vertical listbox, when Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the previous option, and in a single-select listbox selection MAY move with focus (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a listbox, when Home is pressed, then focus MAY move to the first option, and in a single-select listbox selection MAY move with it; supporting Home is strongly recommended for lists of more than five options (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given focus in a listbox, when End is pressed, then focus MAY move to the last option, with the same strong recommendation for lists of more than five options (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given a listbox, then typeahead SHOULD be supported for all listboxes and especially for those with more than seven options (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a listbox, when a single character is typed, then focus MUST move to the next item whose name starts with that character (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a listbox, when multiple characters are typed in rapid succession, then focus MUST move to the next item whose name starts with that whole string (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given options arranged horizontally, then Down Arrow MUST behave as Right Arrow and vice versa, and Up Arrow MUST behave as Left Arrow and vice versa (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, note).
- Given a multi-select listbox, then authors MUST implement one of two selection models: the recommended model, which requires no modifier key while navigating, or the alternative model, in which moving focus without a Shift or Control modifier unselects everything except the focused option (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table, multiple selection).
- Given the recommended multi-select model, when Space is pressed, then the selection state of the focused option MUST toggle (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given the recommended multi-select model, when Shift+Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MAY move to the next option and toggle its selected state (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given the recommended multi-select model, when Shift+Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MAY move to the previous option and toggle its selected state (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when Shift+Space is pressed, then contiguous items from the most recently selected item to the focused item MAY be selected (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when Control+Shift+Home is pressed, then the focused option and all options up to the first MAY be selected, and focus MAY move to the first option (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when Control+Shift+End is pressed, then the focused option and all options down to the last MAY be selected, and focus MAY move to the last option (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when Control+A is pressed, then all options MAY be selected, and if all are already selected it MAY unselect them all (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given the alternative multi-select model, when Control+Down Arrow or Control+Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move without changing any selection state (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table, alternative model).
- Given the alternative multi-select model, when Control+Space is pressed, then the selection state of the focused option MUST change (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, keyboard table, alternative model).
- Given selecting or unselecting all options matters, then separate "Select All" and "Unselect All" controls SHOULD be provided, because that significantly improves accessibility (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, note).
- Given a single-select listbox, when Down Arrow is pressed, then `aria-activedescendant` MUST advance one option AND that option MUST become `aria-selected="true"`, i.e. selection follows focus (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_scrollable.js key-down-arrow assertion; test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js key-down-arrow assertion for the single-select example).
- Given a single-select listbox at its last option, when Down Arrow is pressed repeatedly, then focus MUST clamp at the last option and MUST NOT wrap to the first (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_scrollable.js and listbox_grouped.js, key-down-arrow assertions; content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js findNextOption returning null at the boundary).
- Given a single-select listbox at its first option, when Up Arrow is pressed repeatedly, then focus MUST clamp at the first option and MUST NOT wrap to the last (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_scrollable.js and listbox_grouped.js, key-up-arrow assertions).
- Given a listbox with nothing yet focused, when an arrow key is pressed, then focus MUST land on the first option and no further movement MUST occur on that press (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_grouped.js key-up-arrow assertion; content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js checkKeyPress early return when `activeDescendant` is unset).
- Given a grouped listbox, when Down Arrow is pressed on the last option of a group, then focus MUST move to the first option of the next group: arrow navigation traverses group boundaries as one flat sequence (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_grouped.js, key-down-arrow assertion).
- Given a grouped listbox, when Up Arrow is pressed on the first option of a group, then focus MUST move to the last option of the previous group (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_grouped.js, key-up-arrow assertion).
- Given a listbox, when End is pressed twice, then focus MUST land on the last option and stay there; when Home is pressed twice, on the first option and stay there (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_scrollable.js and listbox_grouped.js, key-end and key-home assertions).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when a plain arrow key moves focus, then `aria-activedescendant` MUST move but the newly focused option MUST remain `aria-selected="false"` (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, key-down-arrow and key-up-arrow assertions for the multi-select example).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when Home or End moves focus, then `aria-activedescendant` MUST move but nothing MUST become selected (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, key-home and key-end assertions).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when Space is pressed, then the focused option's `aria-selected` MUST toggle between `"true"` and `"false"` (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, key-space assertion).
- Given a single-select listbox, when Space is pressed, then nothing MUST toggle, because the APG implementation guards the toggle on `aria-multiselectable` (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js, toggleSelectItem).
- Given a multi-select listbox with the anchor at option 0, when Shift+Down Arrow is pressed, then options 0 and 1 MUST both be selected and everything else unselected (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, key-shift-down-arrow assertion).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when a plain click sets a new anchor and Shift+Down Arrow is then pressed, then the previous selection MUST be overwritten, not extended (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, key-shift-down-arrow "overwrites previous selection" assertion; content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js selectRange, which writes `false` outside the range).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when Shift+Down Arrow is pressed past the last option, then the range MUST clamp at the last option without wrapping or erroring (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, key-shift-down-arrow clamp assertion).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when Shift+Up Arrow is pressed past the first option, then the range MUST clamp at the first option (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, key-shift-up-arrow clamp assertion).
- Given a multi-select listbox with the last option selected, when Control+Shift+Home is pressed, then every option from the first to the focused one MUST be selected (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, key-control-shift-home assertion).
- Given a multi-select listbox with the first option selected, when Control+Shift+End is pressed, then every option from the focused one to the last MUST be selected (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, key-control-shift-end assertion).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when Shift+Home or Shift+End is pressed WITHOUT Control, then the selection MUST NOT change: the Control modifier is required for the range-to-end shortcuts (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, "key shift+home does not change selection" and "key shift+end does not change selection" assertions).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when Control+A is pressed, then all options MUST become selected (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, key-control-a assertion).
- Given a single-select listbox, when Control+A is pressed, then it MUST be a no-op: focus MUST NOT move and no option MUST gain `aria-selected` (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, "Control + A performs no action on single select listbox" assertion).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when the letter "a" is typed without Control, then it MUST perform typeahead and MUST NOT select anything (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, "A without control performs default focus move" assertion).
- Given a listbox, when the bare Shift key is pressed, then the range anchor MUST be captured as the index of the currently active option (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js, `startRangeIndex` assignment in the Shift keydown branch).
- Given a collapsible listbox in the closed state, when Enter, Down Arrow or Up Arrow is pressed on the button, then the listbox MUST open, DOM focus MUST move onto the `listbox` element, and the first option MUST become active (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_collapsible.js, key-enter and key-down-arrow assertions).
- Given an open collapsible listbox, when Enter is pressed, then the listbox MUST close and the button's text MUST show the selected option (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_collapsible.js, key-enter assertion).
- Given an open collapsible listbox, when Escape is pressed, then the listbox MUST close, focus MUST return to the button, and the current highlighted selection MUST be kept, not reverted (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_collapsible.js, key-escape assertion).
- Given a listbox typeahead, then the accumulated search string MUST be cleared after 500 ms of inactivity (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js, clearKeysSoFarAfterDelay).
- Given a listbox typeahead, then matching MUST be a case-insensitive prefix match against the option's text (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js, findMatchInRange).
- Given a listbox typeahead, then the search MUST begin at the option after the currently active one and, on finding no match, MUST wrap and search from the first option up to the active one (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js, findItemToFocus).
- Given a listbox typeahead, when two characters are typed quickly, then only options whose name starts with both characters MUST match; typing "c" then "a" skips an option starting with "c" that is not "ca" (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_collapsible.js, key-character assertion).
- Given a listbox typeahead, when the same character is typed again after the search string has been reset, then focus MUST advance to the next option starting with that character rather than returning to the first match, because the search always resumes after the current position (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_collapsible.js, key-character assertion with a 500 ms pause between two 'b' presses).

### Pointer

- Given a listbox, when an option is clicked, then it MUST become the active option, its selection MUST be toggled in a multi-select listbox or set in a single-select listbox, and the option MUST be scrolled into view (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js, checkClickItem).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when an option is Shift+clicked, then every option from the range anchor to the clicked option MUST become selected and everything outside that range unselected (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, key-shift-up-arrow assertion using a Shift+click action chain).
- Given a multi-select listbox, when a Shift+click lands on an option, then the native text-selection default MUST be suppressed on `mousedown` (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js, checkMouseDown).
- Given a click on something that is not a `role="option"` element, then it MUST be ignored (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js, checkClickItem).

### Focus management

- Given a listbox, then DOM focus, the active element, is functionally distinct from the selected state, and the visual focus indicator MUST be visually distinct from the selected state (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, note; content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, focus vs selection).
- Given a listbox using `aria-activedescendant`, then DOM focus MUST remain on the `listbox` container while `aria-activedescendant` names the active option (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, note; content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, managing focus using aria-activedescendant).
- Given `aria-activedescendant` is used, then the referenced element MUST satisfy one of three DOM relationships: it is a DOM descendant of the focused referencing element, or the referencing element's `aria-owns` includes its id, or the referencing element has role `combobox`, `textbox` or `searchbox` with `aria-controls` pointing at a container that supports `aria-activedescendant` and either contains the element or owns it (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, managing focus using aria-activedescendant).
- Given a listbox using `aria-activedescendant`, then the implementation MUST draw its own visual focus indicator on the active option and MUST scroll that option into view itself, because the user agent will not (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, managing focus using aria-activedescendant; content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js updateScroll).
- Given a listbox whose last option is below the visible area, when End is pressed, then that option MUST be scrolled into view (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_grouped.js, key-end assertion using bounding-rect comparison).
- Given a listbox whose last option is below the visible area, when it is clicked, then it MUST be scrolled into view (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_grouped.js, "Click scrolls listbox option into view" assertion).
- Given a listbox that receives native focus with an active option already set, then that option MUST be scrolled into view; if no option is active, the first option MUST become active (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js, setupFocus).
- Given a roving-tabindex composite instead of `aria-activedescendant`, then the benefit is that the user agent scrolls the newly focused element into view automatically (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, managing focus using a roving tabindex).
- Given `aria-owns` on a listbox pulling in non-child elements, then those elements appear in the reading order after the DOM children in reference order, and the focus-management script MUST make the visual focus order match that reading order (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, note).
- Given an element with a negative `tabindex`, then the user agent MUST consider it a focusable area but SHOULD omit it from tabindex-ordered focus navigation, so `tabindex="-1"` is programmatically and click focusable but not Tab-reachable; this is the spec basis for roving tabindex (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3 The tabindex attribute) MUST for focusability, SHOULD for the omission.
- Given `tabindex="0"`, then the element MUST be included in the sequential focus navigation order at its DOM position (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3) MUST.
- Given positive `tabindex` values, then all of them come before every element with an omitted, invalid, zero or negative value, ordered ascending by value with ties broken by tree order; a headless library MUST NOT emit positive values (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3, processing model) MUST NOT.
- Given the `tabIndex` IDL attribute with no content attribute present, then it MUST default to 0 for `a`, `area`, `button`, `frame`, `iframe`, `input`, `object`, `select`, `textarea`, SVG and MathML `a`, and a `summary` that is its parent `details`'s summary, and to -1 otherwise (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3) MUST.
- Given an element to be a focusable area, then ALL of these MUST hold: its tabindex value is non-null or the user agent determines it focusable; it is either not a shadow host or has a shadow root whose `delegatesFocus` is false; it is not actually disabled; it is not inert; and it is being rendered, delegating rendering to its children, or used as canvas fallback content (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
- Given an element can be focusable without being either sequentially focusable or click focusable, then a headless library MUST treat "focusable", "Tab-reachable" and "click-focusable" as three separate properties rather than one (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2, explicit clarification).
- Given a shadow host whose shadow root has `delegatesFocus: true`, then getting the focusable area MUST return the host's focus delegate: the first descendant with `autofocus`, otherwise the first focusable area in tree order, recursing into further delegating hosts, otherwise null (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.4 Focus, get the focusable area and focus delegate) MUST.
- Given the focusing steps, then they MUST return early when the resolved target's DOM anchor is inert, MUST no-op when the target is already the currently focused area, and MUST otherwise run the focus update steps, firing `blur` on the old chain and `focus` on the new chain with `relatedTarget` set (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.4, focusing steps and focus update steps) MUST.
- Given `element.focus(options)`, then `preventScroll` defaults to false, so scrolling into view is the DEFAULT and MUST be suppressed explicitly with `{ preventScroll: true }` (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.6 The focus() method) MUST.
- Given `element.blur()`, then the user agent SHOULD run the unfocusing steps but MAY selectively or uniformly do nothing, so a headless library MUST NOT rely on `blur()` to move focus anywhere in particular (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.6) SHOULD/MAY.
- Given a focus navigation scope, then each Document, shadow host, slot and popover trigger element owns its own tabindex-ordered focus navigation scope, and Tab traversal walks the flattened recursive composition of those scopes, skipping negative-tabindex elements (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.5 Sequential focus navigation; §6.6.2).

### State

- Given a single-select listbox, then exactly one option MAY carry the selection attribute set to `"true"` at a time, and the APG implementation removes the attribute from the previously selected option rather than setting it to `"false"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js, focusItem and defocusItem).
- Given a multi-select listbox, then every option MUST carry an explicit `aria-selected` value at all times so a conformance test can distinguish "unselected" from "not selectable" (aria-practices: test/tests/listbox_rearrangeable.js, option-aria-selected assertion for the multi-select example).
- Given a range selection, then it MUST be a full overwrite: every option inside the range gets `"true"` and every option outside it gets `"false"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js, selectRange).
- Given focus leaves a listbox that contains a selected option, then the selected state MUST persist (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, focus vs selection).

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="listbox"`, then it MUST have an accessible name from `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`, and there MUST NOT be a mismatch with a wrapping `label`; visible text content is NOT accepted as a name source, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-input-field-name.json, selector including `[role="listbox"]`).
- Given `role="option"`, then it MUST have an accessible name from visible text, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-toggle-field-name.json, selector including `[role="option"]`).
- Given `role="listbox"`, then it MUST own at least one `group` or `option`; a missing required child fails at critical impact, and `listbox` is in the `reviewEmpty` list so an empty listbox is reported as incomplete instead (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-children.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js listbox `requiredOwned`).
- Given `role="option"`, then it MUST be contained by `group` or `listbox`, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-parent.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js option `requiredContext`).
- Given a listbox whose direct children include roles outside `group` and `option`, then `aria-required-children` fails with "Element has children which are not allowed" at critical impact, which is why intervening `li` wrappers MUST carry `role="presentation"` (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-children.json, fail.unallowed).
- Given `role="option"`, then it MUST NOT contain a focusable widget-role descendant, because `option` has `childrenPresentational: true`; this is the axe-enforced version of the APG rule that a listbox cannot present interactive elements, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/nested-interactive.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js option).
- Given `role="listbox"` in the axe role table, then it is a widget-type role, marked a spec difference, whose role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-multiselectable`, `aria-readonly`, `aria-required`, `aria-activedescendant`, `aria-expanded` and `aria-orientation`, its superclass is `select`, and an accessible name is required (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, listbox).
- Given `role="option"` in the axe role table, then its role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-selected`, `aria-checked`, `aria-posinset` and `aria-setsize`, `aria-selected` is allowed rather than required because it is implicit, its superclass is `input`, a name is required and may come from content, and children are presentational (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, option).
- Given a scrollable listbox, then it MUST contain a focusable element or be focusable itself, so Safari users can scroll it from the keyboard; this is WCAG 2.1.1 and 2.1.3 A at serious impact, and a listbox with `tabindex="0"` satisfies it (axe: lib/rules/scrollable-region-focusable.json, checks focusable-content, focusable-element).
- Given a listbox implemented as a native `select`, then it MUST have an accessible name from an implicit or explicit `label`, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, `title` or a presentational role, and the explicit label MUST NOT be hidden, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/select-name.json, checks implicit-label, explicit-label, hidden-explicit-label).
- Given `aria-activedescendant` on a listbox, then its value MUST resolve to an existing element; an unresolvable id reference is reported as incomplete, and the referenced id MUST be unique in the document at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json; lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json).
- Given a listbox using `aria-activedescendant` with `tabindex="0"` on a `div`, then `focus-order-semantics` passes because `listbox` is a widget-type role in the axe table (axe: lib/checks/aria/has-widget-role-evaluate.js).
- Given decorative checkmark glyphs inside options marked `role="presentation"`, then they MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a list of interactive elements such as links, buttons or checkboxes, then a listbox MUST NOT be used, because `option` forces its descendants presentational and the listbox interaction model does not support interacting with elements inside an option; use the grid pattern instead (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given long option names, then they SHOULD be avoided, because the whole name is spoken as a single unit of speech and re-reading a long option by character or word is difficult in a listbox context (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a set of options that all start with the same word or phrase, then the data SHOULD be split into two listboxes instead, because a screen reader user has to listen to the shared prefix before hearing the distinguishing part of each option (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/listbox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a reorderable listbox, then Page Up and Page Down, or Alt+Up Arrow and Alt+Down Arrow, MAY move the focused option up or down in the list; Enter or Delete MAY move selected options to a sibling list, and both are gated on the corresponding button's `aria-keyshortcuts` (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox.js, moveUpItems/moveDownItems and the Backspace/Delete/Enter branches).
- Given a listbox operation that moves or deletes options, then the result SHOULD be announced through a live region (aria-practices: content/patterns/listbox/examples/js/listbox-rearrangeable.js, setHandleItemChange writing to a live region).

## combobox

### Roles and attributes

- Given the element that serves as an input and displays the combobox value, then it MUST have `role="combobox"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a combobox, then it MUST have `aria-controls` referring to the element that serves as the popup; `aria-controls` only needs to be set when the popup is visible, though referencing a hidden element is also valid (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a combobox popup, then it MUST have `role="listbox"`, `role="tree"`, `role="grid"` or `role="dialog"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a popup with a role other than `listbox`, then the combobox MUST have `aria-haspopup` set to `grid`, `tree` or `dialog` to match; elements with `role="combobox"` have an implicit `aria-haspopup` of `listbox` (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties; test/tests/combobox_grid-combo.js combobox-aria-haspopup assertion).
- Given a hidden combobox popup, then the combobox MUST have `aria-expanded="false"`; when the popup is visible, `aria-expanded="true"`; `combobox` has a default `aria-expanded` of `false` (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a combobox receiving focus, then DOM focus MUST be placed on the `combobox` element itself (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a focused descendant of a listbox, grid or tree popup, then DOM focus MUST remain on the combobox and the combobox MUST carry `aria-activedescendant` referring to that focused element (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a suggested value that is visually indicated as currently selected, then the containing `option`, `gridcell`, `row` or `treeitem` MUST have `aria-selected="true"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a combobox that is an HTML element labelable with `label`, such as an `input`, and that has a visible label, then it MUST be labelled with the `label` element; otherwise `aria-labelledby` MUST reference the visible label; otherwise `aria-label` MUST supply the name (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a combobox, then `aria-autocomplete` MUST be set to the value matching its behavior: `none` when the popup's suggestions do not vary with typed characters, `list` when the popup presents suggestions corresponding to the typed characters, or `both` when it does that and also shows an inline completion string after the cursor (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a combobox, then `aria-owns` MUST NOT be used to reference the popup; ARIA 1.0 used `aria-owns` but the APG strongly recommends `aria-controls` (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, note).
- Given a combobox with a graphical open button, then that button MUST have `tabindex="-1"`, its own accessible name from `aria-label`, `aria-controls` pointing at the popup, and `aria-expanded` mirroring the combobox's state (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-none.js, button-tabindex, button-aria-label, button-aria-controls and button-aria-expanded assertions).
- Given a combobox before any interaction, then `aria-activedescendant` MUST be absent; once the popup has been opened and dismissed, the APG examples set it to the empty string rather than removing it (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-none.js, combobox-aria-activedescendant assertion; content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js setActiveDescendant).
- Given a select-only combobox, then `role="combobox"` MAY sit on a `div` with `role="listbox"` on a `div` and `role="option"` on `div` children, with the value displayed as the combobox's own text content rather than an input value (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, combobox-role, listbox-role and option-role assertions).
- Given a select-only combobox, then every option MUST carry an explicit `aria-selected` of `"true"` or `"false"`, never an absent attribute (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, option-aria-selected assertion; content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/select-only.js selectOption).
- Given an editable combobox with a listbox popup, then the APG implementation removes `aria-selected` entirely from non-matching options rather than setting `"false"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js, setCurrentOptionStyle).

### Keyboard

- Given a combobox, then it MUST be in the page Tab sequence, and the popup indicator icon or button, the popup, and all popup descendants MUST be excluded from the page Tab sequence (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in the combobox with the popup available, when Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move into the popup: onto the suggestion following an automatically selected one, or otherwise onto the first focusable element in the popup (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, combobox keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in the combobox with the popup available, when Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MAY move to the last focusable element in the popup (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, combobox keyboard interaction, marked Optional).
- Given focus in the combobox with a visible popup, when Escape is pressed, then the popup MUST be dismissed; if the popup was already hidden, Escape MAY clear the combobox (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, combobox keyboard interaction).
- Given an editable combobox with a suggestion selected in the popup, when Enter is pressed, then that suggestion MUST be accepted, either by placing the input cursor at the end of the accepted value or by performing a default action on the value (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, combobox keyboard interaction).
- Given an editable combobox, when a printable character is typed, then it MUST be inserted into the combobox; some implementations MAY regard certain characters as invalid and prevent their input (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, combobox keyboard interaction).
- Given a non-editable combobox, when printable characters are typed, then focus MAY move to a value that starts with those characters (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, combobox keyboard interaction).
- Given an editable combobox, then all standard single-line text editing keys for the platform MUST keep working, so the implementation MUST NOT capture the key events used for them (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, combobox keyboard interaction and note, marked IMPORTANT).
- Given a combobox whose popup is available but not displayed, when Alt+Down Arrow is pressed, then the popup MAY be displayed without moving focus (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, combobox keyboard interaction, marked Optional).
- Given a combobox with the popup displayed, when Alt+Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MAY return to the combobox if the popup contained it, and the popup MAY close (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, combobox keyboard interaction, marked Optional).
- Given focus in a listbox popup, when Enter is pressed, then the focused option MUST be accepted: the popup closes, the accepted value goes into the combobox, and for an editable combobox the input cursor goes to the end of the value (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, listbox popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in a listbox popup, when Escape is pressed, then the popup MUST close and focus MUST return to the combobox; for an editable combobox the contents MAY be cleared (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, listbox popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in a listbox popup, when Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to and select the next option; on the last option it MUST either return focus to the combobox or do nothing (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, listbox popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in a listbox popup, when Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to and select the previous option; on the first option it MUST either return focus to the combobox or do nothing (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, listbox popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in a listbox popup of an editable combobox, when Right Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST return to the combobox without closing the popup and the input cursor MUST move one character right, staying put if already at the rightmost character (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, listbox popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in a listbox popup of an editable combobox, when Left Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST return to the combobox without closing the popup and the input cursor MUST move one character left, staying put if already at the leftmost character (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, listbox popup keyboard interaction).
