- Given focus in a listbox popup, when Home is pressed, then it MAY either move focus to and select the first option, or for an editable combobox return focus to the combobox with the cursor on the first character (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, listbox popup keyboard interaction, marked Optional).
- Given focus in a listbox popup, when End is pressed, then it MAY either move focus to the last option, or for an editable combobox return focus to the combobox with the cursor after the last character (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, listbox popup keyboard interaction, marked Optional).
- Given focus in a listbox popup of an editable combobox, when any printable character is typed, then focus MUST return to the combobox without closing the popup and the character MUST be typed; for a non-editable combobox, focus MUST instead move to the next option starting with the typed characters (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, listbox popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in a listbox popup of an editable combobox, when Backspace is pressed, then focus MAY return to the combobox and delete the character before the cursor (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, listbox popup keyboard interaction, marked Optional).
- Given focus in a listbox popup of an editable combobox, when Delete is pressed, then focus MAY return to the combobox, remove the selected state from any selected suggestion, and remove the inline autocomplete string (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, listbox popup keyboard interaction, marked Optional).
- Given a combobox with a listbox popup, then selection follows focus in that listbox and only one suggested value MAY be selected at a time (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, note after the listbox popup keyboard table).
- Given focus in a grid popup, when Enter is pressed, then the currently selected suggested value MUST be accepted, closing the popup and placing that value in the combobox (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, grid popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in a grid popup, when Escape is pressed, then the popup MUST close and focus MUST return to the combobox; for an editable combobox the contents MAY be cleared (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, grid popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in a grid popup, when Right Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move one cell right; from the rightmost cell of a row focus MAY move to the first cell of the following row, and from the last cell of the grid it MUST either do nothing or return focus to the combobox (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, grid popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in a grid popup, when Left Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move one cell left; from the leftmost cell of a row focus MAY move to the last cell of the previous row, and from the first cell it MUST either do nothing or return focus to the combobox (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, grid popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in a grid popup, when Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move one cell down; in the last row it MUST either do nothing or return focus to the combobox (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, grid popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in a grid popup, when Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move one cell up; in the first row it MUST either do nothing or return focus to the combobox (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, grid popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in a grid popup, when Page Down is pressed, then focus MAY move down an author-determined number of rows, typically scrolling so the bottom visible row becomes one of the first visible rows; in the last row focus MUST NOT move (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, grid popup keyboard interaction, marked Optional).
- Given focus in a grid popup, when Page Up is pressed, then focus MAY move up an author-determined number of rows; in the first row focus MUST NOT move (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, grid popup keyboard interaction, marked Optional).
- Given focus in a grid popup, when Home is pressed, then it MAY either move focus to the first cell in the current row, or to the first cell in the grid when the grid has fewer than three cells per row or multiple suggested values per row, or for an editable combobox return focus to the combobox with the cursor on the first character (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, grid popup keyboard interaction, marked Optional).
- Given focus in a grid popup, when End is pressed, then it MAY either move focus to the last cell in the current row, or to the last cell in the grid under the same conditions, or for an editable combobox return focus to the combobox with the cursor after the last character (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, grid popup keyboard interaction, marked Optional).
- Given focus in a grid popup, when Control+Home is pressed, then focus MAY move to the first row; when Control+End is pressed, to the last row (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, grid popup keyboard interaction, marked optional).
- Given focus in a grid popup of an editable combobox, when any printable character is typed, then focus MUST return to the combobox without closing the popup and the character MUST be typed (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, grid popup keyboard interaction).
- Given a grid popup where every cell holds a different suggested value, then selection MUST follow focus so the focused cell is selected, horizontal arrow navigation typically wraps row to row, and vertical arrow navigation typically wraps column to column (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, note after the grid popup keyboard table).
- Given a grid popup where all cells in a row describe the same suggested value, then either the focused row is selected or a designated value cell in that row is selected when any cell in the row has focus, horizontal navigation MAY wrap row to row, and vertical navigation MUST NOT wrap column to column (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, note after the grid popup keyboard table).
- Given focus on a closed node in a tree popup, when Right Arrow is pressed, then the node MUST open with focus and selection unchanged (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, tree popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus on an open node in a tree popup, when Right Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the first child node and select it if it is selectable (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, tree popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus on an end node in a tree popup, when Right Arrow is pressed, then nothing MUST happen (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, tree popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus on an open node in a tree popup, when Left Arrow is pressed, then the node MUST close (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, tree popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus on a child node in a tree popup that is an end node or a closed node, when Left Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to its parent node and select it if it is selectable (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, tree popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus on a root node in a tree popup that is an end node or a closed node, when Left Arrow is pressed, then nothing MUST happen (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, tree popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in a tree popup, when Down Arrow or Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the next or previous focusable node without opening or closing anything, selecting it if it is selectable (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, tree popup keyboard interaction).
- Given focus in a tree popup, when Home or End is pressed, then focus MUST move to the first or last focusable node without opening or closing anything, selecting it if it is selectable (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, tree popup keyboard interaction).
- Given a tree popup where focus moves onto a node that is not a selectable value, then either the previously selected node stays selected until focus reaches a selectable node, or there is no selected value; in either case focus MUST be visually distinct from selection (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, note after the tree popup keyboard table).
- Given a dialog popup, then there are exactly two ways to close it and return focus to the combobox: perform an action in the dialog that specifies a value, or cancel out of it with Escape or a cancel button (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, dialog popup keyboard interaction).
- Given a cancelled dialog popup, then focus MUST return either to the text box with the combobox value unchanged, or to the combobox with the combobox cleared (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, dialog popup keyboard interaction).
- Given a dialog popup, then it MUST implement the modal dialog pattern's keyboard interaction, and unlike other popups it does NOT support `aria-activedescendant`, so DOM focus MUST move into the dialog (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, dialog popup keyboard interaction and note).
- Given an editable combobox with `aria-autocomplete="none"`, when a character is typed, then `aria-expanded` MUST become `"true"`, the popup MUST be displayed, and the option list MUST NOT be filtered (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-none.js, combobox-aria-expanded and standard-single-line-editing-keys assertions).
- Given `aria-autocomplete="none"`, when a character is typed, then no option MUST gain `aria-selected`; only an explicit arrow press highlights an option (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-none.js, option-aria-selected assertion).
- Given `aria-autocomplete="list"`, when a character is typed, then the options MUST be filtered to matching entries, and no option MUST be auto-highlighted (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-list.js, standard-single-line-editing-keys and option-aria-selected assertions).
- Given `aria-autocomplete="both"`, when a character is typed, then the first matching option MUST become `aria-selected="true"` immediately and the combobox value MUST be inline-completed to that option's full text (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-both.js, option-aria-selected and textbox-key-enter assertions).
- Given `aria-autocomplete="both"` with an inline completion in place, then the completion string MUST be selected in the input from the typed prefix length to the end, so the next typed character replaces it (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js, setOption calling `setSelectionRange`).
- Given an editable combobox with the popup open on the last option, when Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST wrap to the first option; combobox listbox navigation wraps, unlike the standalone listbox pattern which clamps (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-none.js listbox-key-down-arrow assertion; content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js getNextOption).
- Given an editable combobox with an empty value and a closed popup, when Up Arrow is pressed, then the popup MUST open with the LAST option highlighted (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-none.js, textbox-key-up-arrow assertion).
- Given an editable combobox with an empty value and a closed popup, when Down Arrow is pressed, then the popup MUST open with the FIRST option highlighted (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-none.js, textbox-key-down-arrow assertion).
- Given an editable combobox with `aria-autocomplete="none"`, when Alt+Down Arrow is pressed, then the popup MUST open with NO option highlighted (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-none.js, textbox-key-alt-down-arrow assertion).
- Given an editable combobox with text typed and no option highlighted, when Enter is pressed, then the popup MUST close and the typed value MUST be preserved unchanged (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-none.js and combobox_autocomplete-list.js, textbox-key-enter assertions).
- Given an editable combobox with an option highlighted, when Enter is pressed, then the popup MUST close and the combobox value MUST become that option's text (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-none.js and combobox_autocomplete-list.js, listbox-key-enter assertions).
- Given an editable combobox with the popup open, when Escape is pressed once, then the popup MUST close; when Escape is pressed a second time with the popup already closed, then the combobox value MUST be cleared to the empty string (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-none.js, combobox_autocomplete-list.js, combobox_autocomplete-both.js and combobox_grid-combo.js, the single-escape and double-escape assertions).
- Given `aria-autocomplete="both"` with an inline-completed value, when Escape is pressed once, then the completed value MUST be preserved, not reverted to the raw typed prefix (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-both.js, textbox-key-escape and listbox-key-escape assertions).
- Given an editable combobox with an option highlighted, when Left Arrow, Right Arrow, Home or End is pressed, then the input cursor MUST move accordingly and `aria-activedescendant` MUST be cleared, returning virtual focus to the text field without closing the popup (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-none.js, listbox-key-left-arrow, listbox-key-right-arrow, listbox-key-home and listbox-key-end assertions).
- Given an editable combobox with an option highlighted, when a printable character is typed, then it MUST be inserted into the combobox and `aria-activedescendant` MUST be cleared (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-none.js, listbox-key-char assertion).
- Given `aria-autocomplete="both"` with an option already highlighted and the value inline-completed, when a printable character is typed, then it MUST be appended to the existing completed text rather than replacing it (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-both.js, listbox-characters assertion).
- Given `aria-autocomplete="list"`, when Backspace is pressed, then the popup MUST stay open and the value MUST lose one character, with the option list re-filtered against the shorter prefix (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-list.js, standard-single-line-editing-keys assertions).
- Given `aria-autocomplete="both"` with an inline-completed value, when Backspace is pressed, then the auto-completed suffix MUST be removed, leaving the user-typed prefix, and the popup MUST stay open (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-both.js, standard-single-line-editing-keys assertions).
- Given an editable combobox with an option highlighted, when Tab is pressed, then the popup MUST close and the highlighted option's text MUST be committed as the value, while the default Tab behavior MUST proceed (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js, onComboboxKeyDown Tab case, which commits but does not preventDefault).
- Given a select-only combobox in the closed state, when Down Arrow, Up Arrow, Enter or Space is pressed, then the popup MUST open with the first option highlighted; unlike editable comboboxes, Up Arrow does NOT jump to the last option (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, combobox-key-up-arrow, combobox-key-down-arrow, combobox-key-enter and combobox-key-space assertions).
- Given a select-only combobox in the closed state, when Alt+Down Arrow is pressed, then the popup MUST open with the first option highlighted (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, combobox-key-alt-down-arrow assertion).
- Given a select-only combobox in the closed state, when End is pressed, then the popup MUST open with the LAST option highlighted; when Home is pressed, with the FIRST option highlighted regardless of prior position (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, combobox-key-end and combobox-key-home assertions).
- Given a select-only combobox that was closed with Escape while a non-default option was highlighted, when it is reopened, then it MUST reopen with that same option highlighted rather than resetting to the first (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, "combobox opens on last highlighted option" assertion).
- Given a select-only combobox with the popup open, when Enter, Space or Alt+Up Arrow is pressed, then the highlighted option MUST be committed as the value, `aria-selected="true"` MUST move to it, and the popup MUST close (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, listbox-key-enter, listbox-key-space and listbox-key-alt-up-arrow assertions).
- Given a select-only combobox with the popup open, when Tab is pressed, then the highlighted option MUST be committed and the popup MUST close (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, listbox-key-tab assertion).
- Given a select-only combobox with the popup open and a different option highlighted, when Escape is pressed, then the popup MUST close WITHOUT committing, and the displayed value MUST revert to the previously committed option (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, listbox-key-escape assertion).
- Given a select-only combobox with the popup open, when Down Arrow is pressed on the last option, then the highlight MUST clamp; when Up Arrow is pressed on the first option, it MUST clamp; select-only comboboxes do NOT wrap (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, listbox-key-down-arrow and listbox-key-up-arrow assertions; content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/select-only.js getUpdatedIndex, which clamps with `min` and `max`).
- Given a select-only combobox with the popup open, when Page Down is pressed, then the highlight MUST move 10 options down, clamping at the last option; Page Up MUST move 10 up, clamping at the first (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, listbox-key-pagedown and listbox-key-pageup assertions; content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/select-only.js with `pageSize = 10`).
- Given a select-only combobox, when a character matching an option is typed while closed, then the popup MUST open with that option highlighted (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, printable-chars assertion).
- Given a select-only combobox, when the same character is typed repeatedly, then the highlight MUST cycle through every option starting with that character and then wrap around (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, "Multiple single-character presses cycle through options" assertion; content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/select-only.js getIndexByLetter with its `allSameLetter` retry).
- Given a select-only combobox, when multiple different characters are typed, then the accumulated string MUST narrow the match, with the search string reset 500 ms after the last keystroke (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js "Typing multiple characters refines search" assertion; content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/select-only.js getSearchString with its 500 ms timeout).
- Given a select-only combobox, then the typeahead search MUST resume from the option after the currently active one, wrapping around the list (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/select-only.js, onComboType calling getIndexByLetter with `activeIndex + 1`).
- Given a grid-popup combobox with an empty value, when Down Arrow or Up Arrow is pressed, then the popup MUST NOT open, because there are no results for an empty query (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_grid-combo.js, textbox-key-down-arrow and textbox-key-up-arrow assertions).
- Given a grid-popup combobox with results, when Down Arrow is pressed, then `aria-activedescendant` MUST point at the cell in column 0 of the first row, and that cell MUST also carry a visual focus class, because `aria-activedescendant` alone draws nothing (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_grid-combo.js, textbox-key-down-arrow assertion checking the `focused-cell` class).
- Given a grid-popup combobox, when Down Arrow is pressed in the last row, then the active cell MUST wrap to the first row; when Up Arrow is pressed in the first row, it MUST wrap to the last row (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_grid-combo.js, popup-key-down-arrow and popup-key-up-arrow assertions).
- Given a grid-popup combobox, when Right Arrow is pressed past the last column of a row, then the active cell MUST move to column 0 of the next row, and past the last cell of the grid it MUST wrap to the first cell (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_grid-combo.js, popup-key-right-arrow assertion).
- Given a grid-popup combobox, when Left Arrow is pressed from column 0, then the active cell MUST move to the last column of the previous row, wrapping from the first row to the last (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_grid-combo.js, popup-key-left-arrow assertion).
- Given a grid-popup combobox with a highlighted cell, when Escape is pressed, then the popup MUST close and the typed value MUST be preserved (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_grid-combo.js, popup-key-escape assertion).
- Given a grid-popup combobox, when Tab is pressed, then the popup MUST close (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/grid-combo.js, handleInputKeyDown Tab case).

### Pointer

- Given an open combobox popup, when the pointer is released outside both the combobox and the popup, then the popup MUST close and the highlighted-but-uncommitted value MUST be discarded, leaving the typed value in place (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-list.js, test-additional-behavior assertions).
- Given an editable combobox, then outside-click dismissal MUST use a capturing `pointerup` listener on the document body, and the APG example defers the close by 300 ms, which is why a conformance test MUST wait for the popup to disappear rather than asserting synchronously (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js, onBackgroundPointerUp).
- Given a grid-popup combobox, then outside-click dismissal MUST be immediate with no delay (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/grid-combo.js, handleBodyClick).
- Given an open combobox popup, when an option is clicked, then its text MUST become the combobox value and the popup MUST close (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js, option click handler).
- Given a select-only combobox, when it is clicked, then the popup MUST toggle open and closed (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, test-additional-behavior assertion).
- Given a select-only combobox with the popup open, when an option is clicked, then the popup MUST close, the combobox's own text MUST become that option's text, and that option MUST become `aria-selected="true"` (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_select-only.js, test-additional-behavior assertion).
- Given a select-only combobox, when the pointer presses down on an option, then the blur-driven auto-commit MUST be suppressed so the click can run instead; the APG example sets an `ignoreBlur` flag on `mousedown` (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/select-only.js, onOptionMouseDown).
- Given an open combobox popup, when the pointer leaves an option or the popup, then a non-forced close MAY be scheduled after 300 ms, cancellable if hover or focus returns (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js, option pointerout with its delayed close).
- Given a combobox open button, when it is clicked, then the popup MUST toggle and DOM focus MUST be moved back to the combobox (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js, onButtonClick).

### Focus management

- Given an open listbox, grid or tree popup, then DOM focus MUST stay on the combobox and assistive technology focus MUST be moved with `aria-activedescendant` (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, notes after the listbox, grid and tree popup keyboard tables).
- Given a combobox popup that is a dialog, then DOM focus MUST move into the dialog, because dialogs do not support `aria-activedescendant` (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, note after the dialog popup keyboard table).
- Given a combobox using `aria-activedescendant` across an `aria-controls` boundary, then the relationship is legal only because `combobox`, `textbox` and `searchbox` are explicitly permitted to point `aria-activedescendant` at a descendant of the element named by their `aria-controls` (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, managing focus using aria-activedescendant).
- Given a combobox whose active popup item is out of view, then the implementation MUST scroll it into view itself (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js setActiveDescendant with its `scrollIntoView`; grid-combo.js focusCell).
- Given a select-only combobox that loses focus while the popup is open, then the highlighted option MUST be committed and the popup MUST close, without stealing focus back (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/select-only.js, onComboBlur).
- Given a select-only combobox whose focus moves to an element inside its own popup, then the blur handler MUST do nothing, because focus has not left the widget (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/select-only.js, onComboBlur checking `relatedTarget`).
- Given an element with a negative `tabindex`, then the user agent MUST consider it a focusable area but SHOULD omit it from tabindex-ordered focus navigation, so `tabindex="-1"` is programmatically and click focusable but not Tab-reachable; this is the spec basis for roving tabindex (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3 The tabindex attribute) MUST for focusability, SHOULD for the omission.
- Given `tabindex="0"`, then the element MUST be included in the sequential focus navigation order at its DOM position (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3) MUST.
- Given positive `tabindex` values, then all of them come before every element with an omitted, invalid, zero or negative value, ordered ascending by value with ties broken by tree order; a headless library MUST NOT emit positive values (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3, processing model) MUST NOT.
- Given the `tabIndex` IDL attribute with no content attribute present, then it MUST default to 0 for `a`, `area`, `button`, `frame`, `iframe`, `input`, `object`, `select`, `textarea`, SVG and MathML `a`, and a `summary` that is its parent `details`'s summary, and to -1 otherwise (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.3) MUST.
- Given an element to be a focusable area, then ALL of these MUST hold: its tabindex value is non-null or the user agent determines it focusable; it is either not a shadow host or has a shadow root whose `delegatesFocus` is false; it is not actually disabled; it is not inert; and it is being rendered, delegating rendering to its children, or used as canvas fallback content (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
- Given an element can be focusable without being either sequentially focusable or click focusable, then a headless library MUST treat "focusable", "Tab-reachable" and "click-focusable" as three separate properties rather than one (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2, explicit clarification).
- Given a shadow host whose shadow root has `delegatesFocus: true`, then getting the focusable area MUST return the host's focus delegate: the first descendant with `autofocus`, otherwise the first focusable area in tree order, recursing into further delegating hosts, otherwise null (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.4 Focus, get the focusable area and focus delegate) MUST.
- Given the focusing steps, then they MUST return early when the resolved target's DOM anchor is inert, MUST no-op when the target is already the currently focused area, and MUST otherwise run the focus update steps, firing `blur` on the old chain and `focus` on the new chain with `relatedTarget` set (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.4, focusing steps and focus update steps) MUST.
- Given `element.focus(options)`, then `preventScroll` defaults to false, so scrolling into view is the DEFAULT and MUST be suppressed explicitly with `{ preventScroll: true }` (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.6 The focus() method) MUST.
- Given `element.blur()`, then the user agent SHOULD run the unfocusing steps but MAY selectively or uniformly do nothing, so a headless library MUST NOT rely on `blur()` to move focus anywhere in particular (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.6) SHOULD/MAY.
- Given a focus navigation scope, then each Document, shadow host, slot and popover trigger element owns its own tabindex-ordered focus navigation scope, and Tab traversal walks the flattened recursive composition of those scopes, skipping negative-tabindex elements (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.5 Sequential focus navigation; §6.6.2).

### State

- Given a combobox, then `aria-expanded` MUST be kept in sync on both the combobox element and the separate open button, if one exists (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_autocomplete-none.js, combobox-aria-expanded and button-aria-expanded assertions; content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js open and close).
- Given a combobox popup, then at most one suggestion MAY be selected at a time, because a combobox is a single-select widget where selection follows focus in the popup (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, note before the popup pattern links).
- Given `aria-autocomplete="none"`, then the filter MUST be forced to the empty string internally so the option set never changes as the user types (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js, filterOptions).
- Given a combobox whose typed string matches no suggestion, then the popup MAY be closed and `aria-activedescendant` cleared (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js, onComboboxKeyUp default branch).
- Given `aria-autocomplete="both"` where the typed prefix no longer matches the current inline completion, then the completion MUST be recomputed against the full current input value, not against a stale accumulated filter (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js, onComboboxKeyUp default branch, which checks the prefix against the full value).

### Dismissal and layering

- Given a combobox popup implemented with the `popover` attribute, then `popover="auto"` provides light dismiss on an outside `pointerdown`/`pointerup` pair and response to close requests, matching the APG outside-click and Escape behavior (WHATWG HTML: §6.12 The popover attribute; §6.12.2 Popover light dismiss).
- Given a combobox popup as a popover, then it MUST NOT steal focus on show unless it contains an `autofocus` element, because the popover focusing steps have no fallback to the popover element itself; this is exactly what a combobox needs, since DOM focus must stay on the input (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, popover focusing steps) MUST.
- Given a combobox whose popup is a dialog rather than a listbox, then the native `dialog` element's focusing steps DO fall back to focusing the dialog itself, which is why DOM focus moves into a dialog popup but not into a listbox popup (WHATWG HTML: §4.11.4, dialog focusing steps).
- Given a combobox popup opened by a button with `popovertarget`, then a `show` action while already showing, or a `hide` action while already hidden, MUST be a no-op, so a double-activation cannot desynchronize `aria-expanded` (WHATWG HTML: §6.12.1 The popover target attributes) MUST.
- Given `beforetoggle` on a combobox popup, then cancelling it MUST abort the show or hide entirely, so `aria-expanded` MUST be written from the `toggle` event or after the show call resolves, not optimistically before (WHATWG HTML: §6.12, show popover and hide popover algorithms) MUST.

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="combobox"`, then it MUST have an accessible name from `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`, and there MUST NOT be a mismatch with a wrapping `label`; this is WCAG 4.1.2 A at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-input-field-name.json, selector including `[role="combobox"]`).
- Given `role="combobox"`, then BOTH `aria-expanded` AND `aria-controls` are REQUIRED attributes, so omitting either fails at critical impact; this makes `aria-controls` mandatory in axe even though the APG says it only needs to be set while the popup is visible (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js combobox `requiredAttrs`; lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json).
- Given `role="combobox"` in the axe role table, then it is a widget-type role, marked a spec difference, whose role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-owns`, `aria-autocomplete`, `aria-readonly`, `aria-required`, `aria-activedescendant` and `aria-orientation`, its superclass is `select`, an accessible name is required, and children are NOT presentational (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, combobox).
- Given `aria-controls` on a combobox that also carries `aria-haspopup`, then an unresolvable reference is reported as incomplete with "Unable to determine if aria-controls referenced ID exists on the page while using aria-haspopup", so a lazily rendered popup surfaces as needing review rather than as a failure (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, incomplete.controlsWithinPopup).
- Given `aria-activedescendant` on a combobox pointing into its popup, then the value MUST resolve to an existing element, and the empty-string form used when virtual focus returns to the input is reported as incomplete, "ARIA attribute value is ignored while empty", rather than as a pass (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, incomplete.empty).
- Given `aria-autocomplete` on a combobox, then its value MUST be one of the valid tokens; a typo fails at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json).
- Given a combobox with a `listbox` popup, then all the listbox-side rules apply to the popup: `aria-required-children` on the listbox, `aria-required-parent` on each option, `aria-toggle-field-name` on each option, and `nested-interactive` on each option (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-children.json, aria-required-parent.json, aria-toggle-field-name.json, nested-interactive.json).
- Given a combobox built on a native `input`, then `label` applies at critical impact, `label-title-only` at serious impact, and `form-field-multiple-labels` at moderate impact (axe: lib/rules/label.json, label-title-only.json, form-field-multiple-labels.json).
- Given a combobox built on a native `select`, then `select-name` applies at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/select-name.json).
- Given a combobox input with an `autocomplete` attribute, then the value MUST be correctly formatted; this is WCAG 2.1 AA 1.3.5 at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/autocomplete-valid.json).
- Given a scrollable combobox popup, then it MUST contain a focusable element or be focusable itself, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/scrollable-region-focusable.json).
- Given a select-only combobox built on a `div` with `tabindex="0"`, then `focus-order-semantics` passes because `combobox` is a widget-type role (axe: lib/checks/aria/has-widget-role-evaluate.js).
- Given a combobox popup hidden with `aria-hidden="true"` rather than removed or `display: none`, then its focusable descendants MUST be made non-focusable or removed, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json).
- Given a combobox with `aria-roledescription`, then `combobox` is one of the role table`'`s supported roles for it, though the rule ships disabled and tagged deprecated (axe: lib/rules/aria-roledescription.json, `supportedRoles` including combobox, `enabled: false`).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a combobox that does not support text input, then it is select-only and the only way to set its value is by choosing one in the popup (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a combobox that supports text input, then it is editable, and it either allows any arbitrary value or restricts its value to a discrete set, in which case typing filters the suggestions (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given an editable combobox, then the conditions that trigger expansion are implementation-specific; possibilities include Down Arrow or the open button, focus alone even when the field is empty, or reaching a threshold number of typed characters that match a suggestion (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given an editable combobox with an open button, then expansion on Down Arrow MAY be suppressed while the field contains a string that matches no allowed value (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given an editable combobox with list autocomplete, then the popup MAY appear and disappear as the user types: if a third character forms a string with no matches, the popup MAY close and the inline completion string disappear (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given list autocomplete with manual selection, then the character string the user has typed becomes the combobox value unless the user selects a value in the popup (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given list autocomplete with automatic selection, then the automatically selected suggestion becomes the combobox value when the combobox loses focus, unless the user chooses a different suggestion or changes the typed string (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given list with inline autocomplete, then it is list-with-automatic-selection plus a completion string rendered inline after the cursor, visually highlighted and in a selected state (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a choice between combobox, listbox and menu button, then combobox and menu button both let users explore the available choices and press Escape to back out without changing previous input, whereas navigating a single-select listbox immediately changes its value and Escape provides no undo (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a control that must be marked required, then combobox and listbox both support `aria-required="true"` and both have an accessible name distinct from their value, while a menu button cannot be marked required and has no value, so a menu button MUST NOT be used to convey a choice in its collapsed state (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/combobox-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given an editable combobox with `aria-autocomplete="none"`, then filtering MUST be disabled entirely: the APG implementation forces the filter to the empty string in that mode (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/combobox-autocomplete.js, filterOptions).
- Given a grid-popup combobox, then closing the popup MAY destroy the grid's DOM rather than hiding it; the APG grid combo clears `innerHTML` on close and rebuilds on every search (aria-practices: content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/grid-combo.js, hideResults and updateResults).
- Given a grid-popup combobox, then `aria-selected` is tracked per row on one designated column rather than on the focused cell, so focus and selection move independently across columns; the corresponding APG test for row `aria-selected` is marked failing under issue 859 (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_grid-combo.js, row-aria-selected assertion marked `ariaTest.failing`; content/patterns/combobox/examples/js/grid-combo.js `selectionCol`).
- Given the grid-combo standard-editing-keys assertion, then it is a deliberate always-failing placeholder in the APG suite, so it provides no baseline (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_grid-combo.js, standard-single-line-editing-keys marked `ariaTest.failing` with a bare `t.fail()`).
- Given a grid-popup combobox on macOS, then Home deselects the popup without moving the text cursor, a known bug tracked as APG issue 3191, and the APG test skips that platform (aria-practices: test/tests/combobox_grid-combo.js, popup-key-home assertion with its `isMacOS()` guard).

## toolbar

### Roles and attributes

- Given the container grouping a set of controls, then it MUST have `role="toolbar"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a toolbar with a visible label, then it MUST have `aria-labelledby` referencing that label; otherwise `aria-label` MUST supply the name (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a toolbar whose controls are arranged vertically, then it MUST have `aria-orientation="vertical"`; the default orientation is horizontal (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a toolbar, then it SHOULD be used as a grouping element only when the group contains three or more controls (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a toolbar that controls another element, then it MAY have `aria-controls` referencing that element; the APG toolbar points at the text area it formats (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-aria-controls assertion).
- Given icon glyphs inside toolbar buttons, then they MUST carry `aria-hidden="true"` (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-aria-hidden assertion).
- Given a radio group nested in a toolbar, then it MUST have `role="radiogroup"` with `aria-label`, and each radio MUST have `role="radio"` with `aria-checked` (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-radiogroup-role, toolbar-radiogroup-aria-label, toolbar-radio-role and toolbar-radio-aria-checked assertions).
- Given a menu button nested in a toolbar, then it MUST have `aria-label`, `aria-haspopup="true"`, `aria-controls` pointing at the menu, and `aria-expanded` reflecting the menu's state (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-menubutton-* assertions).
- Given a spinbutton nested in a toolbar, then it MUST have `aria-label`, `aria-valuenow`, `aria-valuetext`, `aria-valuemin` and `aria-valuemax`, and the visible text MUST match `aria-valuetext` (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-spinbutton-* assertions).

### Keyboard

- Given a toolbar, then focus management MUST be implemented so the keyboard tab sequence contains exactly one stop for the whole toolbar and arrow keys move focus among its controls (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given Tab moving focus into a toolbar for the first time, then focus MUST be set on the first control that is not disabled (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given Tab moving focus into a toolbar that has previously contained focus, then focus MAY be set on the control that last had focus; otherwise it MUST be set on the first control that is not disabled (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a horizontal toolbar, when Left Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the previous control, and focus movement MAY wrap from the first element to the last (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a horizontal toolbar, when Right Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the next control, and focus movement MAY wrap from the last element to the first (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, keyboard table).
- Given focus in a toolbar, when Home is pressed, then focus MAY move to the first element; when End is pressed, to the last element (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, keyboard table, marked Optional).
- Given a vertically arranged toolbar, then Down Arrow MUST behave as Right Arrow and Up Arrow MUST behave as Left Arrow (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, note).
- Given a horizontal toolbar, then Up Arrow and Down Arrow MAY duplicate Left Arrow and Right Arrow, or MAY be reserved for operating embedded controls such as spin buttons that need vertical arrows (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a vertical toolbar, then Left Arrow and Right Arrow MAY duplicate Up Arrow and Down Arrow, or MAY be reserved for operating embedded controls such as horizontal sliders (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a toolbar with multiple rows of controls, then Left Arrow and Right Arrow MAY wrap from row to row, leaving vertical arrows free for operating controls (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a toolbar, then controls whose operation requires the arrow-key pair used for toolbar navigation SHOULD be avoided; if unavoidable, at most one such control SHOULD be included and it SHOULD be the last element in the toolbar, for example a text box at the end of a horizontal toolbar (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given an application where quick access to a toolbar matters, such as reaching an editor's toolbar from its text area, then a documented shortcut key for moving focus to the toolbar is recommended (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, note) SHOULD.
- Given focus on the first toolbar control, when Left Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST wrap to the last control (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-left-arrow assertion).
- Given focus on the last toolbar control, when Right Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST wrap to the first control (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-right-arrow assertion).
- Given focus anywhere in the toolbar, when Home is pressed, then focus AND the `tabindex="0"` MUST both move to the very first control in the whole toolbar, crossing group boundaries; End MUST do the same for the last control (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-home and toolbar-end assertions, verified from every position).
- Given the toolbar uses one shared roving tabindex ring, then exactly one `.item` across every functional group MUST have `tabindex="0"` at any moment and all others `tabindex="-1"` (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-item-tabindex assertion via assertRovingTabindex).
- Given focus on the element before the toolbar, when Tab is pressed, then focus MUST enter the toolbar at whichever control currently has `tabindex="0"` (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-tab assertion).
- Given focus on a toolbar control, when Tab is pressed, then focus MUST leave the whole toolbar and land on the next control after it (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-tab assertion).
- Given a toggle button in a toolbar, when Enter or Space is pressed, then `aria-pressed` MUST flip and the corresponding formatting MUST be applied or removed on the controlled element (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-toggle-enter-or-space assertion).
- Given an unchecked radio in a toolbar, when Enter or Space is pressed, then it MUST become `aria-checked="true"` and the previously checked radio in the group MUST become `"false"` (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-radio-enter-or-space assertion).
- Given an already-checked radio in a toolbar, when Enter or Space is pressed, then it MUST stay checked with no toggle-off (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-radio-enter-or-space "do nothing" assertion).
- Given focus on a radio inside a toolbar, when Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the next radio within the group only, wrapping from the last radio to the first (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-radio-down-arrow assertion; content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FormatToolbarItem.js, whose Up and Down handling applies only when `buttonAction === 'align'`).
- Given focus on a radio inside a toolbar, when Up Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST move to the previous radio within the group only, wrapping from the first radio to the last (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-radio-up-arrow assertion).
- Given focus on any non-radio toolbar control, when Up Arrow or Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST NOT move: vertical arrows are reserved for the embedded composites (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FormatToolbarItem.js, handleKeyDown Up and Down cases, gated on the align action).
- Given a toolbar menu button, when Enter, Space, Up Arrow or Down Arrow is pressed, then the menu MUST open and focus MUST land on the currently checked item, which is the first item only when no other choice has been made (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-menubutton-enter-or-space-or-down-or-up assertion; content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FontMenuButton.js, setFocusToCheckedItem).
- Given a toolbar menu item, when Enter or Space is pressed, then the corresponding setting MUST be applied to the controlled element (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-menu-enter-or-space assertion).
- Given focus on the last item of an open toolbar menu, when Down Arrow is pressed, then focus MUST wrap to the first item; from the first item, Up Arrow MUST wrap to the last (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-menu-down-arrow and toolbar-menu-up-arrow assertions).
- Given an open toolbar menu, when Escape is pressed on any item, then the menu MUST close and focus MUST return to the menu button (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-menu-escape assertion).
- Given focus in a toolbar menu, when Right Arrow or Left Arrow is pressed, then nothing MUST happen, although the default MUST still be suppressed (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FontMenuItem.js, handleKeydown Right and Left cases, which set the handled flag with no action).
- Given focus in a toolbar menu, when Tab is pressed, then the menu MUST close and focus MUST return to the menu button, with the default Tab behavior allowed to proceed (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FontMenuItem.js, handleKeydown Tab case).
- Given focus on a toolbar spinbutton, when Down Arrow is pressed, then `aria-valuenow` MUST decrease by 1; Up Arrow MUST increase by 1; Page Down MUST decrease by 5; Page Up MUST increase by 5 (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-spinbutton-down-arrow, -up-arrow, -page-down and -page-up assertions).
- Given focus on a toolbar spinbutton, when Home or End is pressed, then the value MUST jump to the minimum or maximum; the APG implements this although the test suite has no assertion for it (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/SpinButton.js, handleKeyDown Home and End cases).
- Given a toolbar with hover labels, when Escape is pressed anywhere in the document, then every open hover label MUST be hidden (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js toolbar-toggle-esc assertion; content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FormatToolbarItem.js handleHideAllPopupLabels bound to `document.body`).

### Pointer

- Given a toolbar control, when it is clicked, then focus MUST be set on it and its action MUST be performed, so the roving tabindex stays consistent with the pointer state (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js toolbar-item-tabindex assertion; content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FormatToolbarItem.js handleClick).
- Given a toolbar control that is a link, when it is clicked, then the native anchor behavior MUST be allowed to proceed with no scripted action (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FormatToolbarItem.js, handleClick early return for the link action).
- Given a toolbar menu button, when it is clicked, then the menu MUST toggle (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FontMenuButton.js, handleClick).
- Given a toolbar spinbutton's increase or decrease button, when it is clicked, then the value MUST change by one step (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/SpinButton.js, increase and decrease click handlers).
- Given a toolbar hover label, then it MUST appear immediately on `mouseover` and on `focus`, MUST hide immediately on `blur`, and MUST hide 800 ms after `mouseleave` unless hover is regained in the meantime (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FormatToolbarItem.js, showPopupLabel/hidePopupLabel with `popupLabelDelay = 800`).
- Given an element with an activation behavior, then activation is always caused by a `click` event, and when the user triggers it by any other means such as keyboard or voice, the default action of that interaction event MUST be to fire a `click` event at the element; the user agent SHOULD allow such non-pointer triggering (WHATWG HTML: §6.5 Activation behavior of elements) MUST for the click-firing, SHOULD for the allowance.
- Given a headless control that synthesizes activation, then the HTML spec defines no Enter or Space key binding for buttons at all: key activation is a platform convention the user agent supplies for native elements and which the library MUST therefore implement itself (WHATWG HTML: §6.5, which defers key triggering to the UA; §4.16.3 gives the Space-on-button `:active` example as non-normative prose).
- Given `element.click()`, then it MUST return early if the element is a disabled form control, MUST return early if a click is already in progress on that element, and MUST otherwise fire a synthetic pointer event named `click` with the not-trusted flag set, so `isTrusted` is false and the method is reentrancy-guarded per element (WHATWG HTML: §6.5, the click() steps) MUST.

### Focus management

- Given a toolbar, then it MUST occupy exactly one stop in the page tab sequence, with arrow keys moving focus inside it (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, about this pattern; content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, fundamental keyboard navigation conventions).
- Given a toolbar containing embedded composites such as a radio group, a menu button and a spinbutton, then the whole toolbar MUST still share ONE roving tabindex ring rather than each composite owning its own tab stop (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js toolbar-item-tabindex assertion; content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FormatToolbar.js setFocusItem, which writes `tabindex="-1"` across every item).
- Given Home or End pressed inside a toolbar, then focus MUST cross group boundaries to reach the very first or very last control in the toolbar, not merely the bounds of the current group (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-home and toolbar-end assertions).
- Given a toolbar control is clicked, then focus MUST be set on it so the roving `tabindex` stays aligned with the pointer state (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FormatToolbarItem.js, handleClick).

### State

- Given a toolbar control whose function is currently unavailable, then it MUST expose `aria-disabled="true"`; the APG toolbar starts Copy, Cut and Paste disabled because nothing is selected and the clipboard is empty (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-button-aria-disabled assertion).
- Given a disabled toolbar control that the user needs to be able to discover, then it MUST use `aria-disabled="true"` and remain focusable, and its action handler MUST no-op while disabled (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, focusability of disabled controls, which names the toolbar's Copy, Cut and Paste buttons explicitly; content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/FormatToolbar.js activateItem with its `aria-disabled` guard).
- Given a disabled toolbar control the user can infer from its neighbors, then it SHOULD instead use the native `disabled` attribute and be removed from the focus order; the APG names an Up button that is disabled while the first list item is selected, next to an enabled Down button (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, focusability of disabled controls).
- Given a toolbar, then disabled elements MAY be made focusable where discoverability of a function is crucial, so screen reader users are more likely to be aware of them (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, note).
- Given a toolbar whose state depends on selection or clipboard contents, then the `aria-disabled` values MUST be recomputed on every relevant change; in the APG example selecting text enables Copy and Cut, copying enables Paste, and cutting disables Copy and Cut again (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-button-enter-or-space assertion).
- Given radios in a toolbar, then exactly one MUST have `aria-checked="true"` at any time (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-radio-aria-checked assertion, verified for every choice).
- Given `menuitemradio` items in a toolbar's menu, then exactly one MUST have `aria-checked="true"` at any time (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js, toolbar-menuitemradio-aria-checked assertion).
- Given a toolbar spinbutton, then every value change MUST update `aria-valuenow`, `aria-valuetext` and the visible text together, and MUST apply the new setting to the controlled element in the same step (aria-practices: test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js toolbar-spinbutton-aria-valuetext assertion; content/patterns/toolbar/examples/js/SpinButton.js setValue).

### Forms

- Given a control that mimics a native form control without using one, then it MUST be made form-associated deliberately: only `button`, `fieldset`, `input`, `object`, `output`, `select`, `textarea`, `img` and form-associated custom elements are form-associated elements, and only `button`, `input`, `select`, `textarea` and form-associated custom elements are submittable (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.2 Categories, form-associated elements, listed elements and submittable elements lists) MUST.
- Given a control with a `form` attribute, then that attribute's value MUST be the id of a `form` element in the control's tree, and only listed elements may carry it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3 Association of controls and forms) MUST.
- Given a form-associated element with no `form` attribute, then its form owner MUST be its nearest ancestor `form` element, and the owner MUST be recomputed whenever the element is inserted or removed, its `form` attribute changes, or a matching id appears or disappears (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.18.3, reset the form owner algorithm) MUST.
- Given a headless control that needs form participation, then the supported mechanism is a form-associated custom element: set `static formAssociated = true`, call `attachInternals()` once, report the value with `setFormValue(value, state)`, and report validity with `setValidity(flags, message, anchor)`; a second `attachInternals()` call MUST throw `NotSupportedError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13 Custom elements, ElementInternals) MUST.
- Given `setFormValue(null)`, then the element MUST be excluded from the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setFormValue) MUST.
- Given `setValidity` called with any flag true, then the message argument MUST be non-empty or the call MUST throw `TypeError` (WHATWG HTML: §4.13, setValidity) MUST.
- Given a form-associated custom element, then it MUST handle `formAssociatedCallback` when its form owner changes, `formResetCallback` when the owner is reset, `formDisabledCallback` with the new boolean when its disabled state changes, and `formStateRestoreCallback` with a reason of `"autocomplete"` or `"restore"` when the user agent restores its value (WHATWG HTML: §4.13.6, form lifecycle callbacks).
- Given a control that exposes constraint validation, then `willValidate` MUST return whether it is a candidate for constraint validation, `checkValidity()` MUST fire a cancelable `invalid` event and return false when constraints are unmet, `reportValidity()` MUST additionally report the problem to the user and MAY focus the element, and `setCustomValidity(error)` MUST normalize newlines in the message before storing it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3 The constraint validation API) MUST.
- Given a validity state, then the full set of flags is `valueMissing`, `typeMismatch`, `patternMismatch`, `tooLong`, `tooShort`, `rangeUnderflow`, `rangeOverflow`, `stepMismatch`, `badInput`, `customError` and `valid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.21.3, ValidityState).
- Given a submitter with `formnovalidate`, or a form with `novalidate`, then its no-validate state is true and interactive constraint validation MUST be skipped on submission (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.6) MUST.
- Given a form that is reset, then a `reset` event MUST be fired that bubbles and is cancelable, and only if it is not cancelled MUST each resettable element's own reset algorithm run; changes made by those algorithms MUST NOT count as user changes, so they MUST NOT fire `input` events (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.23 Resetting a form) MUST.
- Given `:user-invalid`, then it MUST match only elements whose user validity is true, which is set when the element has been through a change or blur with a changed value or when submission has been attempted, and which is therefore distinct from `:invalid` (WHATWG HTML: §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes).
- Given a control the library renders as disabled without using a native disabled element, then `aria-disabled="true"` alone changes only the accessibility tree: the browser supplies no click suppression, no focus exclusion and no constraint-validation exemption, so every one of those behaviors MUST be implemented in script if they are wanted (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 and §4.15, contrasted with ARIA's `aria-disabled`) MUST.
- Given a native form control that is disabled, then the user agent MUST prevent any click events queued on the user interaction task source from being dispatched on it (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5 Enabling and disabling form controls: the disabled attribute) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST NOT be a focusable area at all, so it is neither click-focusable nor tab-reachable nor focusable by `.focus()`; "the element is not actually disabled" is a precondition of being a focusable area (WHATWG HTML: §6.6.2 Focusable area) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then it MUST be barred from constraint validation and MUST NOT contribute an entry to the submitted entry list (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5; §4.10.22.4 Constructing the entry list) MUST.
- Given a disabled control, then being disabled MUST NOT prevent programmatic modification: setting `value` or `checked` from script still works, so a "disabled" state in a headless library MUST NOT be relied on to freeze state (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.19.5, explicit note).
- Given a `fieldset` with `disabled`, then every form control descendant MUST be disabled except those inside the fieldset's first `legend` child, and this propagates transitively through nested fieldsets (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.15 The fieldset element) MUST.
- Given the `:disabled` pseudo-class, then it MUST match any element that is "actually disabled", whose exhaustive list is a disabled `button`, `input`, `select` or `textarea`, an `optgroup` or `option` that is disabled or whose nearest ancestor `select` is disabled, a disabled `fieldset`, and a disabled form-associated custom element (WHATWG HTML: §4.15 Disabled elements; §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes) MUST.

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="toolbar"`, then the axe role table requires an accessible name, `accessibleNameRequired: true`, but there is NO dedicated `aria-toolbar-name` rule, so a nameless toolbar is not flagged by any shipped rule and a conformance suite MUST assert the name itself (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js toolbar; lib/rules contains no toolbar-name rule).
- Given `role="toolbar"` in the axe role table, then it is a structure-type role whose role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-orientation`, `aria-activedescendant` and `aria-expanded`, its superclass is `group`, and it has no required owned elements or required context, so any mix of controls is structurally legal (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, toolbar).
- Given a toolbar using roving tabindex, then no control MUST have a positive `tabindex`, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json).
- Given toolbar buttons, then `button-name` applies at critical impact for native buttons and `aria-command-name` at serious impact for `role="button"` (axe: lib/rules/button-name.json, aria-command-name.json).
- Given toggle buttons in a toolbar, then `aria-pressed` is allowed on `role="button"` and the `nested-interactive` rule forbids focusable descendants inside them (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js button; lib/rules/nested-interactive.json).
- Given a radio group nested in a toolbar, then `aria-toggle-field-name` applies to each radio and `aria-checked` is a required attribute on each; `radio` has no `requiredContext` in axe, so the `radiogroup` wrapper is not enforced by `aria-required-parent` (axe: lib/rules/aria-toggle-field-name.json, aria-required-attr.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js radio).
- Given a menu button nested in a toolbar, then the whole menu rule set applies to its popup: `aria-required-children` on the `menu`, `aria-required-parent` on each item, and `aria-toggle-field-name` plus required `aria-checked` on each `menuitemradio` (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-children.json, aria-required-parent.json, aria-toggle-field-name.json, aria-required-attr.json).
- Given a spinbutton nested in a toolbar, then `aria-input-field-name` applies at serious impact, and because `spinbutton` is not `childrenPresentational`, its internal increase and decrease buttons do NOT trigger `nested-interactive` (axe: lib/rules/aria-input-field-name.json; lib/rules/nested-interactive-matches.js).
- Given toolbar controls disabled with `aria-disabled="true"` to stay focusable, then no axe rule objects, because axe`'`s focusability model only reacts to the native `disabled` attribute, to `inert` and to hiding (axe: lib/commons/dom/is-focusable.js via focusDisabled).
- Given icon glyphs inside toolbar buttons marked `role="presentation"`, then they MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json).
- Given toolbar controls, then the 24 by 24 CSS pixel target-size rule is the one most likely to bite a dense icon toolbar, though it ships disabled and must be enabled explicitly (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a toolbar, then grouping controls into one can be an effective way of reducing the number of tab stops in the keyboard interface, which is the main reason to use the role beyond conveying the visual grouping (aria-practices: content/patterns/toolbar/toolbar-pattern.html, about this pattern).
- Given a toolbar whose embedded composites include disabled elements, then the design technique for mitigating the cost of keeping disabled controls in the focus path is to add appropriate keyboard shortcuts (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, focusability of disabled controls).
- Given a toolbar, then the tab stop convention is "the first element", the same family as menubar, and different from radio group, tabs, listbox and tree which put the tab stop on the selected element, and from grid and treegrid which restore the last-focused element (aria-practices: content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html, keyboard navigation inside components).

## meter

### Roles and attributes

- Given the element serving as a meter, then it MUST have `role="meter"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/meter/meter-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a meter, then `aria-valuenow` MUST be set to a decimal value between `aria-valuemin` and `aria-valuemax` (aria-practices: content/patterns/meter/meter-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a meter, then `aria-valuemin` MUST be a decimal value less than `aria-valuemax`, and `aria-valuemax` MUST be a decimal value greater than `aria-valuemin` (aria-practices: content/patterns/meter/meter-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a meter whose range is 0 to 100, then `aria-valuemin` and `aria-valuemax` MAY be omitted because those are the role defaults; `aria-valuenow` is always required for `meter` (aria-practices: content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html, range widget table and "range properties with meter").
- Given a meter where a bare percentage would not be user friendly, then `aria-valuetext` MUST be set to a readable string, for example `aria-valuetext="50% (6 hours) remaining"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/meter/meter-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a meter with a visible label, then that label MUST be referenced by `aria-labelledby`; otherwise `aria-label` MUST supply the name (aria-practices: content/patterns/meter/meter-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given the APG meter example, then exactly one `role="meter"` element exists and it is a `div` with a resolvable `aria-labelledby` (aria-practices: test/tests/meter_meter.js, meter-role and meter-aria-labelledby assertions).
- Given a `role="meter"` element, then all of its descendants are forced presentational by the user agent (aria-practices: content/practices/hiding-semantics/hiding-semantics-practice.html, roles that require presentational children).

### Keyboard

- Given a meter, then there is no keyboard interaction: the APG keyboard section reads "Not applicable", so a meter MUST NOT be focusable (aria-practices: content/patterns/meter/meter-pattern.html, keyboard interaction).

### Pointer

- Given a meter, then there is no pointer interaction: a meter is a read-only display, not a control (aria-practices: content/patterns/meter/meter-pattern.html, keyboard interaction "Not applicable").

### State

- Given a meter, then `aria-valuemin` MUST parse as a number, `aria-valuemax` MUST parse as a number and be greater than or equal to `aria-valuemin`, and `aria-valuenow` MUST parse as a number that lies between them inclusive (aria-practices: test/tests/meter_meter.js, meter-aria-valuemin, meter-aria-valuemax and meter-aria-valuenow assertions).
- Given a meter with a visible fill, then the fill's rendered width MUST match `(valuenow - valuemin) / (valuemax - valuemin)` of the track width; the APG test allows a 10 px tolerance and subtracts 8 px for border and padding, and its own comment calls this an approximation better done as a unit test (aria-practices: test/tests/meter_meter.js, meter-aria-valuenow assertion, "fill matches current value").
- Given a meter whose value does not change, then the implementation MUST NOT rewrite `aria-valuemin`, `aria-valuemax` or `aria-valuenow`; the APG example dirty-checks against cached values (aria-practices: content/patterns/meter/examples/js/meter.js, _update).
