- Given the native `meter` element, then its `value` attribute must be specified, `min` defaults to 0, and `max` defaults to 1.0, which differs from the ARIA `meter` role whose defaults are 0 and 100 (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.14 The meter element, compared with aria-practices content/practices/range-related-properties).
- Given the meter gauge region algorithm, then the values MUST be resolved in this order, each depending on the previous: minimum from `min` or 0; maximum from `max` or 1.0, forced equal to the minimum if it would be lower; actual value from `value` or 0, clamped into the minimum-to-maximum range; low boundary from `low` or the minimum, clamped into that range; high boundary from `high` or the maximum, clamped into the low-boundary-to-maximum range; optimum point from `optimum` or the midpoint, clamped into the minimum-to-maximum range (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.14) MUST.
- Given the optimum point lies at or between the low and high boundaries, then the region between those boundaries MUST be treated as optimum and the low and high parts as suboptimal (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.14, region-classification rule) MUST.
- Given the optimum point lies below the low boundary, then the region from the minimum to the low boundary MUST be optimum, the region from low to high MUST be suboptimal, and the remainder MUST be an even less good region (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.14) MUST.
- Given the optimum point lies above the high boundary, then the classification MUST be reversed relative to the below-low case (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.14) MUST.
- Given a headless meter, then it MUST reproduce that three-way bucketing itself to choose a good, suboptimal or bad visual state, because nothing in ARIA conveys low, high or optimum (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.14, compared with aria-practices content/patterns/meter/meter-pattern.html which defines only valuenow, valuemin, valuemax and valuetext) MUST.

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="meter"`, then it MUST have an accessible name from `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`; this is tagged WCAG 1.1.1 A rather than 4.1.2, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-meter-name.json).
- Given `role="meter"`, then `aria-valuenow` is a REQUIRED attribute, so omitting it fails at critical impact (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js meter `requiredAttrs`; lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json).
- Given `role="meter"` in the axe role table, then it is a structure-type role whose role-specific allowed attributes are exactly `aria-valuemax`, `aria-valuemin` and `aria-valuetext`, its superclass is `range`, an accessible name is required, and children are presentational; notably `aria-expanded` is NOT allowed on `meter` (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, meter).
- Given `role="meter"`, then it MUST NOT contain a focusable widget-role descendant, because `meter` has `childrenPresentational: true`, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/nested-interactive.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js meter).
- Given a meter given `tabindex="0"`, then `focus-order-semantics` FAILS at minor impact, because `meter` is a structure-type role and is in neither the widget-role set nor the valid-scrollable-semantics map; a meter MUST NOT be put in the focus order (axe: lib/checks/aria/has-widget-role-evaluate.js; lib/checks/aria/valid-scrollable-semantics-evaluate.js).
- Given `aria-valuenow`, `aria-valuemin` or `aria-valuemax` on a meter set to a non-numeric string, then `aria-valid-attr-value` fails at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a value with no meaningful maximum, such as the world population, then `meter` MUST NOT be used (aria-practices: content/patterns/meter/meter-pattern.html, note).
- Given progress such as loading or percent completion of a task, then `meter` MUST NOT be used; use `role="progressbar"` instead (aria-practices: content/patterns/meter/meter-pattern.html, note).
- Given a meter, then assistive technologies often present `aria-valuenow` as a percentage, which is why `aria-valuetext` exists for cases where a percentage is unhelpful (aria-practices: content/patterns/meter/meter-pattern.html, roles/states/properties).
- Given a meter outside the default range, for example an alkaline pH gauge from 7 to 14, then both `aria-valuemin` and `aria-valuemax` MUST be set explicitly (aria-practices: content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html, range properties with meter).

## progress

### Roles and attributes

- Given an element that communicates progress of a task, then it MUST have `role="progressbar"`, not `role="meter"` (aria-practices: content/patterns/meter/meter-pattern.html, note; content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html, range properties with progress bars).
- Given a progressbar whose range is 0 to 100, then `aria-valuemin` and `aria-valuemax` MAY be omitted because those are the role defaults (aria-practices: content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html, range widget table).
- Given a progressbar with a known value, then `aria-valuenow` MUST be set and MUST fall within the minimum and maximum (aria-practices: content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html, range properties with progress bars).
- Given a progressbar whose value is indeterminate or unknown, then `aria-valuenow` MUST be omitted entirely; `progressbar` is the only range role besides `spinbutton` for which `aria-valuenow` is not required (aria-practices: content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html, range widget table and range properties with progress bars).
- Given a progressbar, then it MUST have an accessible name, from `aria-labelledby` referring to a visible label or from `aria-label` (aria-practices: content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html, progressbar examples, all of which carry `aria-labelledby`).
- Given a progressbar where the number alone is unhelpful, then `aria-valuetext` MAY be set to a readable string (aria-practices: content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html, using aria-valuetext).
- Given a `role="progressbar"` element, then all of its descendants are forced presentational by the user agent (aria-practices: content/practices/hiding-semantics/hiding-semantics-practice.html, roles that require presentational children).

### Keyboard

- Given a progressbar, then it is not an interactive widget and MUST NOT be focusable; the APG has no progressbar pattern and defines no keyboard interaction for the role (aria-practices: content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html, which treats progressbar purely as a range display).

### Pointer

- Given a progressbar, then there is no pointer interaction: it is a read-only display (aria-practices: content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html, which defines progressbar purely as a range display).

### State

- Given the native `progress` element, then it is determinate if and only if the `value` attribute is present; with `value` omitted it MUST be an indeterminate progress bar (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.13 The progress element) MUST.
- Given a `progress` element's maximum, then it comes from parsing `max`, and if `max` is absent, in error, or less than or equal to zero, the maximum MUST be 1.0 (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.13) MUST.
- Given a determinate `progress` element, then its value comes from parsing `value`, MUST be zero if `value` is absent or in error or not positive, and MUST be clamped to the maximum when it exceeds it; the clamp is applied to the current value rather than written back into the DOM (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.13) MUST.
- Given `progress.position`, then it MUST return -1 for an indeterminate progress bar and otherwise the current value divided by the maximum, so -1 is the sentinel a headless progress component MUST mirror for the indeterminate state (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.13) MUST.
- Given `progress.value` read on an indeterminate progress bar, then it MUST return 0 rather than null or undefined, so the IDL value alone cannot distinguish indeterminate from zero; `position` is the discriminator (WHATWG HTML: §4.10.13) MUST.
- Given `:indeterminate`, then it MUST match `progress` elements with no `value` content attribute (WHATWG HTML: §4.16.3 Pseudo-classes) MUST.
- Given the ARIA side of an indeterminate progress bar, then `aria-valuenow` MUST be omitted, which is the accessibility-tree counterpart of a missing `value` attribute (aria-practices: content/practices/range-related-properties/range-related-properties-practice.html, range properties with progress bars) MUST.

### Accessibility rules (axe)

- Given `role="progressbar"`, then it MUST have an accessible name from `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`; this is tagged WCAG 1.1.1 A, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-progressbar-name.json).
- Given `role="progressbar"` in the axe role table, then it is a widget-type role with NO required attributes, whose role-specific allowed attributes are `aria-expanded`, `aria-valuemax`, `aria-valuemin`, `aria-valuenow` and `aria-valuetext`, its superclass is `range`, an accessible name is required, and children are presentational; the absence of `aria-valuenow` from `requiredAttrs` is what makes an indeterminate progress bar legal (axe: lib/standards/aria-roles.js, progressbar).
- Given `role="progressbar"`, then it MUST NOT contain a focusable widget-role descendant, because `progressbar` has `childrenPresentational: true` (axe: lib/rules/nested-interactive.json; lib/standards/aria-roles.js progressbar).
- Given a progressbar given `tabindex="0"`, then `focus-order-semantics` passes the widget-role branch, because `progressbar` is a widget-type role in the axe table, unlike `meter` which is a structure-type role; the two range roles behave differently under this rule (axe: lib/checks/aria/has-widget-role-evaluate.js; lib/standards/aria-roles.js progressbar vs meter).
- Given a progressbar implemented as `role="img"` over a spinner image, then `role-img-alt` requires alternative text from `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/role-img-alt.json).
- Given `aria-valuenow` on a progressbar set to a non-numeric string, then `aria-valid-attr-value` fails at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json).
- Given any element with a `role` attribute, then the role MUST be a valid, non-abstract, widely supported ARIA role; violations are `invalidrole` and `unsupportedrole` at critical impact and `abstractrole` at serious (axe: lib/rules/aria-roles.json, checks invalidrole, abstractrole, unsupportedrole).
- Given any element with a `role`, then every ARIA attribute on it MUST be allowed for that role and for that host element, and MUST NOT be a deprecated or poorly supported attribute; the rule is `wcag2a`/`wcag412` at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-allowed-attr.json, checks aria-allowed-attr, aria-allowed-attr-elm, aria-no-deprecated-attr, aria-unsupported-attr).
- Given any element with a `role`, then all of that role's required ARIA attributes MUST be present; the failure message is "Required ARIA attribute not present" at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-required-attr.json, check aria-required-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its name MUST be a valid ARIA attribute name, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr.json, check aria-valid-attr).
- Given any `aria-*` attribute, then its value MUST be valid for that attribute, at critical impact; an id reference that does not resolve is reported as incomplete rather than a failure, and an empty value is reported as "ignored while empty" (axe: lib/rules/aria-valid-attr-value.json, check aria-valid-attr-value).
- Given an element whose role prohibits `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby`, notably `none` and `presentation`, then neither MUST be present, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-prohibited-attr.json, check aria-prohibited-attr; lib/standards/aria-roles.js, `prohibitedAttrs` on the shared noneRole object).
- Given any element with a `role`, then the role MUST NOT be deprecated, at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-deprecated-role.json, check deprecatedrole).
- Given any element with `tabindex`, then the value MUST NOT be greater than 0; positive tabindex is a best-practice failure at serious impact, so roving-tabindex implementations MUST only ever use 0 and -1 (axe: lib/rules/tabindex.json, check tabindex).
- Given an id referenced by `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-owns` or `aria-activedescendant`, then that id MUST be unique in the document, at critical impact (axe: lib/rules/duplicate-id-aria.json, check duplicate-id-aria).
- Given an element marked `role="presentation"` or `role="none"`, then it MUST NOT also be focusable or carry a global ARIA attribute, because screen readers then disagree about whether to ignore it; best practice at minor impact (axe: lib/rules/presentation-role-conflict.json, checks is-element-focusable, has-global-aria-attribute).
- Given an element with `aria-hidden="true"`, then neither it nor any descendant MUST be focusable; a focusable descendant fails with "Focusable content should have tabindex=\"-1\" or be removed from the DOM" at serious impact, and only the outermost `aria-hidden="true"` element is tested (axe: lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus.json, checks focusable-modal-open, focusable-disabled, focusable-not-tabbable; lib/rules/aria-hidden-focus-matches.js).
- Given `aria-brailleroledescription` or `aria-braillelabel`, then there MUST be a non-empty non-braille equivalent, an `aria-roledescription` or an accessible name respectively, at serious impact (axe: lib/rules/aria-braille-equivalent.json, checks braille-roledescription-equivalent, braille-label-equivalent).
- Given a clickable control, then its touch target SHOULD be at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or leave sufficient space from its neighbors; this is WCAG 2.2 AA at serious impact but the rule ships disabled by default so it MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/target-size.json, checks target-size with `minSize: 24`, target-offset with `minOffset: 24`, `enabled: false`).

### Notes and edge cases

- Given this APG commit, then there is no progressbar pattern page, no example and no test file; the normative material for `progressbar` comes from the range-related-properties practice, the axe role table and the HTML `progress` element (aria-practices: content/patterns contains no progressbar directory).

## Test infrastructure notes

How the APG example tests are structured, and what a conformance suite can borrow from them.

- The runner is Ava plus Selenium WebDriver, driving headless Firefox through geckodriver. `npm run regression` is `ava --timeout=1m`, and Ava's `files` glob is `test/tests/*`, so every file in that directory is a test file (aria-practices: package.json, scripts and ava config; test/index.js).
- Browser coverage is Firefox only. There is no Chromium or WebKit run, so nothing in this baseline has been verified cross-browser by the APG itself (aria-practices: test/index.js, `.forBrowser('firefox')`).
- One browser session is shared across the whole run: `test.before` starts geckodriver and builds the session, `test.after.always` closes both, and `test.beforeEach` hangs the session plus a `waitTime` and the two query helpers onto `t.context` (aria-practices: test/index.js).
- The default inter-action wait is 500 ms, overridable with the `TEST_WAIT_TIME` environment variable, and setting `DEBUG` drops the `-headless` flag so the browser is visible (aria-practices: test/index.js).
- geckodriver is started on a free port found by polling upward from 1022 with a 12-second budget, and port 8432 is bound as a cross-process mutex so concurrent runs serialize their driver startup (aria-practices: test/util/start-geckodriver.js; test/util/force-serial.js).
- Every test is declared through one wrapper, `ariaTest(desc, page, testId, body)`. The example page is loaded from a `file://` URL resolved against the repo root, so the suite needs no web server (aria-practices: test/index.js).
- The `testId` is not decoration. It is turned into the selector `[data-test-id="<testId>"]`, and before the test body runs the harness asserts that at least one element in the example page matches it. A documented behavior with no corresponding row in the example's own keyboard or attribute table fails immediately with "Cannot find behavior description for this test in example page". The one exempt id is `test-additional-behavior` (aria-practices: test/index.js).
- That mechanism is the interesting part to copy: the example page's documentation tables carry `data-test-id` attributes, and the tests bind to those ids, so prose and assertions cannot drift apart. The generated test name is `<page> [data-test-id="<id>"]: <desc>` (aria-practices: test/index.js).
- Tests run with `test.serial` by default, not in parallel, because they share the single browser session (aria-practices: test/index.js).
- `ariaTest.failing` wraps Ava's `test.failing`, so a known-broken behavior is recorded as expected-to-fail and will itself fail if it starts passing. Two combobox grid assertions use it: the row `aria-selected` check under APG issue 859, and a placeholder for standard editing keys whose body is a bare `t.fail()` (aria-practices: test/index.js; test/tests/combobox_grid-combo.js).
- Setting `REGRESSION_COVERAGE_REPORT` replaces every test body with an immediate failure, which is how the coverage tooling enumerates which documented behaviors have tests without actually driving a browser (aria-practices: test/index.js; package.json `coverage-report` script).
- `npm run regression-report` parses the example HTML with cheerio and htmlparser2, walks every `data-test-id` in `content/patterns`, and cross-references them against `test/tests` to report undocumented and untested behaviors. Two ignore lists live in `test/util/report_files`, and the id `test-not-required` opts a documented row out (aria-practices: test/util/report.js).
- Assertions are made with Node's `assert` module rather than Ava's `t` API in most helpers, with a trailing `t.pass()` so Ava sees an assertion. A failure therefore throws rather than producing an Ava diff (aria-practices: test/util/assertRovingTabindex.js and siblings).
- The shared helper vocabulary is worth mirroring directly in a conformance suite. `assertAriaRoles` checks the count and tag name of elements with a role inside an example. `assertAttributeValues` string-compares one attribute across every match. `assertAttributeDNE` asserts an attribute is absent rather than `"false"`. `assertRovingTabindex` walks a set, checking that exactly one element has `tabindex="0"` at each step while sending a key to advance. `assertTabOrder` focuses the first selector then Tabs through an ordered list, waiting for `document.activeElement` to match at each step. `assertHasFocus` polls until `document.activeElement` matches. `assertAriaActivedescendant` checks both that `aria-activedescendant` names the expected option AND that real DOM focus is on the container. `assertAriaSelectedAndActivedescendant` bundles the activedescendant and `aria-selected` checks for selection-follows-focus widgets. `assertAriaLabelledby` and `assertAriaDescribedby` resolve every id in the space-separated value and require the referenced element to have non-empty text. `assertAriaControls` resolves a single-id `aria-controls` value. `assertAriaLabelExists` requires a non-empty `aria-label`. `assertInputChecked` reads the native `.checked` IDL property. `assertDotValue` reads an IDL property such as `element.role` or `element.ariaPressed` rather than the attribute, which is how the ARIA 1.2 reflection example is tested. `assertAttributeCanBeToggled` sends a key twice and requires the attribute to read `"true"` then `"false"`. `assertNoElements` requires zero matches (aria-practices: test/util/).
- `assertAriaControls` handles only a single id. Tests that need a multi-id `aria-controls`, such as the mixed checkbox, hand-roll the split and per-id existence check instead, so a conformance suite SHOULD write the multi-id version once (aria-practices: test/util/assertAriaControls.js; test/tests/checkbox_checkbox-mixed.js).
- Platform differences are handled explicitly rather than abstracted away. `isMacOS()` checks `process.platform === 'darwin'`, and `translatePlatformKeys` maps `Key.CONTROL` to `Key.META` on macOS so a Control+A chord becomes Command+A there. The toolbar clipboard tests use it; the grid combobox Home test uses `isMacOS()` to skip a known macOS bug, APG issue 3191 (aria-practices: test/util/isMacOS.js; test/util/translatePlatformKeys.js; test/tests/toolbar_toolbar.js; test/tests/combobox_grid-combo.js).
- Asynchronous state changes are handled by polling with `session.wait` on a predicate, not by fixed sleeps, in the helpers. Individual tests sometimes still insert literal sleeps, for example a 300 ms settle in the hybrid disclosure navigation test (aria-practices: test/util/assertHasFocus.js; test/tests/disclosure_navigation_hybrid.js).
- Visual assertions exist but are deliberately loose. The meter fill test compares `getRect()` widths with a 10 px tolerance and an 8 px fudge for border and padding, and its own comment says this "would be better in a unit test eventually" (aria-practices: test/tests/meter_meter.js).
- Visibility is asserted with Selenium's `isDisplayed()`, which means CSS-driven hiding, the `hidden` attribute and DOM removal are all indistinguishable at the assertion level. Where the distinction matters, tests read the specific mechanism instead, for example the `inert` property in the disclosure card tests (aria-practices: test/tests/disclosure_card.js).
- Assertion ids are reused across several `ariaTest` calls when the pattern documents one behavior that several keys satisfy. Switch tests map both the Space and the Enter test to `key-space`; tabs map both Enter and Space to `key-enter-or-space`. A conformance suite that keys off ids MUST allow many-to-one (aria-practices: test/tests/switch_switch.js; test/tests/tabs_tabs-manual.js).
- Several test titles in the repository are stale or wrong while the assertions are correct, for example a radio `aria-activedescendant` test titled "on div element" that actually requires `ul`, and a multithumb test titled "end sends value to minimum" that tests the maximum. Titles MUST NOT be trusted as specification (aria-practices: test/tests/radio_radio-activedescendant.js; test/tests/slider_slider-multithumb.js).
- Some behaviors documented in the patterns have no test at all. There is no assertion that a dialog returns focus to its invoker, no Shift+Tab assertion for the alert dialog, no Delete or Ctrl+Page Up test for tabs, and the tooltip pattern has neither an example nor a test file. A conformance suite MUST supply those itself (aria-practices: test/tests/dialog-modal_dialog.js; test/tests/alertdialog_alertdialog.js; test/tests/tabs_tabs-automatic.js; content/patterns/tooltip).
- Two menu-button `aria-activedescendant` tests are commented out as flaky under APG issue 1415, and the links-menu Enter test under issue 1937. Those behaviors are documented but unverified upstream (aria-practices: test/tests/menu-button_actions-active-descendant.js; test/tests/menu-button_links.js).
- On the axe side there is no per-component test suite to mine: rules are declarative JSON in `lib/rules` naming checks in `lib/checks`, and the role facts a conformance suite needs live in one table, `lib/standards/aria-roles.js`, keyed by role with `requiredOwned`, `requiredContext`, `requiredAttrs`, `allowedAttrs`, `superclassRole`, `accessibleNameRequired`, `nameFromContent`, `prohibitedAttrs` and `childrenPresentational`. Two rules relevant here ship disabled, `target-size` and `aria-roledescription`, plus the deprecated `duplicate-id-active`, so they MUST be enabled explicitly to run (axe: lib/rules/, lib/checks/, lib/standards/aria-roles.js).
